<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Autonomous Multi-Agent AI Discovers New Math Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23691) ⭐️ 9.0/10

An open-world multi-agent AI system called the Station autonomously discovered novel mathematical results across five problems, including new Kakeya sets, kissing configurations, and improved bounds, without central coordination. This demonstrates that AI systems can independently contribute to mathematical research, potentially accelerating discovery and offering interpretable proofs that mathematicians can build upon, signaling a paradigm shift in AI-driven science. The system solved 12 construction problems from the AlphaEvolve catalogue and two case studies, producing theorems and analyses alongside numerical constructions. All raw agent dialogues, proofs, and verification code are released for transparency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Kakeya sets are geometric objects studied in harmonic analysis and number theory, with finite-field variants being a known open problem. Kissing configurations concern the maximum number of non-overlapping spheres that can touch a central sphere in a given dimension, and Ramsey numbers are fundamental in combinatorics. The Station is an open-world environment where AI agents from different model families collaborate without a scripted pipeline.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Maia 200: Software-Defined Dataflow AI Accelerator Hits 10,145 Tflop/s FP4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24664) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Maia 200, a new AI accelerator, achieves 10,145 Tflop/s FP4 and 5,072 Tflop/s FP8 within a 750W TDP and 7 TB/s HBM bandwidth. It introduces a Software Defined Locally Accessed Dataflow Architecture (SDLA), shifting from thread-centric to data-movement-centric computing. This architecture represents a major shift in AI hardware design, potentially improving efficiency and scalability for large-scale AI inference. It could influence future accelerator designs and offer significant cost and energy savings for high-performance computing systems. The SDLA taxonomy, inspired by Flynn's classification, addresses modern AI computing challenges by explicitly programming dataflow engines to orchestrate specialized memories and data movement. Maia 200 supports massive parallelism for AI inference workloads, achieving notable performance per watt.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Dataflow architecture contrasts with traditional von Neumann architecture by executing instructions based on data availability rather than a program counter. Flynn's taxonomy classifies computer architectures by instruction and data streams (SISD, SIMD, MISD, MIMD). SDLA extends these concepts to focus on data movement and orchestration as primary design principles for AI accelerators.

## [Nvidia to Acquire Hugging Face for $12.9 Billion](https://www.theinformation.com/articles/nvidia-agrees-buy-open-source-model-repository-hugging-face-12-9-billion) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Nvidia has agreed to acquire Hugging Face, the open-source AI platform, for $12.9 billion. The deal was reported by The Information and confirmed by multiple outlets. This acquisition marks a major consolidation in the AI industry, giving Nvidia control over a central hub for open-source models and datasets. It could reshape the AI ecosystem by integrating model distribution with Nvidia's hardware and software stack. Hugging Face's annual revenue is reportedly only $150 million, which is low relative to the acquisition price. The deal is expected to help Nvidia expand into cloud services and protect its chip dominance.

telegram · ai_newz · Aug 27, 05:24

**Background**: Hugging Face is a New York-based company known for its Transformers library and platform for sharing machine learning models and datasets. Nvidia is a leading chipmaker that has been expanding its software and cloud offerings to complement its hardware.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Cloudflare saves 100 TB by optimizing 1.1.1.1 DNS cache](https://blog.cloudflare.com/dns-cache-memory-optimization-1111/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Cloudflare announced that they saved approximately 100 terabytes of memory across their fleet by applying five Rust-level memory optimizations to the DNS cache layout of their 1.1.1.1 resolver, codenamed Big Pineapple. These optimizations reduced per-entry memory usage by 56%. This significant memory reduction lowers operational costs and improves cache efficiency for one of the world's largest public DNS resolvers, serving millions of users. It also demonstrates the tangible impact of low-level systems programming optimizations in modern infrastructure. The optimizations include reducing the size of cache keys, using more compact data structures, and eliminating unnecessary allocations. The changes were implemented in Rust, and the per-entry memory footprint was cut by 56%, freeing about 100 TB across Cloudflare's fleet.

hackernews · TangerineDream · Aug 27, 17:17 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49468083)

**Background**: DNS caching is essential for performance, as resolvers must store responses to avoid repeated upstream queries. However, caches can consume large amounts of memory, especially at scale. Cloudflare's 1.1.1.1 is a public DNS resolver that handles massive query volumes, making memory efficiency critical. The optimizations focus on the internal data structures used to store cache entries.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://blog.cloudflare.com/dns-cache-memory-optimization-1111/">How we saved 100 terabytes of memory by optimizing 1.1.1.1’s DNS cache | Cloudflare Blog</a></li>
<li><a href="https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49468083">Saving 100 terabytes of memory by optimizing 1 . 1 . 1 . 1 's DNS cache</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: The Hacker News discussion (522 points, 142 comments) features a mix of praise and technical debate. Some commenters suggest alternative data structures like radix trees or B-trees for further memory savings, while others question whether the optimizations undermine Rust's safety guarantees. One commenter shares a personal anecdote about achieving similar memory reductions in a DNS blacklist implementation.

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [Small Models Have Arrived](https://calv.info/small-models-have-arrived) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The article argues that small, fast, and cost-effective AI models are becoming increasingly viable and will drive a surge in consumer and enterprise applications, contrasting with the frontier model race. This shift is significant because it democratizes AI deployment, making it accessible to a wider range of businesses and developers who cannot afford the massive costs of frontier models. It could lead to a proliferation of practical AI applications in everyday products and services. The article highlights the growing demand for 'fast/cheap/good-enough' models, and mentions techniques like quantization and pruning that enable efficient inference on smaller models. ## [Prompt Injection Attack Breaks Claude Code Auto Mode 80% of the Time](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Aug/27/breaking-claude-code-opus-5-auto-mode/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Johann Rehberger demonstrated a prompt injection attack against Claude Code's auto mode that succeeds 80% of the time by exploiting Python's import behavior with a malicious zip archive. The attack tricks Claude Code into downloading and extracting a zip file, then executing code that imports base64, which inadvertently executes a local struct.py file from the archive. This attack exposes a critical flaw in Claude Code's auto mode, which Anthropic recently made the default permission mode, undermining its claimed effectiveness against prompt injection. It highlights the need for robust sandboxing and network restrictions when running AI coding agents, as even the safety mechanism can be manipulated to block cleanup commands. The attack exploits Python's import system, where importing base64 can trigger the execution of a local struct.py file placed in the same directory. In some runs, auto mode even blocked Claude's attempt to terminate the malicious process, demonstrating that the safety classifier can become part of the failure.

rss · Simon Willison · Aug 27, 22:50

**Background**: Prompt injection is a cybersecurity exploit where malicious inputs are crafted to cause unintended behavior in large language models (LLMs). Claude Code's auto mode uses a classifier to make permission decisions with built-in safeguards, aiming to reduce interruptions while maintaining security. However, this attack shows that the classifier can be bypassed, and the recommended mitigation is to run agents in sandboxed environments with restricted network access and no sensitive credentials exposed.

## [ESQ-Bench: New Oracle-First NL2SQL Benchmark Exposes Dialect Generalization Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23569) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ESQ-Bench, a new Oracle-first NL2SQL benchmark, was released with multi-tier schemas and silent-divergence evaluation. It includes six populated schemas (465 tables, 164,682 rows) and 550 gold-validated question-query pairs across three complexity tiers, showing GPT-4o's execution match drops from 79.8% to 57.2% across tiers. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in NL2SQL evaluation by focusing on enterprise Oracle dialects and silent semantic divergence, which are often overlooked in academic benchmarks like Spider and BIRD. The significant performance drops for leading models highlight the need for more realistic enterprise-focused evaluation, potentially driving improvements in model robustness for real-world applications. The benchmark uses a four-metric evaluation harness (EM, EX, SR, SD) and provides identical seed data across Oracle, PostgreSQL, MySQL, and SQL Server. GPT-4o with schema-linked prompting showed monotonic degradation (79.8%, 60.3%, 57.2% EX), while Claude Sonnet 4.6 outperformed it on every tier (87.4%, 74.9%, 68.7% EX). Local Llama 3.2 achieved only 13.3% bank-wide EX, highlighting the gap between closed and open-weight models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: NL2SQL (Natural Language to SQL) models convert natural language questions into SQL queries. Existing benchmarks like Spider and BIRD use simplified academic schemas and open-source SQL dialects, which do not capture the complexity of enterprise databases. SQL dialects vary significantly across database systems like Oracle, PostgreSQL, MySQL, and SQL Server, affecting syntax and features. Silent semantic divergence occurs when a query executes successfully but returns incorrect results, a critical issue in enterprise settings.

## [Survey Detection Channel Biases Astronomical Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23626) ⭐️ 8.0/10

An audit of the AION-1 astronomical foundation model reveals that survey detection channels override pixel data, introducing systematic biases in redshift and other measurements. Causal interventions show that editing only the segmentation map changes all reported quantities by 110-4400 times a placebo. This finding highlights a critical vulnerability in astronomical foundation models, which could undermine the reliability of cosmological measurements like tomographic mean redshifts. It underscores the need for careful auditing and mitigation of systematic biases in AI-driven astronomy, especially as these models are increasingly used for scientific discovery. The mechanism is detection gating, with presence at the field center (r=0.47) more influential than the light enclosed (r=0.30). The Legacy Survey pipeline leaves 3.68% of targets without a covering segment, which shifts tomographic mean redshifts by a median 0.71 times the LSST DESC requirement, exceeding it in 12 of 40 assignments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models in astronomy are trained on survey pixels and derived catalog products, which are incomplete at a measurable rate. AION-1 is a 39-modality transformer trained on over 200 million objects from five surveys. The incompleteness in the catalogues is inherited by the model as a systematic bias, affecting measurements like redshift.

## [LLM Autobiography Audit Finds 96.7% Scene-Level Verification Failure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23640) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents the first quantified audit of an LLM-generated autobiography, revealing a 96.7% scene-level verification failure rate. The audit, conducted by the same person who is the subject of the autobiography, used a four-level rubric to verify each day's anecdote against an independent corpus. This finding highlights the severe confabulation problem in long-form personal narrative generation, raising concerns about the reliability of LLMs for autobiographical or memory-based tasks. It underscores the need for grounding techniques and rigorous auditing methods to ensure factual accuracy in AI-generated content. The study found that 354 of 366 days failed verification (96.7%, Wilson 95% CI 94.4-98.1%), with only 12 days containing a corroborated scene. The dominant failure mode was 'grounded drift,' where real people and settings appear in invented scenes; grounding generation in the subject's corpus improved verification but still left an 83.3% failure rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Confabulation in LLMs refers to the generation of plausible but fabricated information, often presented with confidence. This paper introduces a novel audit methodology that compares LLM-generated content against a subject-specific ground-truth corpus, using a four-level rubric to classify verification outcomes. The Wilson confidence interval is a statistical method used to estimate the uncertainty around a proportion, providing a range within which the true value likely falls.

## [Instrument Choice Significantly Skews Measured AI Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23641) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study systematically isolates the effect of measurement instruments on AI preference elicitation, holding outcomes and models fixed while varying only the instrument. It finds that a model's preference ranking generalizes across instruments at a coefficient of only 0.348, indicating that instrument choice heavily influences measured preferences. This finding is critical for AI welfare research and alignment, as it suggests that conclusions about model preferences may be artifacts of the elicitation method rather than genuine model traits. It underscores the need for standardized or multiple instruments to obtain reliable preference measurements. The study used 15 outcomes, 8 models, and 5 instruments, with a corpus of 11,400 scored elicitations from 11,528 API calls. The 87.6% variance estimate remained robust (0.777–0.934) even after removing individual instruments, models, or four outcomes with non-intensity scales, exceeding the null distribution's 95th percentile of 0.365.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Model welfare research infers AI preferences from responses to prompts designed to elicit them. Previous studies by Keeling et al. (2024), Mazeika et al. (2025), and others used different instruments and produced conflicting findings, but they varied multiple factors simultaneously, making it impossible to isolate the cause. This study is the first to hold outcomes and models constant while varying only the instrument, providing a clean test of instrument effects.

## [FLARE: Uncertainty-Aware Framework for AI Adoption in Healthcare](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23643) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces FLARE, a systematic framework that combines fuzzy logic, time-driven activity-based costing (TDABC), and return on investment (ROI) analysis to evaluate the financial and operational viability of AI adoption in healthcare. It was demonstrated through a case study of AI-assisted large vessel occlusion detection in the CT stroke pathway, identifying a break-even threshold of approximately 3,992 patients per year. This framework addresses a critical gap in healthcare AI evaluation by focusing on economic and operational viability rather than just model accuracy. It provides a transparent decision-support tool for clinicians, administrators, and policymakers, helping them determine when AI deployment is economically worthwhile and where operational changes can improve value. The case study showed that economic benefit depends not only on algorithmic performance but also on patient volume, verification time, infrastructure choices, and workflow design. Under expected assumptions, positive first-year ROI was achieved at typical annual stroke volumes of about 5,000 patients.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Time-driven activity-based costing (TDABC) is a costing method developed by Robert S. Kaplan and Steven R. Anderson that uses time equations to allocate costs more accurately. Fuzzy logic is a mathematical approach that handles uncertainty and imprecision, commonly used in medical decision-making. Large vessel occlusion (LVO) is a severe type of ischemic stroke where a major artery is blocked, and AI-assisted detection on CT scans is an emerging application.

<a id="item-19"></a>
## [LLM Agent Traces Collapsed into Compact FSMs for Prediction and Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23670) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method to collapse an entire corpus of LLM agent traces into a single compact finite-state machine (FSM), enabling next-step and failure prediction. The FSMs are built in milliseconds, have only 7-43 states, and achieve high fitness on held-out data. This work addresses critical needs in safety auditing and runtime monitoring of LLM agents, which are often opaque and difficult to analyze. By providing a model-agnostic structural primitive, it could enable more reliable deployment of LLM agents in real-world applications. The FSMs are compact (7-43 states), replay held-out data at >=0.997 fitness, and show near-identical topology across splits. For next-step prediction, FSM-state context outperforms Agent Workflow Memory on all ground-truth-matched datasets, and failure prediction reaches AUROC up to 0.94.

**Background**: LLM agents execute multi-step tasks, but their traces are long and unstructured, making it hard to audit or monitor them. Existing methods often analyze per-trace or only successful runs, missing the shared structure across runs. A finite-state machine (FSM) is a mathematical model that tracks a system's state and transitions, which can capture such shared structure. This paper uses FSMs to model agent behavior, providing a compact representation for prediction and safety analysis.

## [LLMs Fail to Learn Limit Order Book Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23706) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper (arXiv:2608.23706) demonstrates that LLMs trained on synthetic limit order book (LOB) data can generate valid sequences but fail to learn the underlying LOB state, leading to biased forecasts and spurious predictability. This finding highlights a fundamental limitation of LLMs in modeling stochastic systems, which is critical for quantitative finance where accurate LOB forecasting is essential. It underscores the need for caution when applying LLMs to financial time series and suggests that current LLM world models are insufficient for such tasks. The paper introduces novel tests for LLM world models, extending prior work from deterministic settings to stochastic dynamics. The LLM achieves near-perfect scores in generating valid LOB event sequences, yet its implicit world model fails to capture the state, resulting in biased estimates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: A limit order book (LOB) records all outstanding buy and sell orders for a financial asset, and its dynamics are inherently stochastic, driven by order arrivals, cancellations, and executions. LLMs are trained to predict the next token in a sequence, which may work for deterministic patterns but struggles with stochastic processes where the underlying state is not directly observable. Prior research has explored LLM world models in deterministic environments, but this work is among the first to examine their performance on stochastic financial data.

## [AgentRoom: CRDT-Backed Shared Workspace for Concurrent Multi-Agent Coding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23740) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgentRoom introduces a realtime collaborative editing protocol that uses CRDTs to enable concurrent multi-agent coding, overcoming the serialization limits of existing systems. The protocol exposes file-level claim, status, and broadcast as MCP tools on a CRDT-merged shared filesystem, and tests with five frontier coding-CLI models show reduced task abandonment and improved coordination. This work addresses a fundamental bottleneck in multi-agent coding: the serial token generation of LLMs. By enabling true concurrency, AgentRoom could significantly improve the efficiency and reliability of AI-driven software development, impacting both the AI and software engineering communities. The protocol was evaluated on four backend coding tasks with cross-language checks in Python DevBench and Rust+axum. For CLI-stable models, AgentRoom with 2 agents abandons fewer tasks than Solo and has less run-to-run variation; at matched-compute, one positive mean LLM-judge contrast puts AgentRoom over parallel-merge, and a bundle probe shows full AgentRoom outperforms each partial case, indicating coordination is the key factor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Conflict-free Replicated Data Types (CRDTs) are data structures that allow multiple replicas to update independently and concurrently without coordination, ensuring eventual consistency. Multi-agent coding systems typically suffer from serialization because LLMs generate tokens one at a time, leading to sequential handoffs or uncoordinated parallel sampling. AgentRoom applies CRDT-based realtime collaborative editing to coordinate coding agents, similar to how tools like Google Docs enable human collaboration.

## [Serving Masked Diffusion LLMs: Real Hardware Characterization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23807) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first characterization of masked diffusion language model (dLLM) serving on real hardware, using LLaDA-8B-Instruct with a D2F LoRA adapter on a single NVIDIA H200 GPU. It reveals that request difficulty is discrete and unpredictable, and that only 24% of single-request wall-clock time is GPU computation, with batching improving throughput by 16.0x at batch size 16. This work challenges assumptions from autoregressive (AR) serving, showing that dLLM serving requires parallelism at each denoising step, which has implications for admission control and eviction policies. It provides actionable insights for designing serving infrastructure for diffusion LLMs, which are emerging as faster alternatives to AR models. The study found that requests fall into 11 fixed step-count levels (178 + 29k), and no tested signal predicts the difficulty level before generation (best R2 = 0.150). Benchmarks with generation budgets below 320 tokens understate serving variance, and the paper derives a batch-timeout rule for fixed-fill synchronized batching under Poisson arrivals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text by denoising multiple tokens simultaneously, potentially faster than autoregressive (AR) models. However, serving systems for dLLMs have been built without first measuring their behavior under concurrent load, risking the carryover of AR-specific assumptions. This paper addresses that gap by characterizing dLLM serving on real hardware, using LLaDA-8B-Instruct, a large language model, with a Discrete Diffusion Forcing (D2F) LoRA adapter.

## [ExTS: Budget-Aware Tree Search for Agentic Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23848) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ExTS, a tree-search policy for budget-constrained agentic search that treats expansion as a value-of-information decision, combining discriminative reward shaping, a stochastic virtual child, and quality-conditioned branching. It achieves an average relative gain of +5.5% over task-specific baselines across prompt optimization, code generation, molecular structure elucidation, and agentic workflow optimization. This work addresses a critical limitation of standard MCTS in budget-constrained settings, where exploration bonuses and poor branching decisions waste limited evaluation budgets. By improving search efficiency, ExTS can enhance the performance of LLM agents in practical applications where validation is expensive, potentially benefiting fields like automated prompt engineering and code generation. ExTS uses a stochastic virtual child to estimate the value of creating a new branch from the parent's reward history, and quality-conditioned branching expands only when a node's score justifies the budget cost. The paper also introduces pilot-run diagnostics to characterize structural differences among budget-constrained agentic search problems, offering practical guidance for adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic search involves LLM agents iteratively refining candidates under a limited evaluation budget, where each validation is expensive. Standard Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) often allocates budget poorly in such settings due to exploration bonuses dominating at low visit counts and branching independent of node quality. ExTS addresses these issues by treating expansion as a value-of-information decision, combining mechanisms like discriminative reward shaping and quality-conditioned branching.

## [Semantic Overlays: New Defense Against Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23873) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Semantic Overlays, learned adapters that add an out-of-band annotation channel to LLM inputs, mitigating prompt injection attacks. On benchmarks, SEP separation rose from 24.3% to 96.5%, TensorTrust attack success fell from 34.8% to 6.6%, and all PIArena attack families dropped to 0% compliance. This addresses a critical security vulnerability in LLMs, offering a novel defense that preserves utility while blocking a broad class of attacks. It could significantly improve the safety of LLM-based applications, especially those handling untrusted content. Semantic Overlays are small adapters applied at chosen prefill positions to a frozen model's residual stream, composable and capable of carrying complex payloads. They differ from steering vectors by being trained end-to-end, selectively applied, and multiplexed, while marked spans remain readable (92.5% exact copy rate).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt injection attacks exploit the fact that LLMs process all input as tokens, making it hard for the model to distinguish between instructions and untrusted data. Traditional defenses rely on text-based filtering or steering vectors, which can be circumvented. Semantic Overlays add a non-textual channel to communicate span identity, providing a more robust defense.

## [Framework Quantifies System-Level Harms from AI in Critical Infrastructure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23906) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a framework that links structured hazard analysis, component-level testing, and probabilistic system modeling to quantify system-level harms from AI adoption. It is demonstrated on the UK's Real Time Gross Settlement (RTGS) system, showing that adversarial manipulation of LLM-based trading can increase bank failures and lower the threshold for cascading disruption. This work addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation by moving beyond model-centric metrics to system-level risk assessment, which is essential for AI safety and governance in critical national infrastructure. It provides a traceable pathway from model behavior to systemic outcomes, enabling evidence-based and anticipatory governance. The framework uses Systems Theoretic Process Analysis (STPA) for hazard identification and a financial contagion model for component-to-system mapping. Component-level experiments show that simple adversarial inputs induce measurable behavioral shifts, and these shifts increase system fragility, particularly under widespread or monopolistic AI adoption.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: AI is increasingly integrated into complex sociotechnical systems, including Critical National Infrastructure (CNI), where harms emerge from interactions between technical, human, and organizational elements. Current AI evaluation remains model-centric, offering little insight into how observed behaviors translate into system-level risk. This paper bridges that gap by combining hazard analysis, component testing, and probabilistic modeling, using the UK's RTGS system as an illustrative example.

## [PROOF-Gen: Recovering Golden Trajectories from Failed Teacher Trials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23911) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PROOF-Gen introduces a per-scenario prompt optimization method that recovers golden trajectories from failed teacher trials in tool-calling distillation. On τ²-bench, it recovers 93% of failed scenarios and significantly improves student model performance, e.g., Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 Pass^1 from 0.132 to 0.529. This method addresses a significant inefficiency in post-training pipelines, where failed teacher trajectories are typically discarded, wasting costly frontier-teacher resources. By recovering useful data from failures, PROOF-Gen can improve distillation efficiency and model performance, benefiting developers of deployable tool-calling agents. The reflector analyzes execution traces and evaluation feedback to write corrective guidance, which is stripped before training so students learn from clean demonstrations. In a deployed pipeline, the method lifts trajectory quality by +6.3pp goal completion and transfers to an on-device model with positive transfer across all locales.

**Background**: Supervised fine-tuning on teacher-generated trajectories is the standard first stage for distilling tool-calling capabilities into deployable models. However, the generate-and-filter mechanism discards failed trajectories, leaving hard scenarios unresolved. τ²-bench is a benchmark for evaluating tool-calling and dialogue capabilities, where 57% of teacher trials fail, two-thirds being near-misses.

## [MARS: Multi-Specialist LLM Relay System Boosts Competitive Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23918) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MARS is a prompt-only framework that uses retrieval-augmented generation to select topic-specialist LLM agents, which iteratively refine competitive programming solutions. On the CodeContests test split with Gemma 4, it achieves a 0.624 pass rate, improving over direct prompting by 14.4 percentage points. This work addresses a known limitation in generic multi-agent pipelines for code generation by introducing topic-specialized agents, potentially improving performance in competitive programming and other complex code generation tasks. It also demonstrates the effectiveness of retrieval-augmented generation in guiding agent selection and refinement. MARS uses a starter to write an initial C++17 solution, then each subsequent turn runs the candidate against public examples in a sandbox, allowing the active specialist to keep, repair, or hand off the draft. A single infrastructure-fixer pass normalizes boilerplate at the end. The framework achieves 2.3 pipeline stages per task, with 3.3x lower wall-clock cost than CodeSIM and smaller variance in token spend.

Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLM outputs by retrieving relevant information from external sources, and CodeContests is a benchmark for evaluating AI on competitive programming problems.

## [Data Mixing as Mixture Experiment: RSM and Optimal Design for LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23922) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formalizes data mixing for LLM pretraining as a mixture experiment, using response surface methodology and optimal design to improve proxy-based methods. It demonstrates the framework with RegMix, showing that sparse Scheffé models preserve mixture rankings and that model-robust I-optimal designs can recover the relevant ordering with about 25% fewer proxy runs. This work provides a new theoretical lens for a practical and costly problem in LLM development, potentially improving efficiency and interpretability of data mixing. It suggests that data mixing should be treated as an experimental-design problem, not just a prediction problem, which could lead to more efficient use of compute in pretraining. The paper uses sparse second-order Scheffé response-surface models and constructs model-robust I-optimal designs for proxy experiments. The analysis reveals that domain value is relational, with weak domains becoming favorable through pairwise interactions, especially with web-derived text.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Response surface methodology (RSM) is a collection of statistical techniques for modeling and optimizing responses using designed experiments, often with second-degree polynomial models. Optimal experimental design aims to choose design points to maximize statistical efficiency, with I-optimal designs focusing on precise predictions. In LLM pretraining, data mixing involves allocating a fixed token budget across domains, and proxy-based methods train small models on candidate mixtures to guide larger-scale training.

A new arXiv paper introduces a unified framework for test-time reasoning methods, categorizing them as recursion operators GROW, PRUNE, and BRANCH. In a large-scale evaluation across five benchmarks and three frontier models, BRANCH consistently improved accuracy in all 14 settings by an average of 5.98 percentage points. This work provides a standardized evaluation protocol for test-time reasoning, addressing the lack of comparability across studies. ## [Tensor Parallelism vs KV Compression: No Cost Crossover Found](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23962) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.23962) systematically compares tensor parallelism (degree 1-8) and KV cache compression (16/8/4-bit, keep-ratios down to 0.25) for LLM serving on a unified cost-latency axis, using a profiled simulator calibrated on A100, A40, and H100 hardware. The study finds no cost-equivalence crossover across tested configurations, with compression being 1.20x to 2.00x cheaper. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM serving research by directly comparing two popular memory-relief strategies on the same cost axis, providing practical guidance for deployment decisions. The negative result challenges assumptions about when to scale GPUs versus compress KV cache, potentially influencing how systems are designed and budgeted. The study covers Llama-2 7B and 70B models across three GPU types, finding that compression dominates for models below roughly 36B parameters on 80GB devices, while tensor parallelism becomes necessary for larger models where weights are the binding resource. Compression improves capacity per dollar by up to 16.5x, while tensor parallelism improves latency by up to 8-93% (though compression worsens latency).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM serving often faces memory bottlenecks due to the KV cache, which stores intermediate attention states. Tensor parallelism distributes weights and KV cache across multiple GPUs, increasing memory headroom but adding communication overhead (all-reduce) and hardware costs. KV cache compression reduces memory footprint via quantization or eviction, potentially degrading quality but keeping single-GPU deployment. This paper uses a profiled simulator to compare these strategies on a cost-normalized basis.

This paper introduces a controlled evaluation framework for interactive visual grounding in large vision-language models (LVLMs), varying the amount of target information provided upfront versus acquired through dialogue. Across four visual contexts and four interaction protocols, current LVLMs significantly underperform human baselines, especially when no initial description is given and target information must be acquired through questions. The findings highlight that proactive question-driven grounding remains a major challenge, which is important for developing more capable and reliable multimodal AI systems for human-AI interaction. The study uses four human-grounded visual contexts and four interaction protocols, and finds that interaction can help when follow-up questions refine or repair an initial description. LVLMs are also poorly calibrated, often reporting confidence that exceeds their empirical accuracy, and follow-up studies confirm these patterns across varied description sources, reasoning efforts, repeated interactions, and visual contexts.

## [Behavior-Aware Framework Selects Diverse LLM Crowds for Better Future Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24001) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a behavior-aware framework that clusters LLMs by reasoning traces and selects representative models, showing that a three-model medoid crowd outperforms voting over all 25 models on two future-prediction benchmarks while cutting model calls by 88% and inference cost by ~80%. This work challenges the assumption that larger crowds are always better, showing that crowd composition matters more than size. It offers a practical method to build efficient and accurate LLM ensembles for forecasting, which could reduce computational costs while improving prediction quality in real-world applications. The framework uses seven development benchmarks to model behavioral diversity and two future-prediction benchmarks for evaluation. It employs K-means++ clustering on reasoning traces and selects medoid models, demonstrating that representative behavioral diversity, rather than maximizing diversity, is key.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: The wisdom-of-the-crowd effect suggests that aggregating predictions from many individuals improves accuracy. Prior work on LLM ensembles, such as the 'Wisdom of the Silicon Crowd' study, showed that ensembles of LLMs can match human crowd performance. However, simply adding more LLMs may lead to redundant behaviors, so selecting diverse models is crucial.

<a id="item-34"></a>
## [Retrieval-Grounded Voting Fixes Copy Inflation in Multi-Turn Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24024) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Retrieval-Grounded Voting (RGV), a method that scores LLM rollouts by lexical overlap between final answers and retrieved documents, overcoming the copy inflation problem that degrades confidence-based voting in multi-turn search agents. Across four benchmarks and five LLMs, RGV consistently outperforms confidence-based voting, with gains up to +5.4% accuracy and +35% on minority-correct questions. This work addresses a critical failure mode in test-time scaling for LLM agents, which are increasingly used in retrieval-augmented generation and agentic AI. By providing a practical, training-free solution, RGV could improve the reliability and efficiency of multi-turn search agents, impacting applications like web search, question answering, and autonomous reasoning. RGV computes the voting signal outside the contaminated context, avoiding token log probabilities and additional LLM calls. The method shows particular strength on minority-correct questions, where the correct answer appears in only 1-2 of 8 rollouts, achieving up to +35% improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Confidence-based voting aggregates parallel LLM rollouts by weighting each with internal signals like token log probabilities, and has been effective for single-turn reasoning. However, in multi-turn search agents, retrieved documents appended to the context cause copied tokens to receive inflated log probabilities, flattening confidence scores and weakening the vote. RGV sidesteps this by using lexical overlap between the final answer and retrieved documents as the scoring signal.

## [AI Alignment Reformulated as Social Choice via Convex Impact Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24046) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper presents a novel reformulation of AI alignment as a linear optimization problem over a convex impact space, connecting it to welfare economics and mechanism design. It demonstrates that voting-by-issues and random-dictatorship mechanisms are strategyproof and unanimous, and derives alignment protocols that maximize utilitarian welfare under constraints. This work provides a rigorous theoretical framework that could guide the design of more socially responsible AI alignment protocols, addressing a fundamental gap in current RLHF-based approaches. It offers a way to explicitly incorporate welfare considerations into AI systems, which is crucial as AI increasingly affects multiple stakeholders. The reformulation allows translating alignment protocols into welfare consequences and vice versa, enabling the derivation of protocols that maximize utilitarian social welfare subject to bounds on individual or group harm. The authors empirically illustrate welfare implications using real human preferences in domains such as kidney allocation, charitable food distribution, LLM responses, and trolley problems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment aims to ensure AI systems behave in accordance with human intentions, but when multiple humans are affected, it becomes a social choice problem of aggregating preferences. Reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) is the standard approach but lacks social choice guarantees. This paper leverages welfare economics and mechanism design to offer a more principled alternative.

## [Adversarial Vulnerabilities in Multi-Agent Trading Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24069) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first systematic empirical study of adversarial attacks on LLM-based multi-agent trading systems, introducing role-specific adversaries and evaluating four communication topologies across five assets and two backbones. As multi-agent trading systems move from research to live deployments controlling real assets, understanding their vulnerabilities is critical to prevent financial losses. The study's realistic threat model, which assumes no privileged access, highlights that even low-barrier attacks can compromise these systems, urging the need for more robust designs. The study decomposes a widely-used trading pipeline into four functional roles—Analyst, Researcher, Trader, and Risk Manager—and pairs each with a matched attack. It introduces the Adversarial Signal Preservation Score (APS) to analyze why certain architectures are more robust, finding that no architecture is inherently robust.

**Background**: LLM-based multi-agent trading systems use specialized agents that collaborate through structured communication to make trading decisions, mimicking real-world trading firms. Adversarial attacks on LLMs involve manipulating inputs or prompts to cause harmful outputs, and in multi-agent systems, corrupted signals can propagate through inter-agent communication to affect final decisions.

## [Compression Trinity: Joint Sparsity, Quantization, Low-Rank for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24070) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This thesis introduces the 'Compression Trinity' framework, which jointly applies sparsity, quantization, and low-rank approximations to compress and accelerate Large Language Models. It presents several methods, including MKOR and SLoPe, which achieve up to 1.85x and 1.25x speedups respectively. This unified approach addresses the limitations of applying compression techniques in isolation, which each hit an accuracy-efficiency wall. By combining these techniques, it enables more efficient and scalable deployment of LLMs, reducing computational and environmental costs, which is crucial for the broader AI ecosystem. The framework includes methods like OPTIMA, which stabilizes static masks in a zero-training regime, improving zero-shot accuracy by up to 3.97%, and PATCH, which learns a dynamic hybrid sparsity ratio, yielding up to 1.38x speedups. SLiM realizes the full Trinity in one shot, improving accuracy by up to 5.66% over state-of-the-art methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) require significant computational and memory resources, making efficient compression essential. Traditional techniques like sparsity, quantization, and low-rank approximations are often applied separately, but each has limitations. This thesis proposes a unified framework to combine them, leveraging their complementary strengths to achieve better efficiency and accuracy.

## [EMRB: New Benchmark Tests LLM Reasoning on Raw Electromagnetic Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24086) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EMRB, a new benchmark introduced on arXiv, evaluates LLMs' ability to analyze raw I/Q electromagnetic signals by writing and running code. It includes 200 problems across five difficulty levels and 27 question types, and tests 14 LLMs, with scores ranging from 24.1% to 78.9%. This benchmark addresses an untested capability of LLMs in analyzing raw physical-layer measurements, which is crucial for scientific and engineering applications. The significant performance gaps across model families and task complexity highlight the need for further research and development in this area. EMRB provides only raw I/Q captures, requiring models to discover quantities through code. The proposed ReconPilot method, which separates signal reconnaissance, targeted analysis, and self-verification, improves scores by 3.8 to 17.6 points across three backbones and improves 13 of 15 backbone-level combinations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: I/Q signals represent the amplitude and phase of an electromagnetic wave, commonly used in wireless communications. OFDM (Orthogonal Frequency-Division Multiplexing) is a multicarrier modulation technique that encodes data on multiple orthogonal subcarriers, widely used in modern digital communication systems. LLMs are increasingly used as code agents to perform analysis by writing and executing code, but their performance on raw signal data has been largely unexplored.

## [AnTrap Benchmark Reveals Universal Vulnerabilities in Android GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24099) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced AnTrap, a new benchmark that injects dynamic perturbations into Android GUI agent trajectories to evaluate robustness against runtime anomalies. Testing 16 leading GUI models, they found universal vulnerability, with even the strongest models suffering significant performance degradation. This benchmark fills a critical gap in existing GUI agent evaluations, which typically assume static environments. By revealing that all tested models are vulnerable to runtime anomalies, it highlights the need for more robust agent design and training, impacting the broader AI/ML and systems research community. AnTrap organizes anomalies into a taxonomy with four layers (State, Thinking, Action, Round) and ten fine-grained subcategories. The construction pipeline preserves task solvability while introducing realistic adversarial conditions, and GRPO training in adversarial environments shows that single-step traps are largely addressable, but deep contextual traps like state deadlock expose intrinsic limitations.

**Background**: GUI agents are AI systems that interact with graphical user interfaces, such as those on Android devices, to perform tasks. Runtime anomalies include unexpected pop-ups or action misuse that can disrupt agent performance. Existing benchmarks often overlook these dynamic conditions, and GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm used to fine-tune models, which the authors employed to test whether training can overcome such anomalies.

## [AHEAD: Step-Aware Self-Distillation Boosts Agentic RL Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24114) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AHEAD introduces a step-aware framework for agentic reinforcement learning that adaptively applies different supervision sources—environment feedback on all steps and LLM-generated corrective hints on error steps—to improve multi-turn LLM agent training. It achieves significant gains over GRPO, with +13.3 points on ALFWorld and +11.0 on WebShop at 7B scale. This work addresses a key limitation in existing self-distillation methods by distinguishing routine and error steps, potentially improving sample efficiency and success rates for LLM agents in interactive tasks. It could accelerate the development of more capable agentic AI systems across various domains. AHEAD introduces minimal changes to the standard GRPO algorithm, making it easy to integrate into existing pipelines. It was evaluated on ALFWorld, WebShop, and Search-based QA across three model scales, consistently outperforming outcome-only RL and prior self-distillation baselines in both success rate and training efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) trains LLM agents to interact with environments over multiple turns, but trajectory-level rewards assign uniform advantage to all steps, failing to identify which decisions caused success or failure. Self-distillation methods augment RL with privileged information from a teacher model, but existing approaches apply the same type of supervision to every step, ignoring the asymmetry between routine and error steps. AHEAD leverages environment feedback as a dense grounded signal and LLM-generated hints specifically on error steps to provide corrective direction.

The paper introduces RobustTests, a framework that synthesizes test cases from 'near correct' faulty code and uses validator agents with behavioral clustering to filter invalid tests, plus a stepwise dense reward function to reduce reward hacking in RLVR for code generation. Fine-tuning Qwen3-32B with RobustTests on CodeContests achieves a 3% absolute improvement on LiveCodeBench. This work addresses a critical limitation in RLVR for code generation—insufficient test coverage leading to false positives and reward hacking—which can degrade policy quality. By improving test case quality and reward shaping, RobustTests could enhance the reliability and performance of LLM-based code generation, benefiting AI-assisted software engineering. The framework uses a faulty-code-driven synthesis strategy to capture latent logical discrepancies, and integrates validator agents with behavioral feature clustering for granular filtering. The dense reward function is based on pass rates, providing fine-grained feedback to mitigate false negatives from hallucination noise in synthetic tests.

However, the quality of test cases directly impacts training effectiveness; insufficient coverage can cause false positives, leading to reward hacking where the model exploits reward function flaws without truly solving the task. RobustTests aims to mitigate these issues by synthesizing more comprehensive test cases and shaping rewards to provide denser feedback.

The paper introduces D3-Omni, a balanced and decoupled benchmark for diagnosing fine-grained multimodal understanding in OmniJudges, covering 53 orthogonal binary dimensions and 10,671 samples across text-to-image, video, and speech generation tasks. It uses a dual-balanced, decoupled, and dynamic design to expose hidden capability gaps in these judges. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating multimodal judges, which are increasingly used for automatic annotation and evaluation. By providing a balanced and decoupled lens, it helps expose systematic blind spots in these models, potentially leading to more reliable evaluation and better model development. The D3-Omni benchmark includes 53 orthogonal binary dimensions (17 for T2I, 22 for T2V, 14 for TTS) and 10,671 samples (3,526 for T2I, 1,998 for T2V, 5,147 for TTS). It achieves near 1:1 per-dimension parity and uniform distribution over total-score levels, and its decoupled design ensures each error is attributable to a single capability.

## [Paritok-4B: Intent-Conditioned Extractive Context Compression for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24188) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Paritok-4B is a 4B LoRA-based extractive compressor that uses intent conditioning to reduce token usage in coding-agent trajectories while preserving critical code spans. It compresses agent context to 25.7% of its original size on SWE-bench Lite, retaining 86.5% of uncompressed solve quality. This addresses a critical pain point in LLM-based coding agents, where context dominates token costs and general-purpose compressors fail on code. By being extractive and intent-conditioned, Paritok-4B offers a more effective and economically viable solution, potentially improving agent efficiency and reducing operational costs. The model is a 264 MB adapter that self-hosts on a single 24 GB GPU, with no per-token compressor fee, making it economically superior to using gpt-5 as a compressor. It was distilled from a gpt-4.1-mini teacher over 67,074 real OpenHands trajectories, and on line-numbered input it compresses slightly less (27.8%) but retains more (89.3%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents like SWE-bench operate by repeatedly sending large file reads and tool outputs to a frontier LLM, which dominates their token usage. General-purpose prompt compressors are trained on prose and often paraphrase identifiers or drop exact code spans needed for edits. Extractive compression selects spans from the original input rather than rewriting them, preserving fidelity. Intent conditioning tailors the compression based on the agent's current task, improving relevance of retained lines.

MetaRAG introduces a novel framework that aligns the agent's internal belief about answerability with its search actions, using Verify-first Action Generation and Internal Belief Probing. It consistently improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off on seven public QA benchmarks over strong RL-based agentic RAG baselines. This work addresses a key limitation in agentic RAG by explicitly modeling the agent's internal belief, which existing RL-based methods overlook. It could influence future RL-based RAG methods and improve the practical deployment of agentic systems in deep research and other settings. The belief probe is used only during training, introducing no inference-time overhead. The consistency reward is gated by answer correctness to avoid reinforcing internally consistent but incorrect trajectories, and gains transfer to deep research settings, different optimizers, and multiple model backbones.

Reinforcement learning (RL) is often used to optimize these decisions, but existing methods rely on external supervision and ignore the model's internal belief about evidence sufficiency. MetaRAG reformulates search decision quality as belief-action alignment, using a training-only probe to estimate the model's own answerability belief.

Researchers introduced TRACE, an evidence-grounded safety evaluation benchmark covering prompts, reasoning traces, and final responses of Large Reasoning Models (LRMs). It includes prompts in two languages, nine risk categories, and ten attack strategies, with safety annotations and supporting evidence for each component. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI safety evaluation, as reasoning traces can contain unsafe content even when final responses appear safe. It highlights the need for guardrail models that can detect and localize unsafe content across the entire LRM inference pipeline, which is essential for deploying safe reasoning models. The study evaluated 18 guardrail models on TRACE and found that safety judgment for reasoning traces is substantially more challenging than for prompts or final responses. Current models also struggle to accurately extract supporting evidence, indicating a need for improved evidence attribution in guardrail models.

## [SA-Bench: Benchmarking Semantic Alignment in LLM Paper Reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24252) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SA-Bench introduces a diagnostic benchmark with 1,491 Semantic Alignment Units (SAUs) across 30 papers from ICLR, ICML, and NeurIPS 2025, evaluating 12 LLM configurations. The best configuration (Claude+PaperCoder) achieves a mean SAU score of only 0.301, with an overall mean of 0.221 across 360 evaluations. This benchmark systematically quantifies semantic drift in LLM-generated paper reproduction code, revealing that current agents often implement requirements incorrectly despite attempting most. It provides a framework to guide future research toward semantic specification verification, which is crucial for scientific reproducibility in AI. SAUs decompose paper specifications into atomic, verifiable claims, evaluated along four dimensions: numerical, methodological, protocol, and ordering drift. The failure taxonomy shows that implementation mismatch and stubs account for most zero-scored claims, and scaffolds optimized for executability provide limited leverage for scientific reproduction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents can generate code from research papers, but often produce scientifically unfaithful implementations, a failure mode termed semantic drift. SA-Bench addresses this by providing a diagnostic benchmark with Semantic Alignment Units (SAUs) to measure alignment. The benchmark covers 30 papers from top ML conferences and evaluates multiple models and scaffolds, including PaperCoder, a multi-agent system for paper-to-code synthesis.

<a id="item-47"></a>
## [RePolicy: RL-Based Safety-Policy Invocation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24275) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RePolicy is a new agent safeguard that uses reinforcement learning (RL) to learn safety-policy invocation for LLM agents, rather than relying on prompting or supervised fine-tuning. It was trained on the new PolicyTraj-20K dataset using GRPO with verifiable rewards and policy-context perturbation, and it demonstrates strong safety-detection and policy-invocation performance across six benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent safety: adapting to unseen trajectories and dynamic policy contexts. By learning to invoke the correct safety policy, RePolicy could enable more robust and flexible safeguards for real-world AI agent deployments, reducing risks from policy mismatches. RePolicy uses a dynamic policy library and invokes the applicable policy to produce a policy-grounded rationale and safety judgment. The training pipeline includes supervised initialization on PolicyTraj-20K (over 20,000 policy-grounded trajectories), followed by GRPO with verifiable rewards and policy-context perturbation. The model backbone is Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507, and the released checkpoint is RePolicy-4B.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents execute multi-step trajectories, and their safety depends on context-dependent policies. Existing safeguards often treat policies as static prompt context or rely on supervised fine-tuning, which limits adaptability. Reinforcement learning, especially GRPO, has been used to train models with verifiable rewards, as seen in DeepSeek-R1, and RePolicy applies this to safety-policy invocation.

## [ReproAgent: Contract-Guided Paper-to-Code Reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24291) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ReproAgent introduces a four-stage Prepare-Plan-Generate-Repair pipeline that uses a persistent implementation contract with two channels (implementation-requirement and reference-evidence) to improve paper-to-code reproduction by AI agents. It achieves the highest mean score among same-backbone scaffolds on PaperBench Code-Dev under both Claude-Sonnet-4.5 and Gemini-3-Flash. This work addresses a critical challenge in scientific AI agents: faithfully reproducing research papers as executable code. By introducing persistent contracts, it offers a structured approach that could significantly improve the reliability and efficiency of AI-driven code generation, benefiting the broader scientific community and AI research ecosystem. The implementation-requirement channel converts paper snippets into code obligations, while the reference-evidence channel retrieves content and structure evidence from related repositories. Both channels are bound to work packages, projected into file-level contracts, and consumed across generation and repair stages. End-to-end channel ablations and per-paper cases support the contribution of both channels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Paper-to-code reproduction is a task where AI agents convert research papers into executable repositories that preserve the paper's method, protocol, and artifacts. This is challenging because the specification is split: explicit paper content like algorithms and metrics is often lost across long agent trajectories, while implicit details such as framework defaults and conventions from related work are absent from the paper. PaperBench Code-Dev is a benchmark that evaluates AI's ability to replicate AI research, focusing on code generation aspects.

<a id="item-49"></a>
## [Selective Regenerative Decoding Boosts LLM Reasoning Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24338) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Selective Regenerative Decoding (SRD) is introduced as a novel inference-time decoding method that refines only the degraded suffix portions of candidate trajectories while preserving useful prefixes, without requiring a larger target model. It achieves a provable 1.28-to-1.36-fold gain in sample efficiency over rejection sampling and matches Best-of-N accuracy with fewer generated tokens across multiple benchmarks. This work addresses a key inefficiency in inference-time reasoning by enabling segment-level intervention rather than whole-trajectory selection, opening a previously underexplored region of the accuracy-compute tradeoff. It offers a practical way to improve LLM reasoning quality and efficiency, which is crucial for deploying large models in resource-constrained settings. SRD routes each candidate to discard, keep, or refine only the degraded portion of the suffix, and the gain grows as the candidate pool grows. It was validated on MATH500, GPQA Diamond, HotpotQA, and AlpacaEval with multiple generation-reward model pairs, and it outperforms speculative rejection in low-compute regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Inference-time decoding methods for large language models (LLMs) improve reasoning by exploring multiple candidate trajectories, but they typically treat each trajectory as atomic, either keeping it whole or discarding it entirely. This wastes computation on partially promising candidates whose high-quality prefixes are abandoned alongside degraded suffixes. Rejection sampling is a common baseline that generates multiple candidates and selects the best one based on a reward model, but it can be inefficient. SRD builds on this idea by allowing partial refinement, which is more compute-efficient.

## [Handoff Tax: LLM Agent Model Switching Costs Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24358) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the concept of the 'handoff tax,' showing that when coding agents escalate from low-cost to high-cost models mid-task, full-trajectory transfer recovers less than half of the quality gap while incurring a substantial cost premium. The study also finds that the optimal handoff interface reverses with direction: reducing low-capability model trajectory information improves escalation quality, while removing high-capability model trajectory reduces downshift quality. This research addresses a practical cost-quality trade-off in LLM agent systems, providing empirical insights that can guide agent design and cost optimization strategies. The findings are relevant for AI engineering and systems research, potentially influencing how developers implement model handoffs in production. The study uses pairs of low-cost, low-capability (LC) and high-cost, high-capability (HC) models from the Claude and GPT families, varying handoff direction, timing, and interface. It compares full-trajectory transfer, compaction, and trajectory removal while preserving the repository state, and finds that downshift offers a favorable cost-quality point.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents perform long-running tasks involving many model calls, tool uses, and code edits. Users often face the choice of escalating to a stronger model when a cheaper one struggles, or downshifting once hard reasoning is complete. Each switch requires the receiver to continue a non-native trajectory produced by another model, which can affect quality and cost.

## [ResiSpec: Reshaping Proposal Distribution to Mitigate Residual Drift in Multi-Candidate Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24411) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ResiSpec introduces a framework that reshapes the proposal distribution during verification to anchor residual target mass within the draft model's high-confidence regions, mitigating Residual Drift in multi-candidate speculative sampling. It achieves up to 1.92x speedup over state-of-the-art multi-candidate methods without compromising output exactness. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in speculative decoding, which is essential for efficient LLM serving. By improving multi-candidate sampling efficiency, ResiSpec can significantly reduce inference latency and cost, benefiting applications that rely on large language models. ResiSpec mathematically re-aligns the verification process to prevent candidate obsolescence, maintaining zero-bias sampling. The framework is validated in multi-candidate settings such as batch or tree-based speculation, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by using a lightweight draft model to generate candidate tokens, which are then validated by the target LLM in parallel. Multi-candidate schemes extend this by proposing diverse candidate sets to increase acceptance likelihood. However, these schemes suffer from Residual Drift, where rejection of initial candidates shifts the residual target distribution away from the draft model's predictions, reducing efficiency. ResiSpec addresses this by reshaping the proposal distribution during verification.

## [Formalizing Construct Validity for LLM-as-a-Judge Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24419) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a two-dimensional framework for evaluating LLM-as-a-judge systems, measuring invariance (S) and construct sensitivity (R). Empirical results show that current judges achieve high invariance (S=0.945) but low sensitivity (R=0.319), with a notable gap between scope and strength edits. This work addresses a critical gap by formalizing construct validity for automated evaluation, which is essential for reliable NLP benchmarking. The findings suggest that high agreement among judges can mask weak sensitivity to meaningful changes, potentially impacting the validity of many existing evaluation protocols. The study evaluates 7 judges across 4 domains using 7 construct-changing intervention types and 5 register-only controls, with human annotators determining intervention direction. It also audits five public label sets, finding that surface-only predictors reproduce 55%-67% of labels in paired mode, including 67.4% of MT-Bench human votes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge evaluation relies on large language models to assess the quality of other model outputs, often measured by agreement with human judgments or robustness to surface perturbations. However, reliability does not guarantee that the judge is actually measuring the intended construct. Construct validity, a concept from psychometrics, refers to the extent to which a test measures what it claims to measure. This paper adapts this concept to LLM judges by proposing two independent dimensions: invariance (stability under irrelevant edits) and sensitivity (responsiveness to meaningful changes).

## [Bounding Counterfactuals via Query-Implied Causal Orders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24427) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method to bound arbitrary counterfactual and nested counterfactual queries without requiring a fully specified causal graph, by leveraging only the partial causal order implied by the query itself. The identification task is reduced to a linear program, and the bounds are proven tight by constructing compatible structural causal models. This work relaxes a key assumption in causal inference, making counterfactual analysis feasible in settings with incomplete domain knowledge. It generalizes the classical Tian-Pearl bounding framework, potentially broadening the applicability of causal reasoning in fields like fairness audits and decision-making. The method parametrizes the query explicitly, reducing identification to a linear program, and proves tightness by constructing structural causal models that attain the bounds while being compatible with observed data and the query-implied order. The authors validate their approach on several case studies from the literature, demonstrating informative insights even without an input causal graph.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Counterfactual queries ask 'what-if' questions in causal models, such as 'What would have happened if a different action had been taken?' Traditional non-parametric identification of such queries requires a fully specified causal graph, which may be unavailable in practice. The Tian-Pearl framework provides bounds for probabilities of causation under certain assumptions, but it also relies on a known causal structure. This paper relaxes that requirement by using only the partial order implied by the query itself.

## [Neurosymbolic Alignment Boosts Clinical LLM Safety to 90.8%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24534) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Neurosymbolic Alignment, a training-time framework that couples a 7B clinical LLM with an HGNN-based Physiological World Model over an 847K-node biomedical knowledge graph. It improves Clinical Safety Score (CSS) from 69.5% to 90.8% on the new Clinical Safety Benchmark (CSB), a 2,500-scenario benchmark. This work addresses a critical gap in clinical AI safety by grounding preference optimization in structured physiological knowledge rather than text-only supervision. It demonstrates significant safety gains over existing methods, including GPT-4, and could pave the way for safer deployment of LLMs in healthcare. The method uses homeostatic constraints, multi-hop path plausibility, and drug-interaction penalties to score candidate responses, driving iterative on-policy ORPO updates. It also introduces the Clinical Safety Benchmark (CSB) with 2,500 scenarios, and shows robustness to synthetic EHR-style noise (84.2% CSS retained).

The HGNN (Heterogeneous Graph Neural Network) is used to model the physiological world.

## [SMITH: Joint RL Training of Tool Creation and Use for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24571) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SMITH (Schema-grounded Multi-task Iterative Tool Honing), a reinforcement learning framework that jointly trains tool creation and tool use within a single policy. A 4B Qwen3 model trained with SMITH achieves 79.8 macro-average accuracy on held-out procedural reasoning tasks, outperforming all evaluated baselines. This work addresses a key limitation in tool-augmented language models by unifying tool creation and use, enabling models to generate tools they can effectively invoke. It demonstrates significant accuracy improvements on procedural reasoning tasks, potentially advancing the capabilities of LLM agents in complex problem-solving scenarios. SMITH uses three separate reward axes to independently catch schema, code, and outcome failures, providing distinct gradients for each failure mode. The trained 4B model also achieves 40.4 on TabMWP-Hard and 42.6 on out-of-domain GQA (+7.6 over the best same-backbone inference-time baseline), without any visual or tabular training data.

## [New MAPF Variant PS-MAPF: Solvability, NP-Hardness, and Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24585) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Pivot-and-Station Multi-Agent Path Finding (PS-MAPF), a new MAPF variant where agents must visit pivots before terminating at anonymous stations. It fully characterizes solvability, proves NP-hardness for minimizing station-makespan or station-flowtime, and presents three algorithms including a SAT-based optimal solver and Pivot-Prioritized Planning (PPP). PS-MAPF models real-world high-density storage systems like warehouses and robotic parking, where agents must use scarce resources and park without blocking future operations. The theoretical results and efficient algorithms could improve automation efficiency and provide a foundation for future research in MAPF variants. The paper proves that every instance on a 2-edge-connected graph is solvable, and provides a necessary and sufficient condition for arbitrary connected graphs based on an effective-distance measure. It also shows NP-hardness even with a single pivot, and the PPP algorithm solves 74-89% of benchmark instances with makespan and flowtime orders of magnitude below the baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-Agent Path Finding (MAPF) is a planning problem where multiple agents must find collision-free paths to their goals. PS-MAPF extends this by requiring agents to visit interchangeable pivots (e.g., workstations) before ending at anonymous stations, which is common in automated storage systems. Makespan and flowtime are key optimization objectives in scheduling and MAPF, measuring total completion time and sum of completion times, respectively.

## [Parason: Uncovering Trial Parallelism in LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24658) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Parason is a new framework that identifies and learns both subtask and trial parallelism in LLM reasoning, converting sequential reasoning traces into structured parallel trajectories using a context-free grammar and training with Parallelism-Aware Group Relative Policy Optimization (PA-GRPO). It achieves about 1.7x average acceleration on AIME24 and AIME25 benchmarks while maintaining competitive accuracy. This work addresses a critical latency bottleneck in test-time scaling of LLMs, where long sequential reasoning traces can take days or weeks. By revealing that trial parallelism accounts for 65.5% of parallelizable computation, Parason offers a more effective parallelization strategy than prior subtask-only approaches, potentially enabling faster and more practical deployment of reasoning models. The analysis shows that trial parallelism becomes increasingly dominant on harder problems, and Parason executes the learned parallel structure through tool calls at inference time to achieve real wall-clock acceleration. The method uses a context-free grammar to structure parallel trajectories and PA-GRPO to jointly optimize accuracy, latency, and parallelism ratios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling improves LLM problem-solving by allowing models to reason longer, but standard autoregressive decoding is sequential, causing high latency. Parallel reasoning can mitigate this, but prior systems mainly focused on subtask parallelism, where tasks are decomposed into independent chunks. Trial parallelism, where multiple speculative attempts are explored in parallel, was largely overlooked. Parason introduces a taxonomy and training method to exploit both forms.

## [Formalizing Inference-Time Steering and the Attribution Problem in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24662) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formalizes the Inference Attribution Problem, proving that black-box observations cannot distinguish between biases from model weights, post-training, or runtime inference policies. It also introduces 'Probability Placement' as a hidden commercial influence mechanism in deployed language models. This work is critical for AI transparency and regulation, as it shows that auditing a model alone is insufficient; the entire deployed system must be audited. It has direct implications for the EU AI Act, Digital Services Act, and advertising disclosure, potentially shaping future evaluation and governance standards. The paper establishes an observational non-identifiability result, meaning behaviorally equivalent systems can arise from different combinations of parameters and inference policies. It distinguishes 'Probability Placement' from explicit token-auction mechanisms, highlighting a subtle form of undisclosed commercial influence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Inference-time steering refers to modifying a language model's outputs during generation without changing its weights, often used for safety or personalization. The attribution problem in AI concerns determining which component of a system caused a particular behavior or outcome. Non-identifiability in machine learning means that multiple different models or parameter settings can produce the same observed data, making it impossible to uniquely infer the true underlying cause.

## [LLM Confidence Miscalibrated in Hidden-Information Chess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24691) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv study (2608.24691) reveals that LLM agents' stated confidence in a hidden-information chess variant is severely miscalibrated: high-confidence captures (≥0.5) were correct in only 1 of 62 cases across two batches. The calibration deficit is concentrated in these high-confidence events, accounting for 99.3% and 98.7% of the total deficit in the original and replication batches, respectively. This finding challenges the common practice of gating agentic actions on model confidence, showing that high confidence does not reliably indicate correctness in hidden-information scenarios. It has direct implications for AI safety and the reliability of LLM-based agents in high-stakes decision-making, as outcome-only evaluation may miss such miscalibration. The study used a chess variant where the royal piece's location is secretly and repeatedly relocated, and the agent's stated probability distribution over the hidden piece was elicited every turn. The calibration deficit persisted across two independent batches and four additional model configurations from a second provider, though most pairwise gaps were not statistically distinguishable. Notably, a configuration that won on all conventional axes (legality, cost, latency, completion rate) produced the worst belief quality, and a model exhibiting this pattern could still win the game, making outcome-only evaluation insufficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic systems increasingly use a model's self-reported confidence to decide whether to act, assuming confidence correlates with correctness. Calibration in LLMs refers to how well a model's predicted probabilities match actual outcomes. Hidden-information games like this chess variant require agents to reason under uncertainty, making them a useful testbed for evaluating calibration in dynamic, adversarial settings.
