<a id="item-61"></a>
## [Meta^n: Recursive Self-Improvement via Fixed Meta-Operation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24735) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Meta^n introduces a recursive self-improvement method for LLMs that keeps a fixed meta-operation Ω and applies it repeatedly to its own outputs, enabling emergent depth. Across two backbones, it outperforms prior self-improving agents on all eight benchmark families, notably scoring above zero on ARC-AGI-2 where others fail. This work addresses a key limitation in existing meta-level approaches by allowing deeper recursive self-improvement without destabilizing the system. It could significantly advance AI research by enabling LLMs to reason from progressively higher vantage points, potentially leading to more capable and autonomous agents. The meta-operation Ω reads traces of the solver stack and the code that produced them, then writes the next layer as a strategic pre-process and a library of callable helpers. Depth is determined by convergence rather than fixed in advance, and an evolutionary archive searches over layer chains; ablations show most gains come from conditioning passed between layers, with distinct layer roles emerging without explicit prompts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Recursive self-improvement is a hypothesized process where AI systems rewrite their own code to enhance capabilities, potentially leading to superintelligence. Existing methods either refine answers without improving the process, or add a fixed meta-level that caps meta-depth at roughly two. Meta^n keeps the meta-operation fixed but recurses on its input, allowing the input to grow and enabling deeper reasoning layers without destabilizing the system.

## [RACE: A Scalable Statistical Framework for Neuron Consistency in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24758) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RACE (Residual Alignment for Consistency Estimation) is introduced as a forward-pass statistical framework that evaluates the domain-wide functional consistency of Transformer neurons, achieving two orders of magnitude lower computational overhead than gradient-based methods. Perturbation experiments show it has superior domain specificity compared to gradient-based point estimates. This work addresses a key challenge in mechanistic interpretability by enabling scalable, domain-wide analysis of neuron behavior, which is crucial for understanding and ensuring the safety of large language models. The significant computational advantage could make neuron-level analysis practical for large-scale models, potentially accelerating research in interpretability and alignment. RACE recasts neuron analysis as inference over latent alignment distributions, isolating neurons whose domain contributions are directionally aligned and statistically stable. The framework is validated through perturbation experiments and token-distribution-level results, confirming the association between selected neurons and target domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks into human-understandable algorithms and circuits, which is important for AI safety. Traditional methods for analyzing neuron behavior often rely on gradient-based point estimates, which can be computationally expensive and may obscure population-level variability. RACE offers a forward-pass statistical approach that estimates functional consistency across entire domains, providing a more scalable and domain-specific alternative.

## [StepGuard: Step-Level Guardrails for Safer LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24777) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StepGuard introduces a step-level guard model for LLM agents, trained with an automatic data engine (StepGen) and a balancing algorithm (Balance-GRPO), achieving state-of-the-art safety accuracy among open-weight models and reducing attack success rate by 77.3% on AgentDojo and AgentDyn. This addresses a critical gap in LLM agent safety by enabling pre-execution monitoring of tool actions, which is essential for real-world deployment where agents interact with external environments. The open-weight model with performance comparable to GPT-5.4 could democratize access to robust safety mechanisms. StepGuard is a 4B parameter model that supports both pre-execution evaluation of candidate tool actions and post-hoc auditing of completed trajectories. The Balance-GRPO algorithm dynamically adjusts learning weights based on observed accuracy to mitigate over-defense and under-defense.

**Background**: LLM agents can invoke tools to interact with external environments, but this introduces security risks such as file modification and information leakage. Traditional guardrails often evaluate completed trajectories, missing the opportunity to prevent unsafe actions before execution. StepGuard leverages GRPO, a reinforcement learning algorithm used in models like DeepSeek-R1, to balance safety and utility during training.

## [Medical Chain-of-Thought Often Decoupled from Answers, Audit Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24790) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new perturbation audit of medical chain-of-thought reasoning reveals that LLMs' reasoning chains are often not causally linked to their answers. Across 14 LLMs and four medical QA benchmarks, the Chain-Decoupling Rate (CDR) was 72.9% on clinically meaningful destructive edits, and chain corruption did not change accuracy. This matters because clinicians may rely on chain-of-thought rationales as evidence of medical reasoning, but if these chains are not faithful, they could mislead clinical decisions. The findings highlight a critical safety gap in medical AI, urging more rigorous evaluation before deployment. The audit uses a 30-operator battery with clinically motivated edits (severity reversal, negation flip, demographic swap, evidence ablation) and a joint chain-update/answer-flip analysis. Two board-certified clinicians re-annotated 197 perturbed questions, confirming 98.5% of gold answers remained defensible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting encourages LLMs to produce step-by-step reasoning before answering, which often improves performance. However, faithfulness—whether the stated reasoning actually reflects the model's internal process—is a known concern. General-domain CoT faithfulness probes often ignore clinical cost, and medical LLM evaluations treat the chain as a black box. This paper introduces a medical-specific perturbation audit to address that gap.

## [CAFE: Co-Evolving Feedback for Self-Improving Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24794) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CAFE introduces a framework where a shared-parameter model alternates between search-agent and critic roles, using online RL with comparative feedback estimation and offline preference optimization to co-evolve feedback with the agent's policy. It outperforms existing RL-based search agents on seven benchmarks and reduces hallucinations. This work addresses a critical limitation in outcome-supervised search agents: the inability to correct errors mid-trajectory. By co-evolving feedback, CAFE enables more robust self-improvement, potentially advancing the field of self-improving AI systems and improving reliability in agentic search tasks. CAFE uses a prompt-level call-skip success gap to shape request returns and feedback-aware advantage shaping to reweight token advantages. Offline, it employs rollout-derived preference optimization from matched successful and unsuccessful trajectories. Ablations show that alternating agent and critic updates is essential, as improving only one plateaus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Outcome-supervised search agents learn when and how to retrieve evidence, but terminal rewards do not localize intermediate errors or redirect ongoing trajectories. Corrective feedback as a learned in-trajectory intervention couples agent and critic roles, requiring co-evolution. CAFE builds on this by sharing parameters and using online/offline optimization.

## [StarHarness: Evolving Agent Harnesses via Stratified Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24804) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StarHarness introduces a framework that evolves environment-specific agent harnesses while keeping model weights fixed, using stratified search to improve full-benchmark performance by 20-35 percentage points across IT and finance domains. This approach addresses persistent model-environment mismatch in tool-rich enterprise tasks, offering a practical way to boost agent performance without retraining models. It demonstrates transferability across model families, potentially reducing the need for per-model tuning in enterprise automation. The evolved harness can include prompt and task framing, tool interfaces, skills, MCP-backed providers, subagent structure, and agent-loop configuration. It constructs a compact evolution pool by stratifying tasks based on baseline failure behavior, separates proposer-visible search tasks from proposer-hidden selection tasks, and reserves held-out tasks for evaluating generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness, also known as agent scaffolding, is the software infrastructure surrounding a large language model (LLM) that enables it to operate as an AI agent, managing tool use, memory, state persistence, execution environments, and feedback loops. Stratified search is a heuristic search technique that partitions the state space to guide search more efficiently, especially when heuristics are weak. MCP (Model Context Protocol) is a protocol that standardizes how AI agents interact with external tools and data sources.

## [Recuris: Recursive Memory Architecture Boosts Long-Horizon Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24876) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Recuris introduces a recursive Experiential-Working Memory architecture for long-horizon agent harnesses, where Working Memory tracks task progress and guides skill selection from Experiential Memory, enabling localized, validation-gated updates to Skill Memory. Across four benchmarks and ten models, it improved task success in 35 of 37 model-benchmark pairs, adding up to +17.8 points to GPT-5.6 Sol on tau-bench and +32.2 points on the longest tasks. This work addresses a key challenge in AI agents—maintaining task state and skill relevance over long horizons—by externalizing self-improvement to a memory-control layer, which could scale recursive self-improvement (RSI) more effectively. The significant gains across multiple models and benchmarks suggest that evolving memory is a promising foundation for building more capable long-horizon agents. The architecture couples Working Memory with Experiential Memory to ground skill selection in current task state rather than full history, and uses a fixed Meta-Agent to convert execution evidence into localized, validation-gated updates to Skill Memory, forming a bounded recursive loop. The advantage widens with interaction horizon, and common long-horizon failures drop by up to 80%; code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agent tasks require sustained reasoning and tool use across many steps, but growing histories can obscure task state and lead to misaligned skill invocation. Traditional agent harnesses often maintain execution and state within a growing context, making tracking difficult. Recuris externalizes memory into an experiential-working memory architecture, allowing agents to evolve their skills based on validated evidence, which is a form of recursive self-improvement (RSI).

## [Evidence-Carrying Termination: Safe Stopping for Tool-Using LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23623) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Evidence-Carrying Termination (ECT), a method requiring tool-using agents to return COMPLETE only when a typed certificate binds answer claims to valid trace evidence and deterministic replay reconstructs the claimed value. In controlled tests, ECT achieved 0/288 unsafe completions versus 252/288 for the termination-critic core, and 0/66 premature unsupported terminations versus 40/66 for the controller. This work addresses a critical AI safety problem: ensuring tool-using LLM agents stop only when their answers are supported by evidence, reducing unsafe completions and premature terminations. It provides a formal, verifiable mechanism that could be integrated into agent frameworks to improve reliability and trustworthiness in real-world deployments. The study crossed 48 fully synthetic tasks across six tool-use families with eight faults, and a frozen 576-trajectory study compared ECT with a critic core, a faithful controller, and a full-trace LLM critic. ECT achieved supported completion of 97/132 versus 92/132 for the controller, satisfying a -10-point noninferiority margin, and executed successful recovery in 18/66 trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-using LLM agents combine large language models with external tools and APIs, but their non-deterministic nature makes it hard to verify when they should stop. Existing systems gate terminal success, certify execution traces, or enforce runtime policies, but none combine typed certificates with deterministic replay at the COMPLETE boundary. ECT requires a typed certificate binding answer claims to trace evidence and a deterministic replay to reconstruct the claimed value, ensuring that termination is supported by verifiable evidence.

ToolRobustBench introduces a stage-wise diagnostic benchmark that systematically evaluates tool-calling agents under four perturbation families (tool-interface, user-intent, tool-output/observation, and runtime-environment). It attributes failures to specific stages such as tool selection, schema grounding, argument binding, and feedback handling, based on experiments with 15,456 instances across 7 models and 16 tools. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM agent robustness by moving beyond clean end-to-end accuracy to pinpoint where and how failures occur. It provides a deterministic, cascade-aware framework that can guide future improvements in tool-calling agents, which are fundamental to many real-world LLM applications. The benchmark includes 14 perturbation subtypes and reveals that tool-output/observation perturbations are the dominant bottleneck, causing substantial robustness degradation. Mixed-family experiments show non-additive failure patterns, indicating that isolated single-family results cannot predict combined effects.

## [Failure Feedback Backfires in Small Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23651) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.23651) reveals that recording failed tool calls and error messages in agent transcripts increases the probability that small language models repeat the same failed action, contrary to the assumption that such feedback is corrective. Across 6 instruction-tuned checkpoints (135M-1.7B, 4 families) in simulated tool calling and MBPP program repair, the corrective gain is negative, with the probability of repeating a failed call rising from 0.06 to 0.54. This finding challenges a common assumption in agent design and has significant implications for building reliable AI agents, especially in software engineering and tool-use scenarios. It suggests that current harness designs may inadvertently reinforce errors, and highlights the need for alternative feedback mechanisms. The study defines corrective gain as the change in log-probability of re-emitting the failed action, finding a normalized effect of about -1.03 nats per action token (a factor of 2.8 in odds). Counterfactual experiments show that the surface form of the failed call accounts for 83% of the damage, while the semantic marking of failure has a small and inconsistent effect. Replacing the verbatim call with a runtime-generated description removes 76% of the inversion, but an explicit 'do not repeat' instruction and deleting the failed attempt are ineffective or harmful.

**Background**: Language model agents use harnesses that record tool calls and observations in a transcript, and when a call fails, the error message is appended as feedback for the model to correct itself. This paper investigates whether such failure feedback actually helps, focusing on small instruction-tuned models. The study runs end to end on a CPU and releases all artifacts.

## [Elastic KV Cache Reclaims Idle Memory, But Chunked Prefill May Suffice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23658) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an elastic KV cache mechanism that dynamically lends reserved memory to the KV pool during decode and returns it before prefill, improving memory utilization without kernel changes. It reports an honest negative result: the benefit is minimal because chunked prefill already mitigates the prefill latency penalty. This work addresses a common inefficiency in LLM serving engines where reserved KV cache memory sits idle during decode-dominant phases. The findings suggest that simpler configuration changes, like lowering max_num_batched_tokens, may achieve similar benefits, guiding future memory management optimizations. The mechanism uses pure userspace CUDA virtual memory management, mapping two physical handles into one contiguous virtual range per layer, requiring no driver patches. It decommits in milliseconds and recommits in tens of milliseconds, works with CUDA graphs and prefix caching, and never triggers OOM; however, the reserve dilutes under tensor parallelism from 16% of KV at TP1 to 2.7% at TP4.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: In LLM serving, the KV cache stores intermediate key and value tensors for attention, and its size is fixed at startup to accommodate worst-case prefill memory. Prefill processes input tokens in parallel, while decode generates tokens autoregressively; chunked prefill splits long prompts into smaller chunks to interleave with decode, reducing latency spikes. CUDA virtual memory management (introduced in CUDA 10.2) allows fine-grained control over physical memory allocation and mapping, enabling dynamic memory reuse.

## [LLMs as Causal-Edge Classifiers: Recall-Dominant and Miscalibrated](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23660) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically evaluates 12 instruction-tuned open-weight LLMs on direct causal-edge classification across six benchmark graphs, five prompting strategies, and four confidence sources, revealing that LLMs are recall-dominant, overpredict edges, and are miscalibrated. These findings highlight critical reliability issues for researchers using LLMs in causal discovery, suggesting that LLMs should be treated as sources of soft causal priors rather than direct evidence, which could influence future model design and prompting strategies. LLMs misclassify 40.0% of indirect and 36.0% of reversed non-edges as direct edges, with 80.8% and 84.6% of these false positives receiving verbalized confidence of at least 80%. Logit-based confidence collapses near 1.0, while cross-prompt and cross-model agreement offer better calibration, though not statistically significant after Holm correction.

## [Audit Finds On-Device LLM Confidently Wrong, Over-Refusing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23663) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper presents a reproducible reliability audit of a deployed on-device language model, revealing severe task-asymmetric miscalibration: it confabulates on 69% of false-premise questions while over-refusing 18% of benign inputs, with self-reported confidence being non-discriminative (AUROC 0.47, ECE 70). The authors also show that confident-correct and confident-wrong outputs are surface-indistinguishable, and propose a black-box consistency wrapper that reduces confident confabulation from 75% to 3%. This matters because on-device language models are shipped to hundreds of millions of devices without server-side moderation, and users or developers have no reliable way to know when the model is wrong. The findings highlight a critical gap in AI safety oversight for deployed models, and the proposed audit protocol and consistency wrapper offer practical tools for improving reliability. The audit used a model-agnostic protocol and released code and frozen evaluation items. The consistency wrapper requires no model access and improves selective accuracy from 43% to 83% at a tunable cost. A classifier over 15 user-visible features could only separate confident-correct from confident-wrong outputs at AUROC 0.55, confirming surface-indistinguishability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: On-device language models are increasingly deployed on edge devices, but they often lack server-side moderation, making reliability audits crucial. Calibration refers to how well a model's confidence matches its actual accuracy; miscalibration can lead to overconfident errors. Confabulation (or hallucination) is when a model generates plausible but false information, while over-refusal is when it rejects benign prompts due to over-conservative safety alignment. The paper's audit protocol and consistency wrapper address these issues in a resource-constrained setting.

## [TrustShiftProbe: Characterizing and Defending Staged Trust Attacks on MCP Servers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23763) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces TrustShift, a staged attack on MCP servers where a compromised server behaves benignly initially to build trust before switching to adversarial payloads. It also presents TrustShiftProbe, a framework with four contributions: a temporal threat model, a language-agnostic attack engine, a multi-tier defense called SHIELD, and a taxonomy of nine attack variants. This research highlights a critical security vulnerability in the emerging LLM agent ecosystem, where MCP servers are a standard connection layer. The proposed defense, SHIELD, reduces attack success from 69.5% to 42.7%, offering a practical mitigation for a threat that predeployment static analysis cannot detect. TrustShift attacks are temporal, not syntactic, meaning the server appears benign at deploy time and only defects after an interaction threshold. The taxonomy includes three execution mechanisms (structural violation, semantic corruption, scope expansion) and three adversarial objectives (disruption, exfiltration, combination). SHIELD is a zero-oracle runtime defense that audits server payloads against behavioral baselines learned during clean trust windows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that connects LLM agents to external tools and data sources, enabling dynamic tool invocation. However, this openness introduces server-side risks, as agents often invoke third-party tools without runtime integrity verification. TrustShift exploits this by having a compromised server behave benignly initially, evading predeployment static analysis, and then switching to adversarial payloads once trust is established.

This work addresses a key challenge in AI-human collaboration: understanding persistent skill rather than transient performance. By providing interpretable and transferable skill representations, SAIL could enable more effective coaching, assistance, and personalized interaction in various applications. SAIL represents each individual with a persistent skill embedding that controls a blend between expert and novice bases. The model is trained using counterfactual subskill swaps to encourage disentanglement, and it demonstrates improved behaviorally grounded disentanglement and downstream AI coaching performance over baselines.

## [LUCAID: Agentic AI System for Lung Cancer Precision Pathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23803) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LUCAID is an agentic AI system that integrates nine diagnostic modules for lung cancer pathology, covering tasks from tumor detection to biomarker scoring, and has been clinically validated. In prospective validation, it achieved 93.0% concordance with an expert-panel reference standard, outperforming five experienced thoracic pathologists (68.3-81.1%). This is significant because it demonstrates that an agentic AI system can handle the full complexity of lung cancer pathology workflow, potentially reducing interobserver variability and improving diagnostic accuracy. It represents a step toward integrating AI into routine clinical practice for precision oncology. The system includes modules for quality control, tumor detection and segmentation, histological subtyping, tumor microenvironment profiling, tumor cellularity quantification, and predictive biomarker scoring (PD-L1, MET, TROP-2), along with automated report generation. The analysis modules achieved F1 scores of 0.82-0.95 against large-scale expert ground-truth annotations.

## [GI-SAE Reveals Cross-Language Reasoning Invariance in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23809) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Geometry-Invariant Sparse Autoencoders (GI-SAE), a contrastive variant of sparse autoencoders that supplements reconstruction loss with an InfoNCE loss to encourage language-invariant feature representations. Applying GI-SAE to five multilingual LLMs on the MGSM dataset, the authors find that cross-language feature sharing is model- and architecture-dependent, with effects varying across models like Qwen, Gemma, Llama, and Phi. This work addresses a fundamental question in multilingual LLM interpretability: whether models use shared reasoning features across languages or language-specific computations. The findings have implications for improving model robustness and understanding cross-lingual generalization, potentially guiding future architecture designs. The study uses Centered Kernel Alignment (CKA) to select layers with cross-language alignment, then trains both a baseline SAE and GI-SAE at those layers. Functional interchangeability is tested by swapping feature values between languages and measuring KL divergence; notably, higher geometric similarity (CKA/Jaccard) does not consistently imply greater functional interchangeability.

ShardMeter is a lightweight analytical model that predicts end-to-end runtime and identifies bottlenecks for sharded, distributed, and decentralized training of transformer models. It estimates per-GPU and per-island throughput, training cost, total wall-clock time, and exposes diminishing-return regimes and compute/communication-bound transitions. This addresses the impracticality of exhaustive benchmarking and manual tuning in large-scale AI training, enabling practitioners to quickly explore configuration spaces and choose near-optimal deployment plans. It could significantly reduce cost and time in planning distributed training across multiple clusters or geo-distributed environments. ShardMeter considers tightly coupled factors like model size, GPU memory, batch size, bandwidth, and sharding strategy. It also models cost-throughput trade-offs for large-scale decentralized training, providing insights into hyperparameter trade-offs and bottleneck identification.

## [CloudEmu Automates Cloud Emulator Synthesis from Documentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23842) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CloudEmu is a new automated approach that constructs cloud emulators from cloud documentation using neurosymbolic code synthesis, combining LLMs with symbolic abstractions and using the real cloud as an oracle for testing and repair. It outperforms the leading manually-developed tool LocalStack on AWS and GCP services in both coverage and accuracy. This significantly reduces the manual effort and cost of building cloud emulators, which are essential for DevOps testing. It addresses a major pain point in cloud infrastructure management and could enable more scalable and up-to-date emulators, benefiting developers and organizations that rely on local testing. The approach uses neurosymbolic code synthesis, combining LLMs' strengths in documentation understanding and code generation with cloud-specific symbolic abstractions to suppress hallucinations and enforce precision at scale. The real cloud is used as an oracle for automated testing, repair, and alignment, ensuring accuracy.

## [Revelation Control: A Theory for Decision-Sufficient Information Revelation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23860) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces 'Revelation Control', a novel theoretical framework for choosing priced interventions that reveal hidden state only when it affects consequential decisions. It formalizes decision-sufficient revelation and revelation depth, and provides an exact cost-adjusted factorization criterion for when additional information is decision-relevant. This framework addresses a niche but important problem in AI/ML, potentially impacting interpretability and decision-making in learning systems. By separating pure information value from productive reuse, it offers a principled way to decide how much future interaction to purchase, which could lead to more efficient and targeted data collection in adaptive systems. The framework embeds static Bayes refinement into state-dependent continuation value and proves that bounded stop-flip risk alone cannot certify positive expected utility under unrestricted severity. Experiments on Qwen2.5-7B and Mistral-7B-v0.3 show that deeper future-learning probes have positive decision value and productive reuse yields strict equal-compute utility advantages, with evidence for a decision-nonredundant shallow revealability regime in Qwen and scalar decision sufficiency in Mistral.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: In learning systems, states that are equivalent under current information can respond differently to future training and favor different actions. The theory of Revelation Control addresses the problem of deciding which priced interventions to purchase to reveal hidden state, considering both the information value and the productive progress from the intervention itself. This is relevant to fields like interpretability and causality, where understanding when to probe a model's hidden state can improve decision-making.

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that data-driven models, including a structured hybrid model and a neural ODE, can accurately reproduce plant measurements yet produce substantially different economic optima in real-time optimization. The study uses a vinyl acetate monomer benchmark and shows that even noise-free data and good initialization can lead to phantom optima due to training optimizer artifacts. This finding challenges the common assumption that a good predictive fit guarantees reliable economic optimization, which is critical for industrial applications of machine learning in process systems engineering. It underscores the need for decision-oriented validation of data-driven models before deployment in real-time optimization. The paper compares a structured hybrid model (combining mass balances and thermodynamics with a neural-network closure) and a fully data-driven neural ODE. Both models pass standard validation tests but yield many phantom optima on multistart search, while the plant has a single well-conditioned optimum. The training optimizer itself can introduce errors, even with noise-free data and initialization at weights that recover the plant optimum.

A new arXiv paper (2608.23892) introduces A Mathematical Theory of Interpretation (MTI), treating interpretation as observer-relative spectral measurement under an access structure. It defines Rational Entropy to measure residual uncertainty and analyzes confusability as a resource, with a four-condition certificate for sharp, decodable, medium-faithful, and order-independent readout. This work could provide a unified theoretical foundation for constructing interpretation methods in machine learning and cognitive science, with explicit access assumptions and failure modes. It may influence how researchers design and evaluate interpretability tools, potentially leading to more rigorous guarantees. The theory is built on a learning-invariant Hilbert realization, where Rational Entropy measures uncertainty across knowledge, utility, and medium. In the finite-effective regime, the zero set is classified, and pairwise confusability is shown to be equivalent to uniform atomic collapse, with a unique utility maximum selecting one atom even when other zero-cost states remain non-atomic.

## [New Detector Fights Slopsquatting in LLM Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23897) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a two-layer detector to prevent supply chain attacks from hallucinated Python package names in code generated by local LLMs. The detector combines a deterministic PyPI existence check with a Random Forest classifier and is embedded in a LangGraph state machine that retries at escalating temperatures and falls back to a stronger model. This addresses a novel and timely security issue (slopsquatting) in AI code generation, with implications for supply chain security. The proposed detector offers a practical solution that could be adopted by developers and organizations using LLM-based coding assistants, reducing the risk of malicious package installation. The detector uses an import name reconciler to handle cases like 'import cv2' versus 'pip install opencv-python' without security bypass. Across 300 curated prompts, the pipeline produces hallucination-free code on 76% of runs; the primary model exhausts its retry budget on 28.7%, with intra-model retries recovering about a quarter and cross-model fallback recovering a further 16.5%.

Local coding LLMs may hallucinate package names, and if those names are registered by adversaries, users who follow the generated code could install malicious packages. The paper's detector aims to mitigate this risk by checking package existence and using machine learning to identify suspicious names.

## [Luce: Relightable Gaussians for 3D Asset Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23943) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Luce introduces a unified voxelized Gaussian representation that integrates geometry and PBR materials, enabling high-fidelity, relightable 3D asset generation from a single image. It achieves state-of-the-art results on Toys4K, improving FID by 28% over the strongest baseline. This advancement significantly improves the quality and realism of image-to-3D generation, making it easier to create relightable assets for games, films, and VR. It bridges the gap between generative AI and standard rendering pipelines, potentially accelerating 3D content creation workflows. Luce uses dedicated Gaussian primitives for each PBR modality (albedo, metallic-roughness, normals) within a voxelized multimodal Gaussian cloud. A variational autoencoder compresses this into a material-aware latent space, and a rectified-flow transformer generates the latent from a single image, conditioned on multi-layer features from a pretrained image encoder.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Image-to-3D generation aims to create 3D models from a single photograph, which is challenging due to the need to infer geometry and appearance. Physically based rendering (PBR) materials, such as albedo and metallic-roughness, are essential for realistic relighting in standard pipelines. Gaussian splatting is a recent technique that represents 3D scenes with Gaussian primitives, offering efficient rendering. Rectified-flow transformers are a class of generative models that learn to transport noise to data distributions, often used for high-quality image synthesis.

## [LLM Agent Harnesses Converge on Common Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23953) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A source-level multi-case study of three open-source LLM agent harnesses (LangChain's deepagents, Earendil's pi, and DeepSeek's dsh) reveals that despite opposing design philosophies, the mature harnesses have converged toward a common architectural form consisting of five recurring elements. The study also identifies a notable absence of external verifiability across all harnesses, which the authors interpret as a predictive gap. This convergence suggests that the industry is settling on a standard set of architectural patterns for building LLM agents, which could reduce fragmentation and accelerate tooling development. The identified gap in external verifiability highlights a critical area for future innovation, especially for provenance-sensitive applications. The five convergent elements are: a commoditised loop, an append-only replayable session record, model quirks kept as data, progressive disclosure of context, and explicit extension seams. The study also found that one harness reused another's implementation in a seam, indicating diffusion and literal reuse rather than independent invention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the surrounding code that turns a language model into an autonomous agent, handling context building, tool mediation, loop execution, and state persistence. The harness layer is increasingly seen as the binding constraint on agent behavior, and experiments have shown that changing only the harness can significantly improve benchmark scores.

## [NeuronGuard: A New Defense Against Jailbreak and Neuron-Ablation Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23959) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NeuronGuard is a fine-tuning-stage defense that redistributes safety signals across a broader set of neurons, simultaneously hardening LLMs against both jailbreak and neuron-ablation attacks. It provides a formal guarantee of reducing the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound and demonstrates near-zero ASR across three LLMs and six attack strategies. This addresses a critical vulnerability in LLM safety alignment, as both jailbreak and neuron-ablation attacks exploit the concentration of safety-relevant information in a sparse neuron subset. By providing a unified defense with formal guarantees, NeuronGuard could significantly improve the robustness of deployed LLMs against adaptive adversaries, impacting AI safety and security. NeuronGuard dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons via periodically refreshed per-layer linear classifiers, forces refusal behavior under deliberate neuron ablation, and applies KL-divergence regularization for distributional consistency. A randomized gradient projection strategy preserves downstream task utility by resolving conflicts between defense and task objectives, and experiments include multimodal settings and white-box adaptive adversaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Safety alignment in LLMs aims to make models helpful and harmless, but it remains brittle against attacks. Jailbreak attacks use crafted prompts to bypass safety mechanisms, while neuron-level attacks prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment. Both exploit the concentration of safety-relevant information in a sparse subset of neurons, which NeuronGuard addresses by redistributing safety signals.

## [PolyHuman Dataset Reveals LLMs Fail at Cross-Language Code Equivalence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23961) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces PolyHuman, a dataset of human-written programs in C++, Java, and Python, and evaluates LLMs' ability to judge functional equivalence within and across languages. It finds that models like GPT-o4-mini, Claude-Opus-4.7, and Gemini-3-Flash often misclassify non-equivalent code as equivalent, especially on harder problems. This work highlights a critical limitation in current LLMs' semantic understanding of code, challenging the assumption that strong performance on single-language tasks implies deep reasoning. It provides a new benchmark (PolyHuman) that can drive improvements in cross-language code understanding, which is essential for real-world software engineering tasks like code migration and refactoring. The study manually analyzed 81 cases of systematic disagreement where models incorrectly judged functional equivalence, categorizing failures and comparing across models. GPT-o4-mini showed substantial run-to-run instability under identical settings, indicating inconsistent rather than absent capability, and exhibited a model-specific sensitivity to programming language, particularly being more conservative on Python.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown strong performance on code-understanding tasks, but existing evaluations often use single-language settings or synthetic code, which may not reflect true semantic understanding. Functional equivalence means two programs produce identical outputs for all valid inputs, a deeper property than syntactic similarity. PolyHuman is a new dataset of human-written programs in C++, Java, and Python designed to test cross-language equivalence detection.

## [RAGSentinel: Certifiable Defense Against RAG Document Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23965) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RAGSentinel introduces a training-free, label-free defense against adversarial document injection in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). It uses a surrogate encoder to measure query-conditioned hidden-state shifts, removes shared topic directions, and filters poisoned documents as geometric outliers via robust majority consensus. This addresses a critical security vulnerability in RAG systems, where adversarial documents can hijack the context and steer LLMs to incorrect answers. RAGSentinel provides a provably robust defense under certain assumptions, potentially improving trustworthiness of RAG-based applications across industries. The method is training-free and label-free, designed for black-box RAG systems. It proves exact recovery of a poison-free majority-sized context under an honest-majority assumption and a representation-level separation condition, and experiments show low attack success rates across three QA datasets, three LLM families, and multiple attacks, including adaptive ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLM factuality by grounding responses in external documents, but this introduces indirect prompt injection risks where malicious documents can be retrieved and influence outputs. Existing defenses rely on instruction following, parametric knowledge, or text-level consistency, which adaptive attackers can imitate or optimize against. RAGSentinel leverages geometric consensus on hidden-state shifts to identify and filter poisoned documents without additional training.

## [LLM Service Degradation: A Supply Chain Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.23986) argues that LLM providers' cost-saving service degradation during congestion is economically flawed, proposing a supply-chain model that accounts for retries and customer churn. This paper challenges the prevailing cost-accounting in LLM inference, suggesting that degradation can backfire by inflating traffic and destroying customer lifetime value. It offers a formal framework that could reshape how providers price and allocate compute, with potential industry-wide impact. The paper models inference allocation using a newsvendor model, a geometric retry multiplier, and a two-regime transient queue with endogenous arrivals. It shows that under congestion, throttling is not a cost lever but a demand lever, and that reactive throttling can cross an ignition threshold, manufacturing more traffic than it sheds.

**Background**: LLM providers often degrade service during high demand by routing to smaller models, cutting reasoning effort, or truncating context, believing this saves money. However, this paper argues that such degradation can lead to failed answers, which either return as retries (increasing load) or cause customers to churn (losing lifetime value). The newsvendor model is a classic inventory management framework for balancing overage and underage costs, while queueing theory studies waiting lines and service systems.

## [WebMCP-Phalanx: Enforcing Trust Boundaries for Browser-Integrated LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24017) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WebMCP-Phalanx introduces a dual-layer runtime architecture to enforce trust boundaries for browser-integrated LLM agents, addressing spoofing, lifecycle, and prompt injection risks. The architecture uses a browser-native trust anchor with cryptographically protected capability credentials and a Quarantine Agent (Q-LLM) to inspect content before privileged execution. This work addresses a critical security gap in the emerging WebMCP standard, which enables LLM agents to invoke web page tools. By mitigating prompt injection and tool lifecycle attacks, it enhances the safety of browser-based AI agents, impacting the broader ecosystem of web-integrated AI applications. The browser-native ownership mechanism reduces revocation and overwrite attack success from 100% to 0%, and the dual-agent runtime blocks all 80 prompt-injection attempts in tool descriptions, limiting tool-return attacks to 2 out of 80. However, under a white-box adaptive attacker, description-based filtering can be bypassed via malicious tool names, motivating a call-timing gate.

**Background**: The W3C WebMCP proposal allows web pages to register JavaScript functions as tools that AI agents can invoke, but the existing Same-Origin Policy (SOP) does not provide sufficient provenance and lifecycle guarantees for these tools. Prompt injection is a known vulnerability in LLM agents, where malicious content can manipulate agent behavior. WebMCP-Phalanx aims to enforce trust boundaries by separating semantic inspection from privileged tool use.

## [Attnlocate: Runtime Defense Against Prompt Injection in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24022) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Attnlocate, a runtime framework that localizes behavior-guiding instructions in LLM agents by analyzing attention patterns, treating the problem as object detection. It dynamically adjudicates malicious tool-calling attempts based on the authority of the source of detected spans. Prompt injection is a critical security vulnerability in LLM agents, and existing defenses are insufficient against dynamic inducements during reasoning. Attnlocate offers a novel runtime approach that could significantly improve the security and reliability of AI agents in real-world applications. Attnlocate uses a multi-head, multi-layer attention aggregation scheme to construct token-level features, then employs a 1-D U-Net with an anchor-free detection head to identify behavior-guiding spans. Evaluated on ten agent configurations from five LLM families, it achieves a mean IoU of 0.743, an average AUROC of 0.956, and a 0.934 true-positive rate at 0.067 false-positive rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents integrate external resources and use natural language as a unified context channel, making them vulnerable to prompt injection attacks where untrusted data is parsed as instructions. Existing defenses focus on static detection or isolation at input/output levels, but fail to detect dynamic inducements that arise during model reasoning. Attnlocate addresses this by localizing behavior-guiding instructions in real-time using attention patterns.

## [Bayesian Self-Escalation for LLM Agents: Optimal Stopping Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24087) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel intra-generation delegation mechanism for LLM agents, where an agent decides during its own reasoning to transfer control to a stronger model. It formulates this as a Bayesian optimal-stopping problem, deriving a closed-form myopic escalation threshold and proving the optimal policy is a time-varying threshold. This approach could significantly improve the efficiency and reliability of hierarchical LLM systems by enabling dynamic, cost-aware delegation during reasoning, rather than relying on pre-routing or post-hoc verification. It offers theoretical guarantees on optimality and regret, which is rare in LLM agent design and could influence future system architectures. The paper proves exponential separation of the oracle belief at the Chernoff-information rate, a regret bound governed by posterior calibration, and a finite-sample guarantee: with n labeled calibration trajectories, the deployed plug-in policy's regret decays as 1/sqrt(n). A real-model validation on a Qwen2.5-Coder 1.5B->7B code cascade (MBPP, 257 tasks) confirmed two of three pre-registered predictions, including that the escalation frontier dominates post-hoc routing at equal cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agent systems typically decide delegation either before reasoning (via a router) or after a response (via a verifier). This paper explores a third regime: intra-generation delegation, where the agent itself recognizes its own likely failure during reasoning. The approach leverages Bayesian optimal-stopping theory, which is used in statistics and finance to decide when to stop a process for maximum expected reward. Chernoff information is a statistical divergence that measures how quickly a probability distribution distinguishes between two hypotheses, relevant to the theoretical guarantees.

## [PonderPounce Reuses MLLM Context as Robot Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24115) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PonderPounce introduces a novel robot control architecture that reuses a pretrained multimodal large language model's (MLLM) native causal context as episode memory, eliminating the need for a purpose-built memory module. The system comprises Ponder, a System2 MLLM that accumulates observations and generates subgoals, and Pounce, a System1 vision-language-action (VLA) model that executes actions, trained end-to-end. This work challenges the conventional approach of adding explicit memory mechanisms to VLA models, potentially simplifying architectures and improving efficiency in embodied AI. By leveraging the MLLM's inherent contextual capabilities, it could enable more scalable and flexible robot learning, impacting fields like robotics and autonomous systems. The system achieves p50 latencies of 78ms for cognition refresh and 25ms for action-model invocation, supporting 20Hz action playback. On RoboMME, PonderPounce reaches 60.83% with 9B parameters and 50.04% with 0.8B, outperforming baselines like FrameSamp+Modul (44.51%) and current-observation π0.5 (17.93%); with 9x data, it achieves 75.54% versus 57.88%.

## [CBPO: Contrastive Branch Policy Optimization for Tool-Use RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24300) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Contrastive Branch Policy Optimization (CBPO), a novel reinforcement learning algorithm that separates rollout budget allocation from token-level credit assignment. CBPO uses generation entropy to screen branch positions and applies path-level and node-level decay to distribute a fixed budget, achieving state-of-the-art results on ten benchmarks. CBPO addresses a critical challenge in reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) for multi-turn tool use, where sparse outcome rewards obscure which intermediate decisions led to success. By disentangling budget allocation and credit assignment, it provides a practical, annotation-free solution that could improve training efficiency and performance for tool-integrated language agents. CBPO defines an exact-prefix group consisting of a parent trajectory and branches sharing an identical token prefix, and computes a Contrastive Branch Value (CBV) based on reward variation within this group to rescale continuation advantages without changing their sign. When multiple nodes are selected along the same trajectory, CBPO partitions it into non-overlapping credit segments to avoid duplicated gradients on shared tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) trains language models to interact with external tools over multiple turns, but sparse outcome rewards provide no signal for intermediate decisions. Branch sampling induces local comparisons among alternative continuations, yet existing methods often conflate budget allocation and credit assignment. CBPO builds on this by explicitly separating these two problems, using entropy-based screening and decay mechanisms to allocate budget, and CBV for credit assignment.

FARCA introduces a policy optimization framework that aligns fact verification granularity with policy updates and uses reliability-weighted token-level signals to mitigate hallucination in RL-trained LLMs. It decomposes noisy factual credit assignment into credit localization ambiguity and credit reliability ambiguity, and proposes counterfactual evidence attribution to compute reliability weights. This work addresses a critical challenge in RLHF for LLMs: reducing hallucination through fine-grained factual credit assignment. By improving factuality while preserving reasoning capabilities, FARCA could enhance the reliability of LLMs in factual reasoning tasks, benefiting applications that require trustworthy outputs. FARCA transforms factual supervision into localized, reliability-weighted token-level training signals, achieving fine-grained credit localization by aligning verification granularity with policy updates. It uses counterfactual evidence attribution, where the dependence of a factual judgment on key evidence serves as an empirical proxy for verification reliability, modulating factual rewards and local policy advantages.

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a paradigm that applies RL to LLMs using rewards based on objectively verifiable outcomes, such as correct final answers. However, outcome-driven rewards can incentivize hallucination, and existing process-level supervision methods suffer from coarse-grained aggregation and lack of reliability assessment, leading to noisy credit assignment. Credit assignment in RL refers to determining which actions contributed to future rewards, and hallucination in LLMs refers to generating unfaithful or fabricated content.

## [RACER: Region-Aware Backdoor Repair for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24354) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RACER is a novel model-level framework that repairs backdoors in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by detecting and correcting layer-wise representation inconsistencies in trigger-related token regions. It requires only 100 clean samples and no knowledge of the trigger, attack objective, or even whether the model contains a backdoor. This addresses a critical gap in MLLM security, as existing model-level backdoor removal methods are ineffective on MLLMs, and inference-time defenses do not eliminate embedded backdoors. RACER reduces average attack success rate to 1.1% across 36 settings, reaching 0% in 32 settings, while preserving clean-task utility, making it a significant step toward safer deployment of MLLMs. RACER decomposes the fused representation into visual and textual token regions, normalizes their layer-wise inconsistency separately, and recomposes them with modality-aware weights over a deep-layer window. It uses a min-max optimization to synthesize worst-case perturbations and adversarially fine-tune the model, suppressing deep representational directional shifts that backdoors rely on.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate visual and textual inputs, but they can inherit backdoor vulnerabilities from their construction pipelines, where triggers may reside in images, texts, or both. Existing defenses either fail to remove backdoors at the model level or only filter suspicious inputs at inference time. RACER leverages the observation that backdoors induce abnormal layer-to-layer evolution in internal representations, termed the layer-wise inconsistency anomaly, which is modality-dependent and concentrated in trigger-related token regions.

<a id="item-101"></a>
## [E(3)-Equivariant UQ for Tensor Predictions with SPD Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24386) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel framework for E(3)-equivariant uncertainty quantification (UQ) in symmetric rank-2 tensor predictions, ensuring positive-definite covariance matrices via Lie algebra mapping and irreducible decomposition. The method models the full predictive distribution where both mean and covariance preserve rotational symmetry. This work addresses a significant open problem in geometric deep learning by providing a principled way to quantify uncertainty in tensor-valued predictions, which is crucial for applications in physics, materials science, and robotics. The theoretical guarantees on positive-definiteness and exact equivariance are likely to influence future research in equivariant models and uncertainty estimation. The covariance is decomposed into irreducible representations Sym^2(ρ_c) ≅ 2×(l=0) ⊕ 2×(l=2) ⊕ 1×(l=4), and positive-definiteness is strictly ensured by mapping from the flat Lie algebra sym(6) to the curved SPD manifold via matrix exponentiation. The proposed Log-Euclidean Equivariant Scoring Objective (LE-ESO) is a robust surrogate loss based on the Multivariate Laplace distribution, providing robustness to heavy-tailed errors and stable optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: E(3)-equivariant neural networks are deep learning models that incorporate the full Euclidean group symmetry, ensuring outputs transform consistently with physical laws. They use irreducible tensor feature spaces, Clebsch–Gordan tensor products, and spherical harmonics. Symmetric positive definite (SPD) matrices form a differentiable manifold, and covariance matrices naturally live on this manifold. Kelvin-Mandel notation is a way to represent second-order tensors as vectors, which is used in this work to represent the six coordinates of symmetric rank-2 tensors.

## [Predicting Post-SFT Mechanisms from Pre-SFT Parameters for Better Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24482) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a forward-looking localization framework that estimates post-SFT interpretability states using only pre-SFT parameters and the target dataset, addressing the retrospective bias in mechanistic interpretability. It leverages Taylor expansion to model SFT as continuous parameter evolution and designs dual-granularity localization pipelines. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of traditional interpretability—its inability to identify task-critical mechanisms before training—potentially improving parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) for large language models. It could enhance alignment and downstream task performance by providing better SFT guidance, impacting both interpretability and fine-tuning research. The framework uses Taylor expansion to bridge post-tuning mechanistic objectives with pre-SFT dynamic gradients, and implements neuron- and component-level localization pipelines. Experiments show robust performance and temporal scalability across increasing model sizes, though it is a preprint and not yet peer-reviewed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks into human-understandable algorithms, often used to guide fine-tuning. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) updates only a small subset of parameters to adapt large models, reducing computational costs. Traditional interpretability is retrospective, analyzing models after training, which can mislead when applied to pre-SFT models for novel tasks.

## [INCEPT: Invariance-Oriented EEG Foundation Model Outperforms on Broad Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24597) ⭐️ 8.0/10

INCEPT, an invariance-oriented EEG foundation model, was trained on over 11,000 hours of unlabelled clinical EEG and learns representation-level stability to separate stable neural structure from nuisance variability. It ranks first among recent EEG foundation models on 26 of 30 linear-probing metrics and 24 of 30 fine-tuning metrics across ten datasets spanning signal-level, brain-state, and brain-health tasks. This work challenges the reconstruction-centered paradigm in EEG foundation models by introducing invariance learning as a promising principle, potentially enabling more reusable and transferable EEG representations. It could significantly impact EEG analysis and brain-computer interfaces by providing a model that generalizes across diverse downstream tasks without task-specific retraining. INCEPT is evaluated on a broad-spectrum benchmark of ten datasets covering three levels of post-acquisition EEG analysis: signal-level assessment, brain-state decoding, and brain-health evaluation. It also surpasses strong task-specific specialist encoders in diverse downstream settings, and objective ablations show that invariance-oriented pre-training improves transfer and organizes subject-sensitive neural representations beyond reconstruction alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: EEG foundation models are large-scale machine learning architectures that learn universal representations from heterogeneous EEG data using self-supervised methods. Most existing models are reconstruction-centered, assuming that EEG content predictable from local context is necessarily transferable neural information, but this assumption may not hold. Invariance-oriented pre-training, as used in INCEPT, focuses on learning representation-level stability across correlated observations, which is a domain-specific inductive bias that may better capture the underlying neural structure.

## [Simthesizer: Agent-Driven Simulation Framework for LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24650) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Simthesizer introduces an agent-driven simulation framework that uses a composable infrastructure and a unified dynamic graph to keep LLM serving system simulators up-to-date with emerging workloads and mechanisms. It achieves 2.51% average throughput error versus real systems and simulates up to 284.96x and 23.19x faster than LLMServingSim2.0 and Vidur, respectively. This framework addresses the critical gap between rapidly evolving LLM serving systems and slow, human-driven simulator development. By enabling agent-driven updates, it could significantly accelerate research and development in LLM serving, reducing the cost and time of exploring new designs. The framework uses a harnessed coding agent that lowers natural-language feature requests onto a unified dynamic graph abstraction, with simulator-specific guardrails and fidelity validation. Extensions built on Simthesizer follow a vLLM-based real system with 2.51% average throughput error, compared to 6.03% for existing simulators.

## [OPDVR: Combining On-Policy Distillation with Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OPDVR (On-policy Distillation with Verifiable Reward), a method that integrates on-policy distillation (OPD) with reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards (RLVR) using a ReLU gating mechanism, without adding any hyperparameters. Experiments on six reasoning benchmarks show that OPDVR consistently outperforms standard OPD. This work addresses a key challenge in LLM post-training by providing a simple, hyperparameter-free way to combine dense token-level guidance from distillation with task-level correctness from verifiable rewards. It could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of post-training for reasoning tasks, benefiting the broader LLM and RL communities. The method reformulates the implicit reward of sampled-token OPD based on trajectory correctness, then applies a ReLU gating to ensure correct trajectories get non-negative rewards and incorrect ones get non-positive rewards. This modification transforms sampled-token OPD into a proper RLVR method, making it compatible with any policy gradient algorithm like GRPO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that uses automatic, rule-based checkers to provide binary rewards, but it suffers from sparse task-level feedback. On-policy distillation (OPD) provides dense token-level guidance from a teacher model but ignores trajectory correctness, limiting performance to that of the teacher. Existing combinations often require weighted sums or heuristic switching, introducing extra hyperparameters. OPDVR aims to combine the strengths of both without such overhead.

## [FactoMap: Disentangled Representations via Factor-Space Topographic Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24762) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces FactoMap, a novel framework that learns factor-space topographic maps to achieve disentangled representations while preserving non-uniform factor geometry. It addresses the limitations of Euclidean product coordinates by transferring the lattice's periodicity, collapses, and non-uniform extent to the representation. This work challenges the common assumption that generative factors can be represented as Euclidean product coordinates, offering a more flexible framework that can handle factor spaces with wrapping, collapsing, or position-dependent geometry. It could impact representation learning and unsupervised learning, particularly in areas where factors have complex geometries, such as hue and scale in images. The paper defines factor-space structure, combining factor domains, generator-induced identifications, and position-dependent scales to distinguish topologically equivalent spaces with different factor geometries. Experiments show that matching this structure preserves factor continuity and enables disentanglement of underlying factors, even when factors are not geometrically separable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Disentangled representation learning aims to separate distinct factors of variation in data into independent components. Traditional methods often assume these factors can be represented as Euclidean product coordinates, but real-world factors may have non-uniform geometry, such as hue and scale, which grow at different rates and cannot be fixed by rescaling. Topographic maps, which preserve neighborhood relationships, offer a way to represent such structures.

<a id="item-107"></a>
## [Retrieval-Integration Gap: LLMs Retrieve but Fail to Use Risk Disclosures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24842) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies a retrieval-integration gap in LLM-based financial analysis, showing that as context length increases from 2,000 to 128,000 tokens, the influence of a risk disclosure on investment judgments falls to noise even when retrieval remains accurate. The pattern replicates across model families and tasks, and causal memory interventions reveal that compressed summaries and source-text lookup jointly transmit disclosures into judgments. This finding is significant because it challenges the common practice of evaluating AI analysts based on retrieval accuracy alone, which can certify systems that ignore retrieved information in their judgments. It has implications for AI safety and workflow design in financial analysis, where ignoring risk disclosures could lead to flawed investment decisions. The study holds focal-firm information fixed while varying unrelated context from 2,000 to 128,000 tokens, and also removes real disclosures from actual 10-K filings. More capable models postpone but do not eliminate the gap, and workflow architecture determines whether transmission succeeds: chunk-and-summarize pipelines evict relevant information, whereas a targeted, structured restatement adjacent to the decision restores its influence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as AI analysts to process financial disclosures and support investment decisions. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a common technique that retrieves relevant information and places it into the context window, but this paper shows that retrieval does not guarantee integration into the model's judgment. The concept of a retrieval-integration gap is novel and highlights a limitation in current evaluation methods.

A new arXiv paper (2507.11482) critically examines three core tenets of reinforcement learning (RL) and argues that artificial life research offers a more comprehensive framework for understanding agency and adaptation. The authors propose reconceptualizing learning as adaptation rather than optimization, broadening goals beyond scalar reward, and addressing the lack of a formal theory of the agent. This paper challenges foundational assumptions in RL, which underpins many modern AI systems, and suggests an alternative perspective grounded in artificial life. If adopted, it could shift research directions toward more biologically faithful models of adaptive agents, impacting fields like AI safety, open-endedness, and autonomous systems. The paper specifically critiques the MDP framework, policy optimization, and scalar reward maximization, and draws on open-ended novelty search and thermodynamic theories of origin-of-life and agency. It aims to provide a more biologically faithful and formally grounded account of adaptive agents.

## [ReflCtrl: Efficient LLM Reflection Control via Representation Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13979) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ReflCtrl identifies a reflection direction in the latent space of LLMs and applies stepwise steering to control self-reflection, reducing reasoning tokens by up to 43.2% while preserving accuracy. This work addresses the high inference cost of self-reflection in large reasoning models, offering a fine-grained control method that improves efficiency without sacrificing performance. It provides new insights into how reflection is regulated by internal uncertainty, which could influence future model design and deployment. The method intervenes only at the start of each reasoning step, unlike conventional token-level steering, and outperforms it at matched token budgets. Experiments on math and general reasoning benchmarks show that reflection is often redundant, especially in stronger models.

Self-reflection, the ability to review and revise prior reasoning steps, is key to the performance of large reasoning models but incurs significant inference cost. ReflCtrl leverages these concepts to control reflection frequency efficiently.

<a id="item-114"></a>
## [Explaining LLM Behavioral Shifts Requires New Comparative XAI Standards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02304) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This position paper argues that current explainability methods fail to explain behavioral shifts in large language models (LLMs) after interventions like fine-tuning or scaling, and proposes a new paradigm called Comparative XAI (XAI_Δ) with desiderata including comparability, validity, actionability, and monitoring. This matters because regulatory frameworks like the EU AI Act and Chinese AI regulations require documenting causal chains for substantial system modifications, and current explainability methods are inadequate for this purpose. The proposed standards could improve AI governance and auditing practices. The paper introduces XAI_Δ as a novel paradigm that treats the shift itself as the primary object of explanation, and provides preliminary evidence through illustrative experiments compiled into a transition report for governance and incident documentation. It highlights that traditional XAI methods treat models as static objects, while simple comparisons across checkpoints are insufficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models often undergo interventions such as fine-tuning or reinforcement learning with human feedback, which can cause unexpected behavioral shifts. Traditional explainable AI (XAI) methods typically analyze a single model checkpoint, failing to capture how and why behavior changes between versions. This gap poses governance risks, as regulations increasingly demand causal documentation for model updates.

<a id="item-115"></a>
## [EngiAI: Capability-Based Evaluation Framework for Tool-Connected Engineering Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19743) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces EngiAI, a capability-based evaluation framework for tool-connected engineering agents, and evaluates four LLM backends on the EngiBench Beams2D and Photonics2D problems using a LangGraph reference implementation. The results show significant performance differences across backends, with proprietary models completing 96-97% of workflow tasks on Beams2D compared to 55-78% for open-source models. This framework addresses a critical gap in comparing and diagnosing tool-connected engineering agents, which are proliferating but lack standardized evaluation. By providing separate scores for distinct skills, it enables more meaningful comparisons and helps identify failure mechanisms, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in AI-driven engineering design. The framework evaluates four capabilities: workflow execution, retrieval-assisted parameter selection, HPC orchestration, and training-code authoring. On Photonics2D, workflows requiring tool-based decision-making achieve only 20-53% completion, and Gemini-3-Flash completes every tested HPC pipeline while GPT-5-mini completes 50-70%. In the open-synthesis tier, both proprietary models select conditional variational autoencoders instead of the reference cGAN, with Gemini-3-Flash achieving lower maximum mean discrepancy than the reference in seven of ten Beams2D trials.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-connected LLM agents are AI systems that use large language models to interact with external tools and APIs to perform complex tasks, such as engineering design. Evaluating these agents is challenging because tasks, tools, and success criteria vary widely, making it hard to compare different implementations. EngiAI is a multi-agent framework and benchmark suite that provides a web-based interface and modular architecture for coordinating engineering tools through natural language. LangGraph is a low-level orchestration framework for building stateful, multi-actor applications with LLMs, providing durable execution and streaming capabilities.

<a id="item-116"></a>
## [Self-Evolving AI Agent Designs Interpretable Whitebox Fluid Controllers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08405) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A self-evolving scientific agent, driven by large language models and iterative code generation, autonomously designed and refined a whitebox controller for an underactuated two-joint dogfish swimmer to achieve target reaching in an unsteady flow. The controller, starting from a target-blind seed, achieved target capture across a full generalization test matrix without retraining or retuning. This work demonstrates that LLM-driven agents can automate interpretable control design in complex physical systems, potentially transforming fields like robotics and control theory. It offers a traceable alternative to black-box deep reinforcement learning, which is often opaque and requires extensive retraining. The controller was tested on a highly nonlinear fluid-structure interaction problem, specifically an underactuated swimmer in an unsteady four-cylinder wake. The emergent control architecture included travelling-wave propulsion, body-frame bearing guidance, phase-selective steering, corrective burst, and adaptive relief, all derived from an auditable evolution log.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Fluid-structure interaction (FSI) problems involve the coupling between fluid flow and structural deformation, common in biofluidics and aeroelasticity. Underactuated systems have fewer control inputs than degrees of freedom, requiring sophisticated control strategies. Traditional deep reinforcement learning can optimize policies but often lacks interpretability, whereas this agent maintains a transparent chain of reasoning.

<a id="item-117"></a>
## [SuperLocalMemory 4.0: Governed Memory OS for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08253) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SuperLocalMemory 4.0 introduces a governed, local-first memory operating system for AI agents, featuring multi-channel retrieval via reciprocal-rank fusion, bi-temporal recall, and a hash-chained audit trail. The paper reports a negative result: ten implemented mechanisms were reachable on a live call path but ineffective at their final connection, highlighting the distinction between implemented, reachable, and effective. This work addresses critical challenges in AI agent memory management and reliability, proposing a governance layer and fault-injection testing. The negative result underscores the importance of validating mechanisms with independent oracles, which could influence future research and development in agent memory systems. The reliability spine includes generation-fenced admission, verifiable memory transactions, and hash-checkable completion manifests. Eleven fault-injection scenarios, each repeated 200 times, upheld 2,199 of 2,200 scoped component properties. The paper also corrects a previous overhead figure: the governed write takes 11.0 ms, with the envelope accounting for 70.6%, but the generation fence costs only 1.9 microseconds and the obligation ledger 42 microseconds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents often rely on memory systems to store and retrieve information, but these systems face challenges in reliability and governance. Reciprocal-rank fusion (RRF) is a technique that combines multiple ranked lists into a single ranking, while bi-temporal modeling tracks data along two timelines (valid time and transaction time). A hash-chained audit trail uses cryptographic hashes to ensure tamper-evident logging, similar to blockchain.

<a id="item-118"></a>
## [Model Discovery Agent: LLM-Assisted Bayesian Experiment Design for Mechanistic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Model Discovery Agent (MDA), which combines a novel nested sequential Monte Carlo algorithm (SMC^3) with LLM-proposed models and Bayesian experiment design to efficiently learn mechanistic world models. MDA achieves state-of-the-art performance on three benchmarks and introduces a new harder benchmark, HHbench. This work addresses a significant challenge in scientific discovery: efficiently identifying mechanistic models from data. By integrating LLMs with Bayesian experiment design, it could accelerate AI-driven science and enable more data-efficient experimentation in fields like physics, chemistry, and neuroscience. The SMC^3 algorithm uses three levels of nested sequential Monte Carlo over models, parameters, and latents. The LLM proposes new models when the current hypothesis space is insufficient, and the experiment designer maximizes Value of Information. MDA sets new SOTA on DPbench, CHEMbench, and Boxing, and performs well on the new stochastic single-neuron electrophysiology benchmark HHbench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic or causal world models are essential for explaining phenomena and answering interventional questions. Identifying such models typically requires experiments because passive data leaves mechanisms unidentified. Bayesian experimental design provides a framework for choosing experiments to maximize information gain, and sequential Monte Carlo methods are used for sampling from complex distributions. The M-open Bayesian inference perspective acknowledges that the true model may not be in the candidate set, motivating the use of LLMs to propose new models.
