<a id="item-119"></a>
## [Toby Ord's Math Shows Intelligence Explosions Harder Than Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14426) ⭐️ 8.0/10

In his paper 'The Dynamics of Intelligence Explosions', Toby Ord mathematically analyzes intelligence explosions, showing that singular growth (toward a vertical asymptote) is harder to achieve than recent economics-inspired models suggest. He also identifies a neglected class of growth rates that are faster than exponential but do not lead to a vertical asymptote, and highlights generation time as a pivotal parameter. This work challenges existing assumptions in AI safety and future-of-AI research, potentially reshaping how we assess the risk of an intelligence explosion. By clarifying the mathematical conditions required for such an event, it helps researchers and policymakers better understand the feasibility and urgency of AI risks. Ord shows that singular growth requires generation time to rapidly approach zero, which is a stringent condition. He introduces a new class of growth rates that are super-exponential but lack a vertical asymptote, which may have been overlooked in prior modeling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: An intelligence explosion refers to a hypothetical scenario where AI systems used in AI research and development create a feedback loop, leading to rapidly escalating capabilities. In mathematics, a vertical asymptote represents a point where a function grows without bound, which in this context corresponds to an infinite intelligence level in finite time. Generation time is the time it takes to complete one cycle of the feedback loop, and Ord's analysis shows it plays a crucial role in determining the dynamics.

## [CausalCache Boosts Long-Horizon GUI Agents via Selective Screenshot Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22577) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CausalCache introduces a budgeted fidelity restoration method that selectively reactivates historical screenshots for long-horizon GUI agents, improving success rates by about 13 percentage points on OSWorld-Verified compared to summary-only memory. The method uses a history-gated key/value adapter and a budget-aware selector trained with matched-budget interventions. This work addresses a practical limitation in GUI agents where only a few historical screenshots fit in active context, showing that selecting which past events regain pixels is more effective than spending a fixed visual budget on recency. It has potential impact on GUI automation and agent design, with zero-shot generalization to mobile tasks. Under the official 15-step limit, CausalCache and Recent-4 are statistically indistinguishable, but in a 30-step diagnostic, CausalCache achieves 46.7% success versus 42.4% (+4.3 points). Zero-shot on 117 MobileWorld tasks, CausalCache improves over Recent-4 from 30.2% to 36.8%, with gains concentrated on cross-app memory-candidate splits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon GUI agents can retain complete action histories as compact text, but only a few historical screenshots fit in active context due to token limits. CausalCache formulates this as budgeted fidelity restoration, where every event remains summarized while a fixed budget determines which events regain their archived screenshots. The method scores the complete history and swaps in an older event only when its predicted utility exceeds that of a recent event.

## [MobilePA-Bench: New Benchmark for Mobile Planning Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23035) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MobilePA-Bench is a new interactive, stateful, and tool-centric benchmark for evaluating mobile planning agents, covering 13 functional domains and 212 realistic mobile tools. It goes beyond static function matching by executing agent actions in a mutable sandbox and verifying both action traces and resulting states. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating mobile LLM agents, which existing GUI-centric and static function-calling benchmarks fail to cover. It provides a realistic testbed for developing dependable mobile agents, potentially accelerating progress in on-device AI assistants and agentic reinforcement learning. The benchmark evaluates agents along three advanced dimensions: sub-agent collaboration, memory usage, and skill usage. Experiments show that current frontier LLMs remain unreliable in mobile settings, with performance dropping sharply under strict tool ordering, permission limits, and unexpected runtime errors.

Apodex 1.1 introduces a framework that scales agentic intelligence by enhancing executable environments and training agents to coordinate long-horizon tasks. It achieves leading performance across professional work, finance, scientific research, mathematics, coding, and search, with a 35B-parameter Mini version retaining strong capability in a locally deployable form. This work addresses key challenges in long-horizon task decomposition, delegation, and verifiable delivery, which are critical for real-world AI applications. By combining environment scaling with agentic coordination scaling, Apodex 1.1 demonstrates that smaller models can achieve frontier-level performance, potentially reducing the computational cost of advanced agentic systems. Apodex 1.1 uses a shared execution harness and AgentOS to maintain task state and provenance across tools and agents, and training turns environment trajectories and coordination traces into reliable behavior. The 35B-parameter Mini variant is designed for local deployment, offering a practical option for organizations with limited resources.

## [MediSkill-Evo: Self-Evolving Clinical Agents with Process Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23397) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MediSkill-Evo introduces a process-constrained self-evolution method for clinical agents, enabling them to improve evidence-grounded interactions without fine-tuning the backbone model. It updates four typed knowledge banks (clinical, process, symbolic, visual) under type-specific validation and scope rules, and uses a Process-Constrained Preference Harness to ground candidates in evidence and prioritize safer decisions. This work addresses a critical gap in clinical AI: converting experience into reusable process knowledge with explicit provenance and authority. By improving diagnosis accuracy and reducing critical failures on benchmarks like MIMIC-IV, it could enhance the safety and reliability of AI-assisted healthcare, potentially impacting clinical decision support systems. On Qwen FullChain, MediSkill-Evo improves diagnosis accuracy by 7.81% and treatment-intent coverage by 70.67% over the best-performing prior agent, while reducing critical failures by 43.04%. It also shows gains on stress-process composite (7.77%) and required-action completion (12.41%), and on multimodal NEJM diagnosis, it improves accuracy by 2.56% and core score by 18.96% with optional MedSAM localization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive clinical agents operate under partial observability, meaning they must gather evidence and make decisions without full information. Traditional agents often fail to convert experience into reusable process knowledge with explicit provenance. MediSkill-Evo addresses this by self-evolving governed process knowledge without fine-tuning the backbone, using four typed knowledge banks and a Process-Constrained Preference Harness. The method is evaluated on MIMIC-IV-derived FullChain encounters, stress conditions, and multimodal NEJM cases.

## [RL-Based Eulerian Headway Control Reduces Highway Congestion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02520) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a reinforcement learning-optimized Eulerian headway control system for connected automated vehicles (CAVs) that regulates aggregate density near bottlenecks via headway commands instead of speed commands. In large-scale simulations, both time-headway and distance-headway variants outperform baselines, improving traffic flow by up to 10.6% over human traffic and 6.7% over traditional variable speed limits (VSL). This work addresses limitations of both Eulerian VSL (infrequent updates, low compliance) and Lagrangian strategies (dependence on latent driver intentions), offering a more practical and scalable approach to highway congestion mitigation. It could accelerate the deployment of CAV-based traffic management systems and improve traffic efficiency and safety in real-world highway networks. The system leverages adaptive cruise control (ACC) for reactivity and compliance, and regulates aggregate density near bottlenecks without relying on latent driver intentions. The paper also introduces a novel boundary-aware speed metric to address a recognized flaw in simulation studies with dynamic vehicle entry and exit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Highway congestion control traditionally uses Eulerian variable speed limits (VSL) via roadside signs, but these suffer from infrequent updates and limited driver compliance. Lagrangian strategies directly control individual vehicles, offering high reactivity and compliance, but in multi-lane settings they depend on drivers' latent lane-change intentions, making robust decisions difficult. This paper proposes an Eulerian control system optimized through reinforcement learning that uses headway commands via ACC to regulate aggregate density near bottlenecks, combining the benefits of both approaches.

## [CytoNet: A Foundation Model for the Human Cerebral Cortex at Cellular Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01870) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CytoNet is a foundation model trained on 1 million unlabeled microscopic image patches from over 4,000 histological sections of nine postmortem brains, using co-localization in the cortical sheet for self-supervision. It supports multiple downstream tasks including area classification, laminar segmentation, and quantification of microarchitectural variation. This is a significant advance in neuroimaging and AI, providing a unified framework for scalable analysis of cortical microarchitecture. It could enable large-scale studies linking cellular architecture to brain function and organization, impacting neuroscience research and potentially clinical applications. The model was evaluated on over 2,000 sections from five additional brains excluded from pretraining, demonstrating generalizability. Functional parcellation analyses provided parcellation-dependent evidence for links between cytoarchitecture and macroscale functional organization.

<a id="item-128"></a>
## [CAIT Benchmark Reveals Language Bias in Open-Source MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07737) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CAIT, a benchmark of 400 counter-intuitive visual scenes, and evaluates humans, proprietary models (Claude, Gemini), and 14 open-source MLLMs. It finds that open-source models perform at chance level due to a strong language prior, while humans and proprietary models achieve high accuracy. This work highlights a critical limitation in open-source MLLMs: they often ignore visual evidence in favor of statistical language patterns. The CAIT benchmark provides a resource for future improvements, potentially influencing the development of more visually grounded multimodal models. The benchmark uses high-fidelity synthetic scenes like 'a rabbit is chasing a tiger' to test common-sense contradiction. Chain-of-Thought reasoning improves accuracy but slows responses and introduces a new failure mode where models overthink and reject valid visual content. Targeted fine-tuning and structured prompting can mitigate the language bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) combine visual and textual inputs to perform tasks like image captioning and visual question answering. However, they often rely on language priors—statistical patterns from training data—rather than fully grounding their reasoning in visual evidence. This paper addresses the gap in evaluating MLLMs on scenes that contradict everyday common sense, which is crucial for understanding their true visual understanding capabilities.

<a id="item-129"></a>
## [ImgCoder and SciGenBench: Advancing Scientific Image Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17027) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces ImgCoder, a logic-driven framework that follows an 'understand-plan-code' workflow for programmatic scientific image synthesis, and SciGenBench, a benchmark that evaluates generated images based on information utility and logical validity. The study systematically analyzes pixel-based and programmatic generation paradigms, revealing failure modes and an expressiveness-precision trade-off. This work addresses the critical visual-logic divergence in text-to-image models, where outputs are visually plausible but scientifically incorrect, limiting their use in multimodal scientific reasoning. By introducing a rigorous benchmark and a programmatic framework, it paves the way for high-fidelity scientific image synthesis, potentially enabling scaling of multimodal reasoning capabilities analogous to text domain. ImgCoder decouples planning from implementation to mitigate chaotic layouts in code generation, and SciGenBench uses atomic, visually grounded quizzes and multi-dimensional metrics to assess information utility and logical validity. The evaluation shows that fine-tuning Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) on rigorously verified synthetic scientific images yields consistent reasoning gains, with potential scaling trends.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image (T2I) models have advanced significantly, but they often generate images that are visually appealing yet scientifically inaccurate, a problem known as visual-logic divergence. This limits their utility in scientific reasoning tasks that require precise visual information. The paper proposes a programmatic synthesis approach (ImgCoder) that generates executable code for deterministic rendering, and a benchmark (SciGenBench) to evaluate scientific correctness. This is part of a broader trend to improve multimodal reasoning by using synthetic data.

## [Anytime Pretraining Achieves Minimax Rates via Weight Averaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03702) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces anytime learning-rate schedules for LLM pretraining that do not require knowing the training horizon in advance. The authors prove the existence of such schedules for overparameterized linear regression and empirically validate them on 150M and 300M parameter models, showing comparable final loss to cosine decay. This work addresses a critical open problem in continual and open-ended LLM training, where compute budgets are often unknown. By providing a practical, horizon-free alternative to cosine schedules, it enables more flexible and efficient training pipelines, potentially reducing the need for extensive hyperparameter tuning. The anytime schedules polynomially decay with time, with the decay rate determined by source and capacity conditions. Empirically, the study compares constant and 1/sqrt(t) schedules with weight averaging against a well-tuned cosine schedule, training up to 32x Chinchilla scale, and finds comparable performance across the full training range.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are typically trained with cosine learning rate schedules that require a fixed compute budget. In continual or open-ended settings, the total training horizon is unknown, making such schedules suboptimal. Weight averaging, also known as model merging, combines multiple checkpoints to improve performance and is central to the proposed anytime schedules.

## [Reinforcement Learning Enables End-to-End Tokenization Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13940) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a reinforcement learning approach to learn token boundaries in LLMs, using score function estimates with time discounting to reduce variance. The method outperforms prior straight-through estimates at the 100 million parameter scale. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in LLM training by making tokenization end-to-end learnable, potentially improving model performance and efficiency. It could influence future architecture designs and training pipelines. The method employs score function estimates, which directly optimize discrete token boundaries, and uses time discounting from reinforcement learning to reduce variance. Theoretical guarantees are tighter than prior straight-through estimates, and experiments at 100M parameter scale show qualitative and quantitative improvements.

**Background**: Tokenization is a hardcoded compression step in LLM training, converting raw text into tokens. Prior work attempted to learn token boundaries using straight-through estimates, which treat discrete boundaries as continuous, but this paper shows that score function estimates, a technique from reinforcement learning, offer tighter theoretical guarantees and better performance.

## [VLANeXt: A Practical Recipe for Building Strong VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18532) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces VLANeXt, a new Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model that systematically dissects design choices along three dimensions and distills 12 key findings into a practical recipe. VLANeXt outperforms state-of-the-art methods on the LIBERO and LIBERO-plus benchmarks and shows strong real-world performance, with a unified codebase released. This work addresses the fragmentation in the VLA field by providing a unified framework and evaluation setup, helping researchers identify which design choices truly matter. The practical recipe and open-source codebase can accelerate progress in embodied AI and robotics by enabling reproducible comparisons and faster development of new VLA variants. The study starts from a simple VLA baseline similar to RT-2, the origin of VLA, and examines design choices in foundational components, perception essentials, and action modeling perspectives. The resulting VLANeXt model is evaluated on LIBERO and LIBERO-plus benchmarks, and the codebase is available at https://github.com/DravenALG/VLANeXt.

They were pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in July 2023, which unified perception, reasoning, and control. The VLA field is currently fragmented due to inconsistent training protocols and evaluation settings, making it hard to compare design choices.

## [ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10068) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ADVERSA is an automated red-teaming framework that measures LLM guardrail degradation as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than binary jailbreak events. It uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model and a triple-judge consensus architecture to assess judge reliability as a first-class outcome. This addresses a critical gap in adversarial testing by capturing how safety properties evolve under sustained multi-turn interaction, which single-turn binary evaluations miss. The findings on judge reliability and attacker drift have significant implications for AI safety evaluation methodology and the development of more robust guardrails. In a controlled experiment across Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, and GPT-5.2, ADVERSA observed a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, indicating successful jailbreaks concentrated in early rounds. The framework documents inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift, and attacker refusals as a previously underreported confound.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial testing of LLMs typically evaluates single prompts and reports binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained interaction. ADVERSA introduces continuous compliance trajectories and a structured 5-point rubric to treat partial compliance as a distinct state. The framework also uses a triple-judge consensus to measure judge reliability, addressing the issue that single-judge evaluation in adversarial settings may be structurally unreliable.

## [Omanic: A New Benchmark for Step-Wise Multi-Hop Reasoning Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16654) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Omanic is a newly introduced open-domain 4-hop QA benchmark with step-level annotations, containing 10,296 machine-generated training examples (OmanicSynth) and 967 expert-reviewed human-annotated evaluation examples (OmanicBench). It decomposes each question into single-hop sub-questions, intermediate answers, and structured graph topologies to diagnose reasoning failures in LLMs. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by moving beyond final-answer accuracy to step-wise diagnosis, helping researchers identify where reasoning breaks down. It could significantly impact LLM evaluation and reasoning research, as fine-tuning on OmanicSynth transfers to six reasoning and mathematics benchmarks with a 7.41-point average gain. Experiments with proprietary and open-source LLMs show that Omanic is challenging, and step-wise analysis reveals a later-hop bottleneck, factual knowledge floor, and error propagation along reasoning chains. The data and code are publicly released on Hugging Face and GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-hop QA benchmarks evaluate a model's ability to integrate multiple pieces of evidence for complex reasoning, but many lack step-level annotations, making it hard to diagnose intermediate failures. Step-level evaluation, such as process reward models, provides fine-grained feedback on reasoning chains, which is crucial for improving LLM reasoning. Omanic fills this gap by offering a benchmark specifically designed for step-wise diagnosis.

## [PAuth: Task-Scoped Authorization for AI Agents via Natural Language Slices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17170) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces PAuth, a novel authorization mechanism that grants AI agents permissions based on the specific natural-language task, rather than broad operator-level scopes like OAuth. It uses NL slices and signed envelopes to ensure each operation matches the user's intent, blocking adversarial calls. This addresses a critical security gap in the emerging agentic web, where OAuth's operator-scoped authorization overprivileges AI agents, leading to potential misuse via prompt injection or malware. PAuth could set a new standard for secure agent interactions, enhancing both security and usability. PAuth is evaluated on AuthBench, a benchmark built on AgentDojo and cross-validated on OpenClaw, covering five service suites with 100 benign tasks and 634 adversarial calls, achieving zero false positives and negatives. The mechanism uses NL slices as symbolic specifications and signed envelopes to bind concrete values to symbolic provenance, enabling implicit authorization of intermediate results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: OAuth 2.0 is a widely used authorization framework that grants third-party apps access to resources on behalf of users via scopes, which are typically operator-level permissions (e.g., 'transfer' or 'read'). However, for AI agents executing natural-language tasks, such coarse-grained scopes are insufficient because operands (e.g., 'transfer $100 to Bob') are combinatorial and often derived at runtime. PAuth proposes a shift from operator-scoped to task-scoped authorization, where the task itself defines the exact operations allowed.

## [SIRA: Compressing Multi-Round Search into a Single Retrieval Action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06647) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Superintelligent Retrieval Agent (SIRA), which compresses multi-round exploratory search into a single corpus-discriminative retrieval action. SIRA uses an LLM to enrich documents offline, predict missing query vocabulary, and filter terms using corpus statistics, culminating in a single weighted BM25 call. This approach addresses a significant inefficiency in current retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems by reducing latency and improving recall. It outperforms dense retrievers, learned sparse retrievers, and LLM search-agent baselines on BEIR benchmarks without requiring relevance labels or fine-tuning, potentially making agentic retrieval more efficient and scalable. SIRA introduces a new benchmark, BrowseComp-Wikipedia, with 232 queries over a 25.5M-document Wikipedia index. Even without index-time enrichment, SIRA achieves 9.70% Recall@1, 15.27% Recall@10, and 36.14% Recall@100, outperforming multi-round Perplexity agents at every budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented agents typically treat retrieval as a black box, issuing exploratory queries and reformulating until evidence emerges, which is inefficient. SIRA instead aims to mimic an expert's approach by using strong priors about terminology and likely evidence, compressing the search into a single action. The method leverages corpus statistics to filter terms and uses BM25, a classic sparse retrieval algorithm, for the final retrieval step.

## [Tournament-GRPO: Group-Wise Tournament Rewards Boost RL for Long-Form Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26958) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Tournament-GRPO introduces a group-wise tournament-based reward framework that converts rubric-guided LLM judgments into relative rewards through repeated multi-round tournaments among same-query rollouts. Experiments on Deep Research Bench show it outperforms existing reward-design baselines, achieving a 4.52-point overall-score improvement over the strongest baseline. This work addresses a key challenge in reinforcement learning for open-ended long-form generation, where reliable reference answers and automatic metrics are often unavailable. By providing a more effective reward signal, it could improve LLM alignment and reasoning capabilities, influencing future reward design and RL training methodologies. Tournament-GRPO compares candidates within groups, accumulates tournament outcomes, and normalizes them into group-wise rewards for GRPO training. The paper also analyzes the effectiveness-efficiency trade-off of tournament rewards and shows that tournament design affects training dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm designed for LLM reasoning, which uses group-based normalization to compute advantages. In open-ended long-form generation, traditional pointwise LLM-as-a-judge scoring suffers from calibration issues, weak discrimination, and saturation. Tournament-based relative rewards offer an alternative by comparing responses within groups, potentially providing more robust training signals.

## [SGSD: Skill-Conditioned Gated Self-Distillation Boosts LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28791) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Skill-Conditioned Gated Self-Distillation (SGSD), a novel method that uses an experience-derived skill bank as privileged information for on-policy self-distillation. SGSD constructs a multi-teacher pool from skill-mistake pairs and validates each teacher's polarity with a verifier, applying a gated objective to distill informative disagreements while suppressing uncertain signals. This work challenges the assumption that privileged information must be trusted, showing that a skill bank can serve as a weaker but effective source of supervision. It offers a way to improve LLM reasoning without relying on reference answers, potentially reducing annotation costs and enhancing training efficiency. SGSD consistently outperforms GRPO and remains competitive with answer-conditioned OPSD on multiple mathematical reasoning benchmarks. For example, on Qwen3-1.7B, SGSD improves over GRPO by 6.2% and over OPSD by 1.7% on average across AIME24, AIME25, and HMMT25.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (SD) improves LLM reasoning by using teacher-side privileged information to convert sparse verifier outcomes into dense token-level supervision. Traditional methods assume trusted privileged information like reference answers, but SGSD explores using a skill bank that may contain irrelevant or misleading skills. The gated objective validates teacher hypotheses, ensuring only informative supervision is distilled.

## [Mamba-2 State Sink Causal Localization Is Non-Unique](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00930) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that in Mamba-2, the causal set of heads responsible for the state sink is not unique, breaking the link between probe top-activations and actual causal effect. The study shows that a near-disjoint set of top-activation heads can match or exceed the ablation effect of the specialists. This finding challenges a core assumption in mechanistic interpretability that top-activating units correspond to the circuit executing a computation. It implies that interpretability results based on probes may not uniquely identify causal circuits, affecting how we validate AI safety and model understanding. The paper identifies three ways the label-to-locus link breaks in Mamba-2: non-unique causal sets, dual heads that respond alike but lack causal profile, and dissociation by intervention surface. Perturbing the input-dependent Delta changes loss by at most 0.16 nats, while suppressing the same heads' output changes it by 1.5 to 7.3 nats.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by identifying circuits—sets of units that implement specific computations. Probes are often used to find units that activate strongly on certain inputs, and these are assumed to be causally responsible. Mamba is a state space model (SSM) architecture that uses selective state spaces, and the state sink is an analogue of the attention sink in Transformers, where certain gates fire on boundary tokens.

## [SALIMORY: A New Framework for Cognitive Memory in Conversational Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04120) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SALIMORY introduces a framework that trains a single language model to manage cognitive memory for conversational agents, using hierarchical stage-wise process rewards and reward-decomposed contrastive refinement. It achieves a one-third reduction in memory-attributed failures, over 10% improvement in end-to-end accuracy, and more than doubles the Good Personalization rate. This addresses a critical bottleneck in lifelong conversational agents: efficient memory management without degrading reasoning. By providing isolated supervision for distinct memory operations, it offers a scalable approach that could significantly enhance personalization and reliability in AI companions and assistants. The framework decomposes memory into user facts, preferences, and working memory, and uses a stage-wise process reward system to supervise selective filtering, consolidation, and cue-driven recall. Reward-decomposed contrastive refinement within GRPO rollouts constructs role-specific preference pairs to amplify memory-management signals, addressing the credit-assignment problem.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational agents that act as lifelong companions need to remember past interactions, but simply expanding context windows with raw retrieval degrades reasoning. Standard reinforcement learning for memory agents suffers from a credit-assignment bottleneck in multi-stage pipelines. SALIMORY tackles this by training a single model with hierarchical rewards, providing a more efficient and effective memory management solution.

## [Single Neuron Edit Can Fix LLM Repetition Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13705) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that targeted edits to a few internal neurons can directly reduce repetition loops in LLMs, with a single sign-inverted neuron sufficient to fix the issue in Gemma 4 E2B. Across four Gemma models, detected loops dropped from 46/384 to 12/384 on held-out prompts and seeds. This work offers a training-free, targeted intervention for a common and reproducible failure mode in LLMs, potentially improving model reliability without degrading general performance. It also contributes to interpretability research by linking specific neurons to specific failure behaviors. The method combines per-layer ablation with per-neuron or routed-expert attribution to locate targets, then evaluates weight edits over complete generations. The edits show no statistically detectable regressions on general-purpose benchmarks, but rankings vary across examples, and remaining failures in larger models shift toward 'doom looping'.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Repetition loops are a known failure mode in large language models where the model gets stuck repeating the same tokens or phrases, often on long enumeration tasks. Ablation studies involve removing components to assess their contribution, and routed-expert attribution is used in mixture-of-experts models to trace outputs back to specific experts. Gemma 4 E2B is a small dense model from Google's Gemma 4 family, designed for efficient inference.

## [TW-LegalBench: New Benchmark for Taiwanese Legal LLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18699) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TW-LegalBench is a new benchmark for evaluating large language models on Taiwanese legal reasoning, comprising over 30,000 instances across three task types: 16,000+ multiple-choice questions, 117 open-ended essay questions, and 14,000+ legal judgment prediction instances. The benchmark uses official exam questions and rubrics, and evaluates 13 LLMs, revealing that top models pass the lawyer qualification threshold but fall short for judges and prosecutors. This benchmark fills a critical gap in legal NLP evaluation, which has largely focused on English common-law or Simplified Chinese civil-law systems, by providing a comprehensive resource for Taiwan's Traditional Chinese civil-law jurisdiction. It enables researchers to assess LLMs' legal reasoning in a new linguistic and legal context, potentially driving improvements in legal AI applications for Taiwan and similar jurisdictions. The benchmark includes 16,000+ MCQs from five years of official exams across 18 professional domains, 117 OEQs with official scoring rubrics, and 14,000+ LJP instances covering hundreds of crime categories. Evaluation uses accuracy for MCQs, a decomposed LLM-as-Judge framework for OEQs, and metrics for sentencing accuracy and statute citation for LJP; results show models struggle to cite exact legal articles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Legal benchmarks for LLMs have predominantly focused on English common-law systems (e.g., LegalBench) or Simplified Chinese civil-law systems, leaving Traditional Chinese jurisdictions like Taiwan underrepresented. TW-LegalBench leverages Taiwan's publicly available official legal corpora, including exam questions and court judgments, to create a benchmark that tests rule recall, legal argumentation, and real-world verdict prediction. ## [RARM: Confidence-Gated Progress Rewards for Robot RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22027) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RARM is a lightweight visual comparator that converts a single successful demonstration into a dense, progress-aware reward for reinforcement learning in robot manipulation, eliminating the need for task-specific reward engineering. It is trained once on general-purpose videos with a contrastive temporal objective and requires no robot-specific data or task-specific reward labels. This approach addresses a key bottleneck in RL for robot manipulation by reducing the engineering effort required for reward design, especially in long-horizon tasks. It achieves the best overall success rates across 9 simulated and 4 real-world tasks, with significant gains on long-horizon tasks like cloth folding, potentially accelerating the deployment of RL in real-world robotics. RARM uses a confidence-gated mechanism to reward only confident forward progress, suppressing uncertain matches that could produce false-positive rewards. It is evaluated on LIBERO and MetaWorld benchmarks, showing particular improvements on long-horizon tasks where unreliable progress estimates are harmful.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for robot manipulation often struggles with reward design: sparse success rewards provide weak supervision, while hand-crafted dense rewards are tedious to design and generalize poorly. Progress-based reward models estimate how far an observation has advanced toward task completion, but existing approaches often require task-specific demonstrations or progress labels. RARM builds on contrastive temporal objectives, which learn representations by comparing temporally close observations, and uses a reference-anchored design to avoid rewarding visually plausible but physically incorrect states.

## [CARE Score and ReCARE Framework Preserve Benign Concepts in Diffusion Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24192) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the CARE score, a metric to quantify the preservation of benign co-occurring concepts during diffusion model unlearning, and proposes the ReCARE framework that automatically constructs a CARE-set to safeguard these concepts while erasing only the target concept. This work addresses a critical flaw in existing unlearning methods that often suppress benign concepts like 'person' when erasing 'nudity', which harms model utility. By providing a formal metric and a robust framework, it enables safer and more precise content moderation in diffusion models, with potential to influence future unlearning techniques. The CARE score is a general metric that directly quantifies the preservation of co-occurring concepts across unlearning tasks. ReCARE automatically constructs a CARE-set from target images and leverages it during training, achieving state-of-the-art performance in balancing erasure robustness, overall utility, and CARE preservation across targets like Nudity, Van Gogh style, and Tench object.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are powerful generative models that can create images from text prompts, but they can also generate harmful content. Unlearning is a technique to remove specific concepts from these models, but existing methods often inadvertently suppress benign concepts that co-occur with the target, reducing model utility. The CARE score and ReCARE framework address this by explicitly measuring and preserving these co-occurring concepts.

## [Unified Algebraic Framework for Classification Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04028) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a unified algebraic framework for classification performance evaluation that extends binary measures to multiclass, multilabel, ordinal, hierarchical, cost-sensitive, and soft-label settings using three aggregation operators. It provides theoretical results on micro/macro averaging and proves the uniqueness of the product t-norm for soft ground truth. This framework simplifies the extension of classification metrics across diverse settings, potentially reducing redundant derivations and promoting consistency in evaluation. It also offers theoretical insights that could guide the selection of appropriate metrics in research and practice. The framework represents actual and predicted labels as binary indicator matrices, with global, column-wise, and row-wise aggregation corresponding to micro, macro/weighted, and exemplar averaging. It shows that micro-averaging equals denominator-weighted macro-averaging only for aggregation-decomposable (linear-fractional) measures, and that micro-precision, micro-recall, and micro-F1 collapse to accuracy in multiclass settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Classification performance is often measured using metrics like precision, recall, and F1-score, which are defined for binary classification and extended to multiclass or multilabel problems via averaging strategies such as micro and macro averaging. T-norms are mathematical functions used in fuzzy logic to generalize conjunction, and they play a role in handling soft labels. The paper builds on these concepts to create a unified algebraic treatment.

## [Eluna: Graph-Guided Multi-Agent LLM System for Warehouse SOP Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08960) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Eluna is a production-deployed agentic LLM system that encodes Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs) as directed acyclic graphs with progressive disclosure, and uses asymmetric episodic distillation to train smaller models that match or exceed larger baselines, achieving 94% expert agreement on a ticket processing application. This work demonstrates a practical approach to enforcing procedural compliance in LLM agents for complex industrial tasks, addressing the context overload problem and improving reliability. It highlights the potential of graph-guided multi-agent systems and distillation techniques to make LLM agents viable for real-world automation, impacting warehouse operations and similar domains. The system uses progressive disclosure to avoid context overload, delegating independent tasks to parallel sub-agents with persistent code execution and live data access. Asymmetric episodic distillation involves a strong teacher improved through episodic error memories, then a smaller student fine-tuned on corrected trajectories with memory stripped, achieving production latency and accuracy without inference-time overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Warehouse operations are governed by Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs) that encode complex, multi-system decision logic, requiring reliable execution under strict time constraints. LLM agents often struggle with procedural compliance and context overload when given full SOP specifications. Eluna addresses this by encoding SOPs as DAGs with progressive disclosure, a technique where agents load only necessary information at each step, and by using distillation to compress a strong teacher model into a smaller student model.

This paper introduces a unified conceptual framework for discrete diffusion models, viewing them through the construction of the discrete state space. It unifies existing formulations such as transition-matrix, masking/absorbing-state, and score/ratio-based approaches into a common design space. This framework synthesizes existing approaches and reveals common design trade-offs, which could influence future research in discrete generative modeling. It is highly relevant to the AI/ML community as it provides a clearer understanding of design choices and suggests promising research directions. The framework exposes trade-offs across training objectives, inference algorithms, scaling behavior, systems optimization, and evaluation protocols. It is a theoretical contribution rather than a breakthrough with immediate practical impact, but it synthesizes existing approaches and suggests several promising directions for future research.

Unlike continuous diffusion, where the state space is fixed, DDMs are shaped by how the discrete state space is constructed, including tokenization schemes and vocabulary topology. This paper provides a unified view of these models, which have been developed through various formulations such as transition-matrix and masking approaches.

## [EviPathBench: New Benchmark for VLM Evidence Acquisition in Pathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19261) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EviPathBench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate vision-language models (VLMs) on whole-slide pathology, focusing on evidence acquisition and multi-scale reasoning. It includes 1,822 TCGA whole-slide images and 17,135 diagnostic paths annotated by ten board-certified pathologists, along with a private cohort of 190 breast cancer WSIs. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing pathology benchmarks, which typically evaluate models on pre-cropped patches or pre-extracted features, leaving evidence acquisition from gigapixel images untested. By providing a unified framework to measure both reasoning and evidence acquisition, EviPathBench could drive improvements in VLMs for real-world diagnostic workflows. The benchmark evaluates four capabilities: image-to-text matching, text-to-image retrieval, diagnostic-region localization, and multi-scale reasoning. Results show that leading open-weight models achieve over 93% accuracy in multi-scale reasoning and over 50% in cross-modal matching, but diagnostic-region localization remains challenging, with the best text-guided mean intersection-over-union below 0.09, underperforming a center-based heuristic.

## [LOCKS: Page-Local Compact Key Summaries for Efficient Long-Context Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24555) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LOCKS introduces a page-local spectral summary for KV cache compression, where each page maintains its own rank-r summary (r=8 or r=2), reducing cache size by 10x to 25x. It reconstructs within-page logits, estimates attention mass via log-sum-exp, and attends only top pages, achieving near-full-cache quality on LongBench and RULER benchmarks. This addresses the critical KV cache bottleneck in long-context LLM serving, enabling significant memory and latency reductions (up to 2x decode speedup) without substantial quality loss. It offers a drop-in plugin for vLLM, making it practical for real-world deployment and potentially improving efficiency for long-context applications. LOCKS selects pages based solely on summaries, reading no candidate keys or values during selection, and scans the summary fully each step, reducing per-step KV reads by 10-25x. At a 2048-token budget, it matches FullKV quality at 100K+ context (GLM-4-9B-Chat-1M) while attending about 2% of tokens, and runs in full CUDA graphs for batched decode.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: In long-context LLM serving, the KV cache stores key-value pairs for all tokens and is read fully at each decode step, causing memory and bandwidth bottlenecks. Attention keys are often locally low-rank but globally high-rank, so a fixed low-rank sketch shared across pages fails to capture page-specific directions. LOCKS addresses this by giving each page its own spectral summary, which better preserves page-specific information and enables efficient page selection.

## [MOSAIC: Efficient Secure Outsourcing of Transformer Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29221) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MOSAIC introduces a novel matrix-multiplication masking protocol that scales to large matrices, enabling secure outsourcing of transformer inference with optimal asymptotic client overhead and orders-of-magnitude speedups over prior work. The protocol's security is based on the decisional LWE and LPN assumptions. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in privacy-preserving machine learning by making secure outsourcing of large-scale AI computations practical. It could enable confidential AI inference in data centers with minimal client overhead, benefiting applications that require both privacy and efficiency. MOSAIC introduces small noise to multiplication results, relaxing correctness to achieve efficiency, and uses random Hadamard rotations to bound error accumulation across transformer layers. On 70B models, it achieves perplexity comparable to quantization and matches full-precision BF16 on HumanEval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Secure outsourcing of AI computations involves a trusted client with limited resources delegating computation to an untrusted server while keeping both input and model private. Traditional cryptographic methods like homomorphic encryption or secure multiparty computation are often too slow for large models. Matrix multiplication is a core operation in transformer inference, and masking protocols aim to hide data by adding random masks. The decisional LWE and LPN assumptions are standard hardness assumptions in lattice-based and code-based cryptography, respectively.

TabDPT-Turbo, released as TabDPT v1.2, introduces a more efficient tabular foundation model using row-based attention and long-context pre-training, achieving comparable performance to TabDPT v1.1 on TabArena-Lite, CC18, and CTR23 benchmarks at orders of magnitude faster inference speed. This development significantly improves the practicality of tabular foundation models by addressing the efficiency bottleneck, making them viable for compute-limited or latency-sensitive applications. It also sets a new standard for speed among leading foundation models, potentially accelerating adoption in real-world tabular prediction tasks. The model combines row-based attention with long-context pre-training to eliminate the need for retrieval, along with architectural improvements and SSL pre-training on a larger corpus of real data. In experiments, TabDPT-Turbo is the fastest model overall among leading foundation models, with an average speedup of ~120x on TabArena compared to TabDPT v1.1.

**Background**: Tabular foundation models use in-context learning to make predictions on new datasets without fine-tuning. Traditional approaches like TabPFN and TabDPT rely on row-based attention, but recent methods with cell-based architectures or retrieval have sacrificed efficiency for performance. TabDPT-Turbo returns to row-based attention while incorporating long-context pre-training to maintain performance without the overhead of retrieval.

The proposed model, using MuQ pretrained features and data augmentation, achieves 4.98% SER on classical music, outperforming the previous state-of-the-art of 15.3%. This work fills a critical gap in A2S research by providing a dataset for popular music, which has been underexplored compared to classical music. The significant improvement in SER on classical music and the strong benchmark on popular music could accelerate progress in music information retrieval and automatic transcription. The dataset includes 9,468 clips from 6,066 unique songs, with real commercially released audio paired with **kern lead-sheet encodings. The model achieves 20.92% SER on the SheetSage-A2S dataset, serving as a benchmark, and the dataset, model, and code are publicly available on GitHub.

## [MAPLE: Heterogeneous Expert Allocation Boosts MoE LLM Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15299) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MAPLE introduces a plug-and-play framework that reallocates routed-expert budgets heterogeneously across layers of pretrained Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) large language models without retraining or weight modification. It uses sensitivity-guided allocation to achieve superior performance and efficiency, as demonstrated on models like DeepSeek-MoE-16B. This work challenges the conventional uniform expert allocation in MoE Transformers, offering a principled way to improve efficiency and performance without costly retraining. It has practical implications for deploying large MoE models, potentially reducing latency and increasing throughput in real-world serving systems. MAPLE uses a closed-form sensitivity-guided allocation refined by a genetic search, and on DeepSeek-MoE-16B it improves accuracy on ARC-E, ARC-C, and BoolQ while using only 75% of experts. Implementing MAPLE in SGLang reduces single-GPU serving latency by 32.2% and improves throughput by 47.4%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a neural network architecture that activates only a subset of parameters (experts) per input, enabling larger models with lower computational cost. Traditional MoE Transformers use the same number of routed experts in every layer, which may be suboptimal because different layers have varying redundancy. MAPLE addresses this by allocating expert budgets based on layer-wise sensitivity, a measure of how much each layer's output changes with expert count.

The paper introduces a capability-driven data infrastructure that couples capability-specific supervision construction with capability-aligned curriculum scheduling for generalist image generation. It curates a 440M-image T2I corpus, 120M editing pairs, and over 27M image-entity pairs, and trains 3B and 6B multimodal diffusion models from scratch. The infrastructure comprises three specialized data engines for text-image grounding, inter-image transformation, and image-knowledge association, with caption experts aligning supervision across tasks. A multi-stage curriculum evolves task composition, visual-concept distribution, data quality, and image resolution, with capability-aware evaluation closing the loop.

## [Auditing Step-Level Credit Assignment in LLM Agents Against Executed Replay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19760) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel audit of step-level credit assignment in LLM agents, using policy-conditional ground truth from executed replay in the ALFWorld environment. It finds that none of the audited credit signals—LLM-judge scores, outcome-conditioned logprob ratios, or the policy's own confidence—shows reliable incremental fidelity beyond chance. This work challenges the validity of existing step-level credit assignment methods that are increasingly used in training loops for LLM agents. By demonstrating that common credit signals lack fidelity, it underscores the need for more rigorous evaluation and could influence future research on agent interpretability and training. The study uses a single-agent tool environment (ALFWorld) and finds that 30.5% of decision points exhibit a nonzero replay contrast. It also reveals that measurability is model-dependent, with the fraction of points lacking policy-supported counterfactuals differing twofold (13.1% vs. 26.8%) between two similar-scale policies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Step-level credit assignment in LLM agents involves assigning credit to individual steps (e.g., turns or actions) in a trajectory, which is crucial for training agents with sparse outcome rewards. Executed replay is a method that re-samples the policy's alternatives at each decision point and rolls forward to measure the causal contribution of a step to the final outcome. ALFWorld is a text-based embodied AI environment for household tasks, commonly used to benchmark agent performance.

## [Periodic Row-wise Muon Cuts DiT Training Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20818) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Periodic Row-wise Muon, a variant of the Muon optimizer that performs a full Newton-Schulz spectral update only once every K steps and uses a cheaper row-wise constrained update in between. This reduces optimizer time by 46.9–54.3%, end-to-end step time by 15.7–24.3%, and logical communication volume by 66.7% for Diffusion Transformers up to 15B parameters. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in scaling Muon for large Diffusion Transformers, making its quality advantages practical for real-world training. By significantly cutting overhead while preserving generative quality, it could accelerate large-scale generative model development and reduce training costs. Across scales, Periodic Row-wise Muon stays within 0.5% of vanilla Muon's best generative quality on 1.3B–4B models and improves it by 4.5% at 9B. It also reaches its best generative quality with 33.7–64.8% less active training time, and the distributed implementation uses bucketed all-gather and communication-computation overlap for spectral refreshes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Muon is a matrix-aware optimizer that balances updates across singular directions via Newton-Schulz iterations, improving convergence and quality over AdamW. However, performing these iterations every step and materializing full momentum introduces significant compute and communication overhead at scale. Periodic Row-wise Muon reduces this overhead by only doing the full spectral update periodically and using a cheaper row-wise update otherwise.

## [CIVA: Critic-Induced Value-Subspace Attacks on Visual World-Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21114) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CIVA, a novel white-box, causal, online attack method that efficiently generates temporally smooth adversarial perturbations against visual world-model agents like DreamerV3. It achieves the largest reward drop of 26.07% on DMC walker walk while maintaining low temporal variation, outperforming five recent methods. This work highlights a critical security vulnerability in world-model-based reinforcement learning agents, which are increasingly used in real-world applications. By demonstrating effective attacks, it underscores the need for robust defenses and contributes to the growing field of adversarial robustness in AI systems. CIVA first probes the frozen victim offline with critic-guided PGD and extracts a low-rank value-subspace via SVD. At test time, it optimizes only the subspace coefficients with exponential moving average (EMA) smoothing, mapping them back to pixels, which keeps online optimization cheap and temporally coherent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Visual world-model agents like DreamerV3 operate through a recurrent latent state rather than a single observation, which weakens frame-wise observation attacks and causes perturbations to vary sharply over time under strict per-frame constraints. World models learn to predict future states and rewards from sensory inputs, enabling agents to plan and act in diverse environments. Adversarial attacks on such agents aim to fool the policy by adding imperceptible perturbations to observations, but the recurrent nature poses unique challenges.

## [Visual Scaffolds Boost VLM Spatial Reasoning by 34 Points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21170) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SPaRC, a benchmark for grid-based visual spatial planning, and proposes lightweight input-side visual scaffolds that improve VLM spatial reasoning accuracy by up to 34.0 percentage points over the original visual setting. These scaffolds also complement GRPO-based reinforcement learning training, yielding up to 4.6 additional accuracy points. This work reveals that visual presentation is a central factor in VLM performance, affecting whether benchmarks measure grounded perception, downstream reasoning, or both. The findings offer practical prompt-design strategies for improving multimodal AI systems and highlight the importance of considering visual input structure in VLM evaluation and training. The scaffolds preserve the visual modality while making spatial structure more accessible, and their gains are closely tied to reductions in grounding-related errors, while rule reasoning remains comparatively challenging. The study analyzes both end-to-end task solving and object detection across multiple VLMs, showing that visual presentation shapes performance and failure modes even when the underlying reasoning problem is unchanged.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding for multimodal reasoning, but their failures often stem from an interaction between visual grounding and downstream reasoning. SPaRC is a new benchmark for grid-based visual spatial planning, and GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning method proposed by DeepSeek for training large language models. This paper investigates how the visual presentation of a task affects VLM performance, introducing input-side scaffolds to make spatial structure more explicit.

## [Supervised Structure Learning for Agent-Curated Knowledge Bases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21829) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a supervised structure learning method for agent-curated document stores, where a curator agent iteratively edits the knowledge base based on (question, answer) pairs, treating it as a trainable non-parametric model. The approach is evaluated on two new contamination-free benchmarks, KBGym and PhantomWiki, showing improved generalization with increasing training overlap. This work addresses a significant gap in current RAG pipelines by treating the knowledge base as a trainable component rather than a frozen input, potentially influencing future research on adaptive document stores. It also introduces new benchmarks that could become standard for evaluating knowledge base construction methods. The curated store's advantage grows with overlap: from parity with no shared keys, to +0.176 F1 with both keys shared, and 25% fewer actions at +0.294 F1 on trained questions. It matches HippoRAG's gains with 1,913 links versus 196,112, achieving 1.5x action saving and 2.1x accuracy gain per point of corpus covered.

The approach is evaluated on KBGym, a fictional-universe generator, and PhantomWiki, both designed to avoid contamination.

## [SANE Stabilizes Delta-Rule Models for Extreme Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22354) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies localized norm explosion as the failure mode of Delta-Rule recurrent models on extreme-context sequences and proposes State Anomaly Neutralization (SANE), an adaptive tanh compression method applied at chunk boundaries. SANE stabilizes training and maintains reasoning capability even after a 100M-token prefix, which exceeds training length by over 24,000×. This work addresses a critical stability issue in Delta-Rule recurrent models, which are promising for efficient long-context processing due to their fixed-size state and O(1) inference memory. The proposed SANE method enables these models to extrapolate to extreme contexts without degradation, potentially advancing long-context AI applications. SANE applies adaptive tanh compression at chunk boundaries while preserving the intra-chunk parallel structure. Within a safe threshold range (3 ≤ α ≤ 5), SANE matches baseline performance on 11 short-context reasoning benchmarks with no statistically significant degradation, but overly permissive thresholds (α ≥ 8) lose reasoning capability entirely.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Delta-Rule recurrent models, such as RWKV-7, maintain a fixed-size state that enables O(1) inference memory but can become unstable under extreme-context extrapolation. The paper empirically identifies localized norm explosion as the failure pattern, where a few channels accumulate extreme values due to uneven injections, rather than global state saturation. SANE uses tanh compression, a common activation function that maps inputs to a range between -1 and 1, to neutralize these anomalies.

## [Functional Compatibility Determines Persistent Neural Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22462) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies functional compatibility as a causal determinant of whether new learning persists in neural networks, reframing the stability-plasticity dilemma from preventing forgetting to determining what is retained. It demonstrates this through experiments across architectures and domains, including a ten-seed replication. This provides a new perspective on the stability-plasticity dilemma, a core challenge in continual learning, and could guide the design of more effective continual learning algorithms. It offers an experimentally manipulable variable, which may lead to more principled approaches to balancing stability and plasticity. The study varies functional compatibility from identical neural states while matching unrestricted learning opportunity and imposing a common retention requirement. It finds that persistent learning increases with compatibility across convolutional and transformer architectures, vision and text tasks, and that nonlinear geometry limits the matched intervention at larger update norms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to enable neural networks to learn new tasks without forgetting previously acquired knowledge, a challenge known as the stability-plasticity dilemma. Traditional approaches focus on mitigating catastrophic forgetting, but this paper shifts the focus to understanding what determines whether new learning persists. Functional compatibility refers to the extent to which incoming learning can coexist with behavior that must be preserved.

This paper formalizes prefix invariance for hybrid sequence models and introduces a lightweight audit requiring only two forward passes, no training or gradients, that produces a per-layer score localizing causality breaks. The audit detected all 192/192 injected faults across eight checkpoints, while mask inspection found none, and it identified real defects in Zamba2 and Nemotron-H. This work addresses a critical yet overlooked issue in hybrid sequence models, showing that attention-mask inspection is insufficient for verifying causality. The practical, seconds-fast audit provides a valuable tool for practitioners, potentially improving model reliability and trustworthiness in deployed systems. The audit is based on graph-level causality, revealing that leaks can occur via scans, aggregations, or normalization despite correct masks. Static/dynamic analysis of chunked-scan code in transformers found an inter-chunk axis error in Zamba2 and Nemotron-H, fixed via the reference implementation.

**Background**: Hybrid sequence models combine Transformer attention layers with state-space model (SSM) layers to balance expressivity and efficiency. Prefix invariance requires that representations at position t depend only on past inputs, a fundamental property for causal sequence modeling. Chunked-scan is a technique used in efficient SSM implementations to process sequences in blocks, which can introduce subtle causality violations if not carefully implemented.

<a id="item-168"></a>
## [Data-Driven Distillation Beats Fine-Tuning for Cross-Domain D2T](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23391) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces DDKD, a data-driven knowledge distillation approach with structure-preserving augmentation, which outperforms both fine-tuning and zero-shot inference for cross-domain, multi-task data-to-text generation without in-domain training data. Experiments on five benchmarks show consistent improvements at a constant model size of 1.7B parameters. This work addresses a practical and challenging problem in NLP: generating text from structured data across domains without in-domain training data. It demonstrates that distillation can be more effective than fine-tuning or zero-shot inference, potentially reducing the need for large labeled datasets and enabling more efficient deployment of small models. The method uses structure-preserving augmentation via structural subsampling and perturbation, and constructs QUINTD-5, a fivefold extension of QUINTD-1. The resulting small models outperform a much larger fine-tuned model on two of five domains and achieve comparable performance on the remaining three.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Data-to-text (D2T) generation converts structured data like tables or knowledge graphs into natural language. Traditional approaches rely on task-specific training data or large language models' zero-shot capabilities, but both have limitations in cross-domain scenarios. Knowledge distillation is a technique where a smaller 'student' model learns to mimic a larger 'teacher' model, often using unlabeled data.

<a id="item-169"></a>
## [TimeCatch Benchmark Reveals VLMs Fail at Temporal Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23474) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TimeCatch, a new benchmark for evaluating temporal consistency in vision-language models (VLMs) by framing temporal grounding as an anomaly detection task. It reveals that VLMs perform near chance on temporal anomaly detection while excelling at frame-level anomaly detection, indicating a significant gap in temporal reasoning. This finding is significant because it highlights a fundamental limitation in current VLMs: they can perceive individual frames but struggle to integrate information across time. This could impact applications like video understanding, autonomous driving, and surveillance, and may guide future model development toward better temporal reasoning capabilities. TimeCatch creates temporal anomalies by swapping consecutive frames and frame-level anomalies by replacing a frame with Gaussian noise, and evaluates models on anomaly detection and localization across four datasets. The study also includes a human study, where humans achieve near-ceiling performance, and analyses across model scales, prompting strategies, sequence lengths, and visual similarity suggest the failures are not solely due to perception or capacity limitations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both visual and textual data, often used for tasks like image captioning and video understanding. Temporal consistency refers to the ability to understand the order and relationship of events over time, which is crucial for interpreting videos. Anomaly detection is a common task in computer vision where models identify unusual patterns; TimeCatch adapts this to test temporal grounding.

<a id="item-170"></a>
## [BPCO: A Stable Critic-Based RL Recipe for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23566) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Best Practice Critic Optimization (BPCO), a training recipe that combines DPPO, bounded value predictions, Monte Carlo value targets, unnormalized policy advantages, and length-adaptive GAE to stabilize critic-based RL for LLMs. Across mathematical reasoning tasks with models from 1.5B to 30B-A3B MoE, BPCO consistently improves a strong critic-based baseline and matches or exceeds GRPO while sampling only one response per prompt. This work addresses a known instability issue in critic-based RL for LLMs, offering a reliable alternative to group-relative advantage estimation like GRPO. It could improve sample efficiency and enable the use of reward-defining information (e.g., rubrics) hidden from the policy, impacting LLM alignment and reasoning tasks. BPCO conditions the critic on reward-defining information (e.g., reference answer or grading rubric) that is hidden from the policy, since the critic is only used during training. The recipe also improves learning with rubric-based rewards, and code is available at the provided GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Group-based RL methods like GRPO avoid training a critic by sampling multiple responses per prompt, but a reliable critic could estimate token-level advantages from a single response. However, standard critic-based recipes are often unstable. BPCO combines several techniques to stabilize training, including DPPO (a variant of PPO), Monte Carlo value targets, and length-adaptive GAE, which adjusts the GAE lambda based on response length to balance bias and variance.

It uses OTel traces and text world models to simulate and route requests to the optimal model, with optional opt-in training on usage data. This project addresses the growing need for efficient and cost-effective LLM routing in a fragmented model landscape. By being open-source and markup-free, it challenges existing gateways that charge a premium, potentially lowering barriers for developers and enterprises to adopt multi-model strategies. The gateway adds under 1 ms for BYOK requests and under 2 ms when Experiential supplies the provider key. It supports 1000+ models refreshed daily via a codex agent, and uses a nearest neighbor classifier on prompt embeddings to decide the optimal model, also enabling cache hit optimizations and model suggestions.

It references real AWS Route 53, a DNS service, and plays on the idea of using DNS records as a file storage medium, which is technically impractical but conceptually amusing.

## [RENDER: New Benchmark Control Reveals LLM Memory Formatting Impact](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23568) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RENDER is a new benchmark control that fixes the conversation while varying the reader-facing artifact, such as memory entries, summaries, typed records, or raw dialogue. On 500 LongMemEval questions across nine models, matched-budget resolved packets outperformed recency-truncated raw dialogue by 42.4–72.6 points. This work highlights that the format of information presented to an LLM can significantly affect memory and RAG evaluation results, suggesting that benchmarks should report or control this variable. It could lead to more reliable comparisons of memory systems and better understanding of how to present historical data to LLMs. The RENDER control uses a five-level packet ladder to localize when answer-bearing content enters the input, and deterministic templates approximating ChatGPT-style entries, LangChain summaries, MemGPT-style typed records, and raw conversation. In deployed-style templates, the best-worst spread ranged from 24.6 to 48.8 points per model, and under the primary scorer, ChatGPT-style entries had higher point estimates than raw conversation on 7 of 9 models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Memory and RAG evaluations often treat the answering model's input as an implementation detail, but systems may render the same history in different formats. RENDER addresses this by controlling the reader-facing artifact while keeping the conversation fixed. The study uses LongMemEval, a benchmark for long-term memory in chat assistants, and also tests on HotpotQA to show the effect transfers to other tasks.

## [TRACE: Transition-Aware Framework Boosts Multi-Objective Materials Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRACE is a novel framework that records parent-edit-child transitions and uses observed property deltas to guide local edits, improving multi-objective materials discovery with LLM agents. In a controlled comparison, TRACE raised the macro-average hit rate from 18.13% to 25.96% over the state-of-the-art LLEMA baseline. This framework addresses a key limitation in LLM-based materials discovery: efficiently using costly property evaluations to guide iterative search. By treating edits as the basic unit of feedback, TRACE can improve the efficiency and effectiveness of multi-objective optimization, potentially accelerating materials discovery in real-world applications. TRACE aggregates transition evidence to estimate reusable edit effects and ranks future edits by their predicted ability to reduce constraint violations while avoiding damage to satisfied objectives. The framework retains global LLM proposals for broader exploration, combining local refinement with global search.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective materials discovery aims to find materials that satisfy multiple conflicting property constraints. LLM agents can propose candidate materials, but each property evaluation is costly, so efficient use of feedback is crucial. Traditional agents store evaluated candidates and scores but not the specific edits that caused property changes, limiting local refinement. TRACE addresses this by recording parent-edit-child transitions and using observed property deltas to guide future edits.

## [STEP-KTODER: Function-Level Feedback for Code Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23632) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces STEP-KTODER, a framework that applies process supervision to code generation by defining steps as module-level functions and labeling them via automatically generated unit tests. It combines function-level process supervision with outcome-level feedback within a KTO framework, improving over outcome-only KTO and DPO on benchmarks like HumanEval and MBPP. This work addresses the underexplored area of process supervision in code generation, where defining intermediate steps is challenging. By showing that execution-based labels are crucial and LLM-as-a-judge can be misleading, it provides a more reliable method for improving code LLMs, potentially influencing future alignment techniques. STEP-KTODER uses KTO, which requires only binary desirable/undesirable labels, unlike DPO's pairwise preferences. The method is evaluated on HumanEval(+), MBPP(+), BigCodeBench, and LiveCodeBench, and the code is available on GitHub. The analysis reveals that LLM-as-a-judge annotations systematically over-predict function failures, corrupting positive labels and degrading performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Process supervision has been effective in mathematical reasoning, where steps are natural as chains of thought. In code generation, however, there is no standard notion of a step, making supervision unclear. KTO is an alignment method based on prospect theory that requires only binary feedback per response, unlike DPO which needs preference pairs. Benchmarks like HumanEval and MBPP test code generation by checking functional correctness against unit tests.

## [Framework for Ethical LLM-Assisted Research Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23644) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a normative framework for delegating scientific reasoning to LLMs, distinguishing content origin, verification, responsibility, ownership, and epistemic outcome. It proposes the concept of an 'epistemic audit' to ensure transparency and accountability in AI-assisted research. As LLMs become routine in scientific research, this framework addresses the critical question of maintaining epistemic legitimacy and human accountability. It could influence future guidelines and practices for responsible AI use in academia and research institutions. The framework defines constructs O(g), V(g), R(g), M(g), and E(g) to separate provenance from verification and responsibility. The central proposition is that ethical boundaries depend on adequate verification and human ownership, not the degree of machine involvement.
