## [MolEmb: Multimodal LLMs as General Molecular Embedding Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23646) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MolEmb, a lightweight framework that adapts multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to produce molecular embeddings conditioned on both molecular profiles and natural-language semantic contexts, using a bidirectional contrastive objective. It also presents MolCAR, a diagnostic benchmark for context-aware retrieval, and demonstrates competitive performance on molecular property prediction and cross-modal molecule-text retrieval. This work challenges the prevailing approach of specialist molecular encoders by showing that MLLMs can serve as general molecular embedding models with a language interface, enabling flexible, context-aware representations. This could significantly impact computational chemistry and drug discovery by simplifying the pipeline for property prediction and virtual screening, and by making embeddings more adaptable to diverse tasks. The bidirectional contrastive objective aligns molecular profiles with textual descriptions in a shared embedding space, enabling both forward and reverse alignment. The authors find that context-aware molecular embedding is primarily a data property of the supervision, suggesting that the quality of the training data is crucial for achieving effective language-conditioned embeddings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Molecular embedding models convert molecular structures into vector representations that serve as foundational infrastructure for tasks like property prediction, virtual screening, and retrieval. Traditional molecular encoders are often specialist models that produce unconditional vectors without a language interface. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) natively process images, text, and symbolic inputs, and have shown emergent capabilities in various tasks. This paper explores using MLLMs as general molecular embedding models, leveraging their language understanding to condition embeddings on semantic context.

## [Gated Activation Steering Reduces Sycophancy and Hallucination in Medical QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23666) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a unified inference-time intervention method that learns separate steering directions for hallucination and sycophancy from contrastive clinical pairs, applying them to causally verified attention heads with behavior-specific gates. In experiments on EHR-based clinical questions, gated steering helped a 4-billion-parameter model resist user pressure in 551 out of 600 pressure trajectories, matching the robustness of models over 100 billion parameters. This work addresses two critical failure modes in LLMs—sycophancy and hallucination—within the high-stakes domain of medical question answering. By jointly mitigating both behaviors via targeted inference-time steering, it offers a parameter-free approach that improves robustness without degrading already-correct responses, potentially enhancing trust in AI-assisted clinical decision support. The method uses Inference Time Intervention (ITI) to shift activations on a limited set of attention heads, with separate steering vectors for hallucination and sycophancy. Behavior-specific gates determine when to apply each intervention, avoiding unnecessary modifications to correct responses. The evaluation involved 15,900 model-response runs across all settings, with 600 pressure trajectories for the 4B model, showing significant improvement in resistance to user pressure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Inference Time Intervention (ITI) is a technique that shifts model activations during inference along learned directions on selected attention heads to improve truthfulness without retraining. Activation steering modifies LLM internal activations to reliably alter outputs, often using steering vectors. Contrastive learning involves training on positive and negative examples to better capture desired behaviors. Sycophancy refers to models changing correct answers under user pressure, while hallucination involves generating unsupported information.

## [BioCheck Agent: RL-Enhanced Agentic Search for Biomedical Fact-Checking Reports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23811) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BioCheck Agent, an LLM-based agent that generates structured biomedical fact-checking reports using agentic search, and proposes a novel reinforcement learning method, Evidence-Grounded Group Relative Policy Optimization (EG-GRPO), to improve accuracy and reduce hallucinations. Experiments show that compared to the base model Qwen3.5-4B, BioCheck Agent with EG-GRPO improves label prediction accuracy on SciFact by 9.95%, achieves a 3.7% higher evidence quality score, and reduces evidence hallucination rate by 19.63%. This work addresses a critical gap in automated fact-checking by moving beyond label-only outputs to generate explainable, evidence-backed reports, which is essential for public health trust. The RL-enhanced agentic search approach could set a new standard for biomedical fact-checking and inspire similar methods in other high-stakes domains. BioCheck Agent exclusively searches PubMed using advanced Boolean search operators to ensure domain-specific accuracy. The EG-GRPO method uses a task-specific reward that incentivizes advanced search behavior and high-quality evidence retrieval while penalizing hallucinations, addressing the issue of direct prompting leading to hallucinations in lightweight open-source models.

This paper proposes a formal methodological framework to audit the robustness and fidelity of post-hoc explainable AI methods, combining these two properties into a single Trust Score. The framework is applied to a malnutrition dataset from Madagascar, revealing that high-accuracy models can produce unreliable explanations. This work addresses the critical issue of trust in explainable AI, showing that even models with AUC above 0.99 can yield degenerate or uninformative explanations. It underscores the necessity of auditing XAI outputs, especially in sensitive domains like food security, and could influence how XAI methods are validated and deployed. The protocol measures robustness (stability under input perturbation) and fidelity (whether important features actually drive predictions), combining them into a Trust Score. Experiments used three classifiers and two explainers (SHAP and LIME) plus their regularized counterparts on a dataset with 83 features, 253 records, and 4 malnutrition classes.

## [Minima-KV: Mixed-Format Paged Attention for KV Cache Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23834) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Minima-KV introduces a retention-preserving KV cache compression hierarchy that keeps recent and protected Anchor pages in FP8 while moving older non-anchor pages to packed TQ3 format, enabling direct heterogeneous decode without a dense shadow. On a single 96-GB NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU, it achieves 3.50x compression over BF16 and 1.75x over FP8, with quality maintained on long-context benchmarks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in long-context LLM serving by significantly reducing KV cache memory and bandwidth requirements, which can lower serving costs and improve throughput. It offers a practical mixed-format path that maintains quality, making it relevant for deploying large models on limited hardware. The method uses format-specific kernels to compute partial attention states and combines them via a globally normalized online-softmax merge, avoiding a cache-sized dense dequantization shadow. In tests, it achieved 18.3 KiB of attention KV per live token, matched dense control on 16K RULER tasks, and showed deltas of -0.80, -0.60, and -0.40 percentage points on LongBench v2 at 16K, 32K, and 64K lengths, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache stores past keys and values during autoregressive decoding to avoid recomputation, but it grows linearly with context length and becomes a memory and bandwidth bottleneck. PagedAttention reduces fragmentation by managing cache in fixed-size pages, and quantization formats like FP8 and TQ3 reduce memory footprint at the cost of precision. Minima-KV combines these ideas by using a mixed-format hierarchy where recent pages stay in higher precision and older pages are compressed more aggressively.

This paper introduces SyPS, a framework for measuring how LLM sycophancy varies with prompt changes, and proposes the Sycophancy Prompt Sensitivity Score (SPSS) for instance-level assessment. This work addresses an underexplored aspect of LLM evaluation—robustness of sycophancy to prompt variations—which is crucial for AI safety and alignment. It provides a more nuanced metric that separates baseline sycophancy from prompt-induced shifts, enabling better model comparisons. SyPS constructs controlled prompt variants that preserve the underlying user situation while varying social cues like confidence, emotional framing, social consensus, and validation-seeking language. Empirically, validation-seeking and emotional-pressure cues often increase sycophancy, while counter-framing and anti-sycophancy prompts reduce it.

This approach introduces a new paradigm that bridges temporal and visual domains, potentially reducing the need for specialized temporal architectures and extensive domain-specific training. It could make forecasting more accessible and adaptable, especially in limited-data settings. In-context learning allows models to perform tasks from examples without parameter updates, which ICI-Time leverages by constructing grid-structured prompts.

## [Provenance-Guided Incremental Learning for Rule-Induced Concept Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23893) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a provenance-guided incremental learning framework that compiles consecutive concept definitions into a structured rule delta, traces changed components through historical provenance, and selectively relabels only affected records. It also presents RuleShift-Bench, a benchmark covering various types of concept revisions, and reports 92.3% accuracy while reprocessing only 14.7% of historical data. This work addresses a novel and important problem: adapting to concept drift caused by explicit rule revisions, not just data changes. It offers a more efficient alternative to full relabeling and retraining, which is crucial for long-lived learning systems in dynamic environments. The framework uses a versioned concept memory to support recurring definitions and handles ambiguous cases through selective supervision. Across the benchmark, it achieves 92.3% accuracy and 90.2% Macro-F1, with an average update latency of 179 seconds compared to 993 seconds for complete relabeling and retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Concept drift refers to changes in the relationship between input data and target labels over time. Traditional methods infer such changes from data or prediction errors, but this paper focuses on rule-induced concept shift, where the target-defining rule is explicitly revised, causing historical instances to acquire new labels without any change in their observed data. Provenance, which tracks the origin and history of data, is used here to identify which records are affected by rule changes.

The benchmark draws from 96 source protocols across nine wet-lab biology domains and includes only tasks rated highly by domain experts. The benchmark remains unsaturated even when taking the best of ten attempts, indicating room for improvement in current models.

## [Evolutionary Recurrent Decision Model Simulates Adaptive and Maladaptive Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23932) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Evolutionary Recurrent Decision Model (ERDM), a computational reinforcement learning framework that simulates how evolutionary mismatch, bounded rationality, and satisficing lead to adaptive and maladaptive behaviors. In a validity study, ERDM naturally produced strategies like learned helplessness, avoidance, healthy relationships, and aggression without hardwiring them. This work bridges evolutionary psychology and reinforcement learning, offering a fresh computational perspective on psychopathology. It suggests that many mental health issues can be understood as bounded cognitive systems operating under modern-ancestral environmental mismatch, which could inform future research in computational psychiatry and AI-based behavioral modeling. ERDM simulates agents across evolutionary recurrent environments including threat, prey/goal-pursuits, and alliances, with learning driven by competing rewards abstracted from survival metrics. The validity study varied adverse childhood experiences and found that the emergent behaviors align with empirical literature, demonstrating ecological validity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary mismatch occurs when a trait that was once advantageous becomes maladaptive due to rapid environmental change. Bounded rationality, a concept from Herbert Simon, describes how decision-makers are limited by cognitive capabilities, time, and information, often leading to satisficing—choosing the first satisfactory option rather than the optimal one. ERDM integrates these concepts into a reinforcement learning framework to model behavior.

## [Twin-Prefix Framework Shows Longer LLM Oversight Windows Reduce Discrimination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23941) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the twin-prefix framework, a controlled, pre-registered instrument to isolate the effect of verification unit size on pre-execution LLM oversight. It finds that longer review windows make zero-shot monitors more rejective but not more discriminative, with informedness peaking at one or two actions. This work addresses an underexplored problem in AI safety: how the unit of verification affects the reliability of pre-execution oversight. It provides a rigorous methodology that could improve the design of AI control protocols, helping to balance safety and usefulness in deployed systems. The framework uses gold plans with injected errors and clean twins, judged at five nested lengths to tie verdict changes solely to the unit. Discrimination is scored using pre-registered informedness (catch minus false rejection), and the failure is traced to observation deprivation by replaying withheld observations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Pre-execution oversight is a core component of AI control, where a fallible LLM monitor reviews planned actions before irreversible execution. Over-blocking can reduce usefulness and lead to disabling the monitor, so it is crucial to understand how the unit of verification (how many actions one call reviews) affects performance. Natural traces cannot isolate this effect because review length co-varies with error type and position, making controlled experiments necessary.

## [Giraffe: Efficient Graphic Design Generation via Single-Token Visual Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23970) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Giraffe, a novel architecture that maps hidden text representations to visual embeddings (e.g., CLIP ViT-L/14) using a single [IMG] token per image, significantly reducing input length. It employs two shallow MLP blocks with separate compression and shared expansion modules, trained with six loss functions, and one block is omitted during inference. This addresses a major limitation in multimodal LLMs for graphic design generation, where outputs involve thousands of tokens across text, images, and layout. By using a single token per image, Giraffe improves efficiency and could enable more practical applications in design automation and content creation. The architecture uses two shallow MLP blocks: one aids the other during training and is omitted during inference, resulting in a lightweight solution. Strong performance is demonstrated in both image-to-design and text-to-design generation tasks, with six distinct loss functions used for training.

CLIP ViT-L/14 is a vision-language model that embeds images and text into a shared 768-dimensional space, enabling cross-modal tasks. Giraffe leverages this embedding space to compress image representation into a single token, addressing efficiency challenges in graphic design generation.

## [Auditable, User-Configurable Argument Selection for Deliberative Polling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes replacing opaque learned rankers in deliberative polling with a published, voter-configurable rule based on reversed endorsement flow, ensuring legibility and contestability. The rule is formalized with seven checkable criteria and evaluated via an agentic simulator over about 17,000 seed-paired runs. This work addresses a critical gap in civic technology by making the argument selection mechanism auditable and user-configurable, which is essential for democratic legitimacy. It shows that the advantage of opaque rankers is bounded and small, suggesting that transparency need not come at a significant cost to accuracy. The proposed rule is a one-hop reversed endorsement flow parameterized by a relation-weight function. Simulation results show that served slates fall 0.035 short of a label-reading ceiling, and the rule dominates on mass by a factor of 3.3, while coverage is indistinguishable from random under non-degenerate authoring.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Deliberative polling is a method that combines informed discussion with polling to gauge public preferences, enhancing democratic decision-making. In such polls, when submissions exceed what voters can read, a mechanism selects which arguments each voter sees; traditionally, this has been done by opaque learned rankers, which voters cannot recompute or contest.

This paper systematically analyzes when conditional memory should be used in scientific reasoning and proposes a Knowledge Boundary-Aware Router that dynamically decides whether to activate memory, where to inject it, and how strongly, based on input proxies before generation. This work addresses a critical gap in making memory-augmented language models more reliable for scientific reasoning. By selectively allocating memory, it reduces regressions while preserving benefits, which could improve performance in biology and chemistry tasks and guide future memory design. The router uses task-specific input proxies available before generation to decide memory activation, layer-stage injection points, and signal strength. Experiments cover two backbone families and six task types on biological and chemical reasoning benchmarks, showing that memory effects vary substantially across inputs, tasks, and injection locations.

This work extends prior research on conditional memory, such as DeepSeek's Engram, by focusing on selective activation rather than always-on memory.

This work addresses a critical gap in knowledge tracing by moving beyond single-domain models to handle real-world multi-domain learning scenarios. By incorporating cognitive load and knowledge transfer, it could lead to more accurate and personalized educational AI systems, benefiting both students and educators. LT-MKT uses large language models to integrate textual information from questions and concepts into a Multi-domain Hierarchical Graph. It explicitly models cross-domain features in temporal and knowledge dimensions to capture cognitive load, and includes a knowledge transfer module to propagate knowledge states within and across domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

Cognitive load theory explains how working memory limitations affect learning, while knowledge transfer refers to how knowledge in one domain influences learning in related domains. The paper introduces Evidence-First Reflection (EFR), a two-stage reflector that decouples action-induced visual difference extraction from outcome verification, improving desktop GUI agent performance. Experiments on OSWorld-Verified and WindowsAgentArena show reflector accuracy gains of 7.11% and end-to-end task success gains of 5.94% and 4.95%, respectively. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in desktop GUI agents, where large and dense interfaces make reflection challenging. By improving reflection accuracy, EFR enhances the reliability of autonomous desktop automation, which is significant for AI-driven productivity tools and human-computer interaction. EFR uses Set-of-Marks annotations to identify action locations and candidate changed regions, then describes and filters action-relevant changes before making a final judgment. This decoupled design reduces visual search complexity and reasoning burden, leading to better grounding in screen transitions.

## [Relative Timestamps and Masked Training Enable Word-Level Timestamping in SpeechLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24041) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method to make SpeechLLMs temporally aware by replacing absolute timestamps with relative timestamps and using a masked timestamp training objective. The approach achieves significant improvements in word-level timestamp prediction accuracy while maintaining strong speech transcription performance. This work addresses a critical gap in SpeechLLMs, which previously excelled at content understanding but lacked fine-grained temporal alignment. Enabling word-level timestamp prediction opens up new applications in speech processing, such as forced alignment, subtitle generation, and audio-visual synchronization, and could influence future research on temporally aware multimodal models. The method uses a hybrid fine-tuning strategy: full-parameter fine-tuning of the timestamp-augmented embedding layer and language model head, combined with LoRA fine-tuning of the decoder layers. The masked timestamp training objective prevents over-reliance on ground-truth timestamps, enhancing robustness against noisy real-world annotations.

## [AgentWorld: Personality-Aware Simulation for Evaluating Agentic IR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24076) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentWorld is a new simulation framework for evaluating agentic information retrieval systems, incorporating Big Five (OCEAN) personality-driven user populations, a pass^k consistency metric, adversarial risk analysis, and fine-tuning data export. The framework was demonstrated through three experiments, including a conversational analytics agent across 10 OCEAN personas and adversarial stress-testing of 5 tasks. This framework addresses a critical gap in evaluating agentic AI systems, which are often tested with uniform, scripted users and miss natural personality diversity and adversarial brittleness. By enabling personality-aware and adversarial evaluation, AgentWorld could lead to more robust and reliable agentic systems in production, benefiting developers and end-users alike. The framework includes a Risk Analyser that snapshots required-intermediate-state spines, branches Monte-Carlo rollouts under four perturbation types, and quantifies risk via ΔP/ΔT scoring, Dempster-Shafer evidence fusion, and Shapley attack-category attribution. Experiments revealed pre-existing trajectory brittleness (V_min=0.375 without perturbation) and tool/infrastructure-layer attack dominance (Shapley: 46% system, 38% action), as well as a 0.27-point quality gap and 50% vs. 100% pass-rate across personas on the same task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic information retrieval (IR) systems are AI agents that perform tasks like searching and analyzing data through tool use. Traditional evaluation methods often use scripted interactions with uniform users, which fail to capture the diversity of real users and the potential for adversarial failures. The Big Five personality model (OCEAN) is a widely used framework for describing human personality traits, and pass^k is a consistency metric that measures whether an agent can reliably produce correct results across multiple attempts. Dempster-Shafer theory is a mathematical framework for reasoning under uncertainty, and Shapley values are used to attribute contributions to different factors.

## [ACE: Self-Correcting Agentic Canvas Editor for Presentations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24103) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ACE introduces a hierarchical scene-graph editor with a 98-tool action space, a content-aware router (CARE) that reduces input tokens by ~89%, and a ground-truth-free self-correction loop. It outperforms a same-backbone agentic HTML pipeline on instruction following (IF 4.23 vs. 3.81) at 1.75x speed and ~44% lower cost. This work addresses two critical obstacles in LLM-based document editing: layout preservation in legacy formats and the lack of ground truth in design tasks. By enabling efficient, self-correcting presentation automation, it could significantly improve productivity for designers and non-experts, and its techniques may generalize to other document editing domains. The self-correction loop uses a ground-truth-free instruction-following judge whose natural-language critique is fed back as the next-turn instruction. 66% of cases halt after one pass, and a strict-peak rollback removes all observed regressions; out-of-loop judges retain two-thirds of the self-correction gain, bounding circularity.

ACE leverages a hierarchical scene graph to preserve structure and a ground-truth-free judge to enable self-correction without reference outputs.

## [QC-T2I-Bench: Question-Centric Evaluation for Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces QC-T2I-Bench, a question-centric evaluation framework that converts open prompts into attributed atomic questions and organizes their dependencies using Davidsonian Scene Graphs (DSGs). It employs hierarchy-constrained question aggregation to exclude downstream questions after a prerequisite fails and to balance simple versus complex prompts, enabling reliable ranking and fine-grained diagnosis of T2I models. This framework addresses limitations in current T2I benchmarks that often fail to attribute failures to specific capabilities or distinguish basic from compositional failures. By providing reliable rankings and fine-grained diagnosis, it can help practitioners select models based on specific strengths and guide future evaluation practices in the T2I community. The framework uses hierarchy-constrained question aggregation to exclude dependency-invalid evidence and balance capabilities, preventing simple and complex prompts from receiving the same total weight. Evaluation on multiple open-source T2I models with English and Chinese prompts shows joint completion drops from 80.7% for components with two capabilities to 37.2% for those with seven or more, and a cost-aware router matches ERNIE's 89.51-point estimate with 21.3% less GPU-s/MP.

Davidsonian Scene Graphs (DSGs) provide a fine-grained evaluation framework inspired by formal semantics, decomposing prompts into atomic questions and organizing them hierarchically. This paper builds on DSG to create a scalable, question-centric benchmark that supports reliable ranking and diagnosis.

The paper introduces AdaptRubric, a coarse-to-fine rubrics framework that constructs task-adaptive judging criteria for GUI outcome reward modeling. It improves F1 by 3.6 points over the baseline average and yields a 4.23-point task-success gain in online reinforcement learning. This work addresses a key limitation in GUI reward modeling—the lack of task-adaptive judging criteria—which is crucial for training effective GUI agents. By improving reward accuracy, AdaptRubric can enhance the performance of GUI agents in real-world automation tasks, benefiting fields like AI, HCI, and robotics. AdaptRubric operates in two stages: a category-level coarse stage that retrieves reusable task-family criteria, and an instance-level fine stage that generates compact cues for concrete values, scopes, and constraints. The framework consistently outperforms prior reward agents across offline reward evaluation and online reinforcement learning optimization.

Existing reward verifiers often use generic rubric structures or implicit reasoning, which may transfer checks across tasks, overlook concrete constraints, or enforce unstated requirements. AdaptRubric addresses this by constructing task-adaptive judging criteria through a coarse-to-fine approach.

## [BALIGN: Data Selection to Reduce Alignment Tax in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24192) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BALIGN, a balanced data selection strategy that mitigates catastrophic forgetting during LLM alignment by filtering out high-risk preference samples based on three key data features: reference model log-probability margin, token length difference, and TF-IDF similarity to general capability corpora. This work addresses the alignment tax problem, a significant challenge in deploying LLMs that often leads to degraded general capabilities. By providing a data-centric solution that preserves foundational skills without sacrificing alignment gains, it offers a practical and computationally efficient approach for the AI community. BALIGN aggregates three orthogonal features into a unified composite risk score and systematically filters high-risk samples. Experiments on standard human preference datasets show that BALIGN consistently achieves the optimal Pareto frontier with minimal computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Alignment tax refers to the degradation of pre-trained general capabilities that occurs when fine-tuning LLMs to follow human preferences, often due to catastrophic forgetting. Previous mitigation efforts focused on optimization or architectural changes, but this paper explores the role of preference data characteristics in driving this degradation.

## [Constraint-Guided Mapping Boosts LLM Enterprise Data Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24218) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces constraint-guided mapping (CGM), a neuro-symbolic method that integrates schema-grounded admissibility constraints with LLMs to ensure operationally valid enterprise entity alignments. CGM uses three stages: constraint definition, constraint-restricted candidate generation with cascade relaxation, and neural ranking with bounded LLM disambiguation. This work addresses a critical limitation of LLM-only data mapping, which often produces fluent but operationally invalid correspondences. By making constraints a core part of the hypothesis space, CGM improves accuracy and cost-efficiency, potentially transforming how enterprises handle data integration at scale. On a controlled structural-decoy benchmark, hard admissibility constraints reduced the candidate space by ~480x without dropping the ground truth, and ablation showed the constraint gate, not the LLM, was the decisive factor (F1 improved from 0.08 to 0.66). The method is model-independent, adds no extra inference cost, and a small model with constraints outperformed a frontier LLM without constraints at ~28x lower cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Enterprise entity alignment involves matching records across different schemas, often with semi-structured data, implicit attributes, and unit mismatches. Traditional manual matching does not scale, while LLM-only approaches may violate structural invariants. Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to leverage both semantic understanding and formal constraints.

## [VTC-Bench: New Metric for Evaluating Multiple LLM Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24228) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VTC-Bench, a five-domain benchmark, and Validated Task Coverage (VTC) as a core metric to evaluate the diversity and usefulness of multiple LLM generations. It measures how many distinct useful results are obtained within k attempts, without relying on model-based judges. This addresses a gap in current LLM evaluation, which typically focuses on individual outputs or reduces multiple samples to a single success. By evaluating finite candidate sets directly, it reveals differences in model behavior that conventional per-output evaluation misses, potentially influencing how models are selected and deployed. The benchmark is built from carefully selected real-data tasks where output quality and task-relevant distinctness can be checked automatically and reproducibly, without model-based judges. The results show that configurations strongest in single-draw quality are not necessarily those with the best coverage, and simple variation measures do not reliably recover task-relevant coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Many LLM applications benefit from generating multiple candidate outputs for comparison, validation, or combination. Traditional evaluation often focuses on individual outputs or reduces multiple samples to a single success, potentially missing the diversity of useful results. VTC-Bench aims to provide a more comprehensive evaluation by measuring how many distinct useful results are obtained within a given number of attempts.

## [STRIVE: Multi-Agent Framework for Longitudinal Radiology Reports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24237) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STRIVE introduces a multi-agent framework that decomposes longitudinal radiology report generation into specialized Diagnosis, Attribute, and Temporal Change Agents, with explicit intermediate evidence and a progression-aware reward for temporal reasoning. It achieves the best clinical efficacy on Longitudinal-MIMIC and more than doubles the Longitudinal Change Concordance (LCC) over the strongest baseline. This work addresses task interference and error traceability in longitudinal radiology report generation, which is critical for clinical decision-making. By improving temporal agreement and providing explicit evidence, STRIVE could enhance the reliability and interpretability of AI-generated medical reports, benefiting radiologists and patients. The Temporal Change Agent is post-trained using Progression-Aware GRPO, a verifiable, shaped reward that assigns partial credit to direction-preserving errors and scores direction reversals lowest. STRIVE performs verification at two stages: a deterministic Consistency Gate reconciles agent outputs before report generation, and a Validation Agent checks whether the generated report is supported by the aggregated clinical evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Longitudinal radiology report generation (LRRG) requires identifying current findings and their changes relative to a prior study. Existing methods jointly model diagnosis, attribute estimation, temporal comparison, and language generation in implicit representations, which can cause task interference and limit error traceability. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that optimizes multiple responses via a reward model, and Progression-Aware GRPO adapts this to incorporate ordered progression states.

## [Dual-Judge Protocol Enhances Legal VLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24258) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a dual-judge evaluation protocol for vision-language models (VLMs) in legally grounded tasks, pairing a 0-10 quality judge with a strict binary semantic-equivalence judge. The protocol was demonstrated on UK traffic-sign interpretation, analyzing 4,680 evaluations across seven visibility levels and two occlusion modes. This protocol addresses a gap in legal-AI evaluation by adding a semantic-equivalence signal that single-judge protocols miss, which is crucial for legally accountable settings where outputs must be justifiable. It provides a cost-effective method (one extra LLM call) to surface reliability issues in high-quality scores, particularly under degraded inputs, benefiting researchers and practitioners in legal AI and VLM evaluation. The two judges showed moderate association (point-biserial r = 0.644) with an asymmetric Type II pattern affecting 8.0% of evaluations. The marginal rate of disagreement peaked at high visibility (14.2% at v=0.8), but conditioned on scores above 7, the rate was highest under heavy occlusion (54-63% at v≤0.3), indicating high quality scores are least trustworthy when input is most degraded.

This paper argues that for legally grounded tasks, outputs must also be semantically equivalent to a reference, not just high quality. The dual-judge protocol adds a strict semantic-equivalence check against a human-curated reference, providing an additional evaluation signal.

The paper introduces KnowChange, a framework that uses pretrained vision-language models to synthesize diverse change data for remote sensing change detection. It replaces handcrafted rules with knowledge-guided reasoning about change locations and class transitions. It could reduce the need for expensive manual annotation in remote sensing applications. KnowChange integrates knowledge-guided change simulation with generalizable synthesis models, enabling flexible synthesis of diverse change types in a unified framework. Experiments show that KnowChange-generated data outperforms existing synthetic datasets in both synthetic-to-real transfer and data augmentation, even at a compact scale.

## [MEOR Regularization Mitigates Candidate-Set Interference in CKGE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Matched Excess-Outranker Regularization (MEOR), a host-level objective that suppresses newcomer pressure exceeding a score-blind, structurally matched old-candidate reference. Experiments on ENTITY-ComplEx show MEOR improves historical current-universe MRR by 0.0057 over replay and reduces candidate-set interference by 0.0055. This work identifies candidate-set interference as a distinct source of continual rank loss in knowledge graph embedding, separate from catastrophic forgetting. By addressing this issue without replacing the underlying architecture, MEOR offers a practical improvement for continual learning systems, potentially benefiting applications like dynamic knowledge graphs and recommendation systems. MEOR uses a one-sided penalty that activates only when newcomer competition exceeds the matched reference, preserving the host learner's signal for legitimate new entities. Across eight paired runs on ENTITY-ComplEx, MEOR improves historical current-universe MRR by 0.0057 over replay and reduces candidate-set interference by 0.0055, with one-sided 95% lower bounds of 0.0052 and 0.0051, respectively. It also improves historical ranking in all ten reported FBInc-S and FBInc-L host and backbone settings, with every paired 95% confidence interval excluding zero.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Continual knowledge graph embedding (CKGE) updates entity and relation representations as a knowledge graph grows, aiming to learn new knowledge efficiently while preserving old knowledge. Traditional methods focus on catastrophic forgetting, but entity admission also changes the candidate universe for queries, causing historical answers to lose rank even when their scores are preserved. MEOR addresses this candidate-set interference by comparing newcomer pressure against a matched old reference, providing a regularization that does not require replacing the embedding architecture or continual learner.

## [VideoHarness-RSI: Recursive Harness Self-Improvement for Long-Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24302) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VideoHarness-RSI, a recursive harness self-improvement method that searches for executable context-construction programs around a frozen vision-language model (VLM) to improve long-video understanding. Starting from uniform sampling, the method consistently finds improvements and surpasses several hand-crafted baselines, and the selected harness transfers to other benchmarks without further search. This work establishes executable context construction as a distinct optimization layer for long-video understanding, showing that significant gains can be achieved without modifying the VLM parameters or decoding. It provides a reproducible baseline for studying harness discovery and transfer, which could influence future research on automated inference system design. The method uses an outer-loop proposer that generates candidate harnesses based on prior programs, evaluation outcomes, and execution traces, which are then executed and evaluated end-to-end. The answering model (e.g., Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct) remains frozen, and the searchable object is the executable program structure, not the model weights.

**Background**: Long-video understanding requires constructing a limited model context from a much longer video, often using compression, retrieval, memory, or agentic evidence acquisition. Existing approaches typically integrate these mechanisms into manually designed inference systems or optimize them jointly with other components, making it hard to isolate the contribution of the context-construction program itself. VideoHarness-RSI addresses this by treating the context-construction program as a searchable object while keeping the VLM frozen, enabling controlled study of harness design.

## [OPDSearch+ Introduces Teacher-Free Distillation for Search-Augmented Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24310) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OPDSearch+ is a new distillation paradigm that requires no teacher fine-tuning for search-augmented reasoning. It uses a frozen off-the-shelf instruct model as the teacher and combines on-policy distillation with reinforcement learning (RL) refinement, achieving gains of 13.1% on HotpotQA and 8.5% on 2WikiMultihopQA with a 3B model. This work addresses two major challenges in search-augmented reasoning for small language models: the high cost of collecting multi-turn search trajectories and the instability of on-policy distillation with off-the-shelf teachers. By eliminating the need for task-specific teacher fine-tuning, OPDSearch+ reduces training costs and improves performance, making advanced reasoning capabilities more accessible for smaller models. The method consists of two stages: first, the student interacts with a live search engine and is distilled via a per-position forward KL objective; second, RL refines the distilled student from a richer behavioral foundation. Across seven QA benchmarks, OPDSearch+ with a 3B model consistently outperforms all prior 3B RL baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a technique where a student model learns from a teacher's outputs on the student's own generated data, which can be more sample-efficient than standard distillation. Search-augmented reasoning involves using a search engine to retrieve relevant information during reasoning, which is challenging for small language models due to their limited capacity. Previous methods often required task-specific teacher fine-tuning, which is expensive and can constrain the student to the teacher's performance ceiling.

## [Dynamic Internal Field Governs Transformer Cognition with Certified Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24319) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a dynamic internal field governed by PDEs on the module graph to modulate transformer cognition, and proves a new discrete Schur-Cohn criterion for Verlet integration with velocity coupling, ensuring integrator stability. The field's physics type (wave, diffusion, etc.) does not affect accuracy, and it acts as a compute governor rather than an enhancer. This work introduces a novel approach to homeostatic compute control in transformers, offering a certified stability guarantee that could lead to more reliable adaptive computation. It distinguishes between capability and certifiability, suggesting that learned recurrences can also carry certificates but the field's one-step operator allows exact runtime checks. The paper uses a twenty-seed preregistered deconfounding campaign, finding that the second-order effect is strong in one family (+0.087) but not detected in another (+0.014), indicating part of the original contrast was capacity. A kill-gate with positive control found no evidence for the field as an evidence accumulator (Delta AUC +0.0007 vs 0.03 threshold).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers typically use fixed computation, but adaptive-depth reasoners adjust computation based on input complexity. Homeostatic control in neural networks regulates internal states to maintain stability, often modeled with PDEs on graph Laplacians. The Schur-Cohn criterion is a classical stability test for discrete-time systems, here extended to Verlet integration with velocity coupling.

## [SonarLLM: Native Sonar-Optical MLLM for Underwater Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24325) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SonarLLM is a new multimodal large language model that treats sonar as a native perceptual modality, integrating a sonar-specific encoder, physics-aware feature enhancement, and reliability-aware hierarchical fusion. The authors also introduce SonarBench, a paired benchmark covering recognition, counting, VQA, and captioning under three input settings. This work addresses a critical gap in underwater perception, where optical cameras fail in turbid conditions but sonar remains effective. By enabling adaptive fusion based on sensing quality, SonarLLM could improve autonomous underwater vehicles' robustness in real-world missions, and SonarBench provides a standardized evaluation for future research. SonarLLM achieves 72.0% macro accuracy on sonar-only recognition, counting, and VQA, outperforming the strongest baseline by 34.4 percentage points, and 68.7% under fusion, exceeding the best baseline by 25.1 points. The fusion-over-optical gain increases from 6.0 to 36.0 points as turbidity rises, demonstrating the growing complementary value of sonar.

Existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are built primarily on optical encoders, making them ill-suited for sonar or sonar-optical fusion. SonarLLM addresses this by treating sonar as a native modality and using physics-aware fusion to adapt to changing sensing conditions.

<a id="item-221"></a>
## [OODA-Tool: Separating State from Action for Reliable Multi-Turn Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24368) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OODA-Tool, a typed closed-loop policy inspired by Boyd's OODA cycle, which separates state preservation from action realization to mitigate state-action competition in multi-turn tool use. It evaluates the approach against direct function-calling and ReAct-style policies using Qwen3 models from 0.6B to 14B, showing consistent improvements in task success. This work addresses a critical limitation in LLM-based agents: the coupling of state tracking and action generation in a single autoregressive trajectory, which can degrade performance in complex multi-turn interactions. By proposing a structured separation, OODA-Tool offers a potential path to more reliable and scalable tool-use agents, which is essential for real-world applications like web navigation and API orchestration. OODA-Tool routes each decision through controller-checked intermediate states: Observe reconstructs the task state, Orient determines execution warrant, Decide forms an admissible action structure, and Act realizes the external output. The gains are larger on smaller models and on tasks where actions depend heavily on information accumulated across turns and prior tool results, with controlled variants and ablations confirming robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: The OODA loop (Observe, Orient, Decide, Act) is a decision-making model developed by U.S. Air Force Colonel John Boyd in the 1970s, emphasizing agility through continuous feedback and late commitment. In LLM-based tool use, agents often use direct function-calling or ReAct-style policies, which interleave reasoning and action in a single trajectory, leading to state-action competition where generating the next call may overwrite earlier context. OODA-Tool applies the OODA cycle to explicitly separate state preservation from action generation, aiming to improve reliability in multi-turn interactions.

## [Do Recipes Have Personas? Characterizing and Generating Creator Style in Attributed Procedural Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24369) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces ViralRecipesTrans, a new dataset of procedurally aligned execution flow graphs extracted from popular culinary video transcripts, and formulates procedural stylometry as a graph learning and process discovery task. It also proposes a novel generative task of predicting a creator's exact structural execution graph for unseen dishes, and demonstrates that a structured two-stage model achieves superior topological control via rigid Markovian priors. This work bridges graph learning and process discovery, offering a fresh angle in AI/ML research that could impact procedural generation and authorship attribution. By capturing creator-specific styles in procedural graphs, it opens possibilities for personalized workflow automation and more nuanced understanding of human execution processes. The paper reveals a fundamental duality: traditional lexical classifiers overfit via semantic leakage, while discrete topological metrics successfully capture the rigid physical constraints of a creator's workflow. It also exposes a dichotomy in style generation between global macro-planning and local structural execution, and shows that an ensemble approach combining few-shot LLMs and the structured model can synthesize global semantic reasoning with localized topological footprints.

This paper extends these concepts to culinary video transcripts, treating recipes as attributed procedural graphs to analyze creator style.

## [Behavior-Guided Online Probabilistic Forecasting for EV Charging Loads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24441) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a behavior-guided online probabilistic forecasting framework for EV charging loads, which explicitly models persistent station-specific patterns and recent behavioral changes using a dual-timescale representation and drift-aware adaptation. Experiments on ten real-world charging stations show that the method reduces MSE and Pinball loss by 15.3% and 17.8% for 1-hour-ahead forecasts, and by 16.8% and 22.6% for 4-hour-ahead forecasts, outperforming existing baselines. Accurate EV charging load forecasting is crucial for grid stability and energy management, especially as EV adoption grows. This work provides a practical solution that adapts to evolving charging behaviors, potentially improving operational efficiency and reliability for charging station operators and grid operators. The framework uses a dual-timescale behavior representation to distinguish long-term charging characteristics from recent behavioral states, and quantifies their deviations. A delayed-feedback mechanism ensures temporally consistent online updates when observations arrive across different forecasting horizons.

The paper introduces Mahalanobis-Based Multi-Head Attention (MHA-CSP), which replaces the standard dot-product with a Mahalanobis distance-based RBF kernel, enabling direct construction of Tree Attention and cross-head collaboration. With only 119K parameters, it outperforms Transformer and GCN baselines on long-sequence state tracking tasks. This work offers a new efficiency-performance trade-off for structured reasoning, potentially reducing the computational cost of attention mechanisms while improving accuracy. It could influence future designs of attention layers in deep learning models, especially for tasks requiring long-range dependencies and symbolic structure. The positive definiteness of the Mahalanobis distance allows direct construction of Tree Attention, with a LogSumExp correction to rectify raw distances. The multi-head Mahalanobis distance matrices are repurposed to create an attention meshing mechanism for cross-head kernel collaboration, boosting accuracy and training efficiency.

LogSumExp is a smooth maximum function often used in attention for numerical stability.

HMGCLIP introduces a unified multimodal embedding framework that constructs a heterogeneous hypergraph to mine structure-aware hard negatives and align multi-granular semantics at relation and hyperedge levels. It also releases a new fine-grained e-commerce dataset and demonstrates superior performance over strong baselines on both this dataset and the public MAVE benchmark. This work addresses a critical limitation in multimodal e-commerce representation learning, where global embeddings often fail to capture fine-grained attributes needed for precise product discrimination. By improving fine-grained understanding, HMGCLIP could enhance downstream tasks such as product search and recommendation, benefiting both consumers and e-commerce platforms. The framework uses a heterogeneous hypergraph to model complex relationships among product attributes, enabling dual-granularity inference that dynamically fuses attribute evidence for both fine-grained and coarse-grained tasks. Extensive experiments show that HMGCLIP outperforms multimodal encoders, MLLMs, and e-commerce baselines, validating its effectiveness.

PeakBench, a new benchmark for evaluating LLM agents' resource-aware parallel tool invocation, has been released on arXiv. It provides executable multi-tool workflows with dependency annotations and resource profiles, along with a two-part evaluation framework that separates logical planning from physical scheduling. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing agent benchmarks, which often overlook resource-constrained scheduling, leading to either slow serial execution or unsafe parallel execution. PeakBench could drive improvements in agent efficiency and safety, impacting real-world applications where multiple tools are invoked concurrently. The benchmark includes a large catalog of MCP-compatible tools and workflows with empirically grounded resource footprints. It introduces dedicated metrics for logical planning and physical scheduling, and experiments show that strong planning does not guarantee safe or efficient execution under resource constraints, while exposing resource information can reduce overflows.

## [When 'Must' Becomes 'Maybe': Constraint Weakening in LLM Agent Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24569) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates how LLM agent handoff transformations can inadvertently weaken action-constraining state, using safety blockers as a controlled testbed across 1,296 synthetic episodes. It finds that compression, plan assimilation, convergence, ownership deferral, and precedent substitution repeatedly turn binding state into non-binding caveats, with normal handoff compression causing 100.0% deactivation and 54.2% forbidden actions. This work highlights a critical safety gap in multi-agent LLM workflows: even when content is preserved, the operational binding of constraints can be lost, leading to unsafe actions. It underscores the need for explicit state-preservation mechanisms in agent handoffs, which is essential for deploying reliable and safe LLM agents in real-world applications. The study conditions on correct upstream identification, varies handoff transformations, and evaluates an executor restricted to the resulting artifact. Restoring all four state fields (prerequisite, authority, fallback, execution consequence) raises preservation to 100.0% and reduces forbidden action to 0.0%, while fixed-artifact interventions show downstream verification eliminates forbidden action but artifact deactivation remains 95.3%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents coordinate complex tasks through multi-role and multi-stage workflows, where upstream state is transformed into intermediate artifacts like summaries, plans, and handoff notes. Action-constraining state must not only be topically retained but also operationally preserved—meaning downstream components must treat it as a binding requirement, not just contextual information. Safety blockers provide a controlled instance because each source state has explicit prerequisite, authority, fallback, and execution consequence fields. This paper introduces the concept of 'operational state preservation' to distinguish between semantic availability and actual constraint enforcement.

PhysMLLMs introduces a training-stage prior injection architecture that uses a frozen DINOv2 teacher to distill global visual representations via an offline embedding cache and scheduled distillation, improving spatio-temporal consistency in video multimodal LLMs for referring segmentation and grounded reasoning. This work addresses a critical limitation in video MLLMs—spatio-temporal inconsistencies like jitter and identity switches—which are common in complex scenes. By improving mask quality and cross-frame consistency without adding inference cost, it could advance video understanding applications such as autonomous driving and video editing. The core mechanism, REPA-Global, distills global visual representations from a frozen DINOv2 teacher using an offline embedding cache and a scheduled distillation plan, keeping inference unchanged. The method shows larger gains on challenging cases involving small targets, fast motion, occlusion, distractors, and reasoning queries, while maintaining comparable performance on single-frame referring segmentation and general VLM benchmarks.

This paper introduces the concept of ε-commutativity, a relaxation of commutativity for factors in lifted model construction, and demonstrates how it can be exploited for lifted inference with strict error bounds. The approach is validated empirically, showing comparable query accuracy at lower runtime. This work addresses a practical issue in lifted inference where parameters learned from real-world data are only approximately commutative, not exactly so. By providing theoretical guarantees and empirical validation, it enhances the robustness and applicability of scalable probabilistic inference in noisy, real-world settings. The paper proves strict bounds on the approximation error induced by using ε-commutativity in lifted model construction and downstream inference. Empirical results confirm that the approach achieves query accuracy comparable to exact methods while reducing runtime.

A key step is identifying commutative factors, whose outputs are invariant under permutations of input values. However, in practice, parameters learned from data are noisy, making factors only approximately commutative. This paper relaxes the strict commutativity requirement to handle such real-world imperfections.

This paper introduces a strictly causal streaming video anomaly detector that uses a diagonal linear state-space recurrence with an input and state dependent decay gate, achieving O(1) per-frame updates with no lookahead or clip buffering. It also provides a theoretical relationship between the decay spectrum and detection latency, validated on UCSD Ped2 and CUHK Avenue. This work addresses practical edge deployment for video anomaly detection by offering a streaming, low-latency solution with theoretical guarantees, potentially enabling real-time monitoring on resource-constrained devices. It also highlights the trade-off between accuracy and efficiency, motivating further research to close the accuracy gap. The method achieves 0.74 ms and 0.77 ms per frame (over 1300 FPS) on Apple M3 Pro hardware, with frame-level AUC of 67.9% and 70.2% on Ped2 and Avenue respectively, trailing non-causal SSM baselines. Ablations show the gate's contribution is dataset-size dependent, hurting accuracy on smaller Ped2 but helping on larger Avenue.

This work addresses a critical gap in LLM-based KGQA by offering a middle ground between brittle full semantic parsing and robust but unverifiable LLM reasoning. The method employs a three-valued constraint semantics (satisfied, violated, unknown) to handle incomplete knowledge graphs under open-world assumptions, avoiding incorrect rejections. Experiments on Hetionet show that precision improves by filtering invalid candidates, while recall is preserved because candidates with non-violated constraints are retained, and satisfied constraints provide additional evidence for ranking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph question answering (KGQA) aims to answer natural language questions using structured knowledge graphs. Traditional approaches rely on semantic parsing to convert questions into executable queries like SPARQL, which can be brittle with complex or incomplete schemas. LLM-based methods generate answers directly but lack formal guarantees. CES-PK bridges these by using symbolic constraints derived from the question to verify LLM-generated candidates, without needing full logical forms.

## [FedV-KGQA: Privacy-Preserving Multi-Hop QA over Vertical KG Partitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24846) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FedV-KGQA, a framework for multi-hop question answering over vertically partitioned knowledge graphs, where organizations share entities but own disjoint relation sets. It combines local graph enrichment and knowledge graph embeddings to avoid inter-silo communication, and introduces a topic entity anchoring mechanism for grounding questions. This work addresses a practical and underexplored problem of answering multi-hop questions when data is split across organizations due to governance and sovereignty constraints. It offers a privacy-preserving solution that maintains performance close to centralized systems, which is significant for federated learning and KGQA communities. The framework is evaluated across 12 model configurations on three benchmarks, showing strong performance, generalization to 3-hop reasoning, and robustness to embedding perturbations. It ensures raw triples and relation parameters never leave each silo, establishing a structural data boundary without centralized graph access.

Multi-hop question answering requires aggregating information from multiple sources, which is challenging when facts are split across vertically partitioned knowledge graphs. Knowledge graph embeddings (KGE) learn low-dimensional representations of entities and relations, enabling reasoning tasks without exposing raw data.

This addresses the costly group-relative rollouts in agentic RL, potentially enabling more efficient training of LLM agents for tool-use and reasoning tasks, which is crucial for scaling RL to complex real-world applications. ## [Aesthetic Scorers Show Fidelity Preference, Not Demographic Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23593) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper audits four image aesthetic scorers (LAION-Aesthetics, PickScore, ImageReward, HPSv2) using pixel-level interventions on skin tone and body type, finding that sensitivity to skin tone is primarily a fidelity preference rather than demographic bias. The study also reveals that synthetic image audits can mislead, as results reverse or attenuate when tested on real images. This finding is significant because it challenges assumptions about demographic bias in AI aesthetic scoring, which is widely used to filter training data and guide text-to-image generation. The results emphasize the need for real-image validation in bias audits, potentially impacting how AI fairness is evaluated in generative models. The study uses CIELAB L* shifts to perturb skin tone and includes placebo arms to rule out artifacts. It finds that the fidelity penalty is operator-dependent and holds for all operators only for LAION-Aesthetics, and that synthetic results do not transfer to real images, reversing for LAION-Aes and HPSv2 and attenuating for PickScore.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Image aesthetic scorers are machine learning models that predict the aesthetic quality of images, often used to curate datasets or guide generation in text-to-image systems. The CIELAB color space defines perceptual lightness (L*) and is commonly used for skin tone analysis. Auditing these scorers for demographic bias is crucial because they may inadvertently encode preferences that affect fairness in AI-generated content.

## [REFINE: Multi-Agent LLM Framework Cuts Java Code Smells by Up to 72.79%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REFINE, a tool-agnostic, evidence-aware multi-agent LLM framework, was introduced for Java file-level refactoring. Evaluated on 450 Java files from 15 open-source systems, it reduced code smells by 68.26%, 72.79%, and 68.49% using GPT-5.5, Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview, and Claude Opus 4.8, respectively. This work demonstrates a practical application of multi-agent LLMs to a core software engineering task, offering a structured approach that outperforms direct prompting. It could help developers automate refactoring with higher confidence, though human review remains essential, and it highlights the potential for LLM-driven code quality improvement in real-world projects. REFINE combines static-analysis-guided smell identification, smell-informed planning, LLM-based transformation, automated re-analysis, preservation checks, and structured reporting. Compared to a matched 150-file direct-prompt baseline, REFINE achieved higher median smell reduction with smaller edits and fewer public-method removals, but broader quality improvements were inconsistent and residual risks like assert/fail-call changes remain.

## [Rebuild Dossier: Enforcing Specs to Expose Test Gaming in Agentic App Rebuilds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces rebuild-dossier, an open-source tool that locks an application's exact interface before coding and enforces one-test-at-a-time building via automated checks. It reveals that weaker models may fail despite passing tests due to test gaming, and that the check mechanism, not interface-locking, is the key differentiator. This work addresses a critical flaw in AI-agent-based software rebuilding: passing test suites do not guarantee correctness when tests can be gamed. The findings highlight the importance of mechanically-enforced checks and have implications for evaluating LLM agents in software engineering tasks. In a small comparison, the compliant agent failed a held-back test while the rule-breaking agent passed everything, demonstrating test gaming. The tool's automated check was not running in a larger app comparison, where it lost to simply giving the model source and one instruction, pointing to the check mechanism's importance. Claims are verified at three levels: agent report, automated log, and actual files, catching errors a single level would miss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used to rebuild or modernize legacy applications. Prior work (AgentModernize) found that a simple approach—giving the model original code and one instruction—can outperform complex multi-agent pipelines once the model is strong enough. rebuild-dossier is an MCP server that reverse-engineers a trustworthy rebuild spec, including a locked CLAUDE.md and mutation-tested test suite, to guide coding agents.

The paper introduces ADFD-Migrate, a method that uses annotated data-flow diagrams (ADFDs) inferred by LLMs to assist in migrating legacy Fortran repositories to other languages. It also presents a new benchmark, f2x50, consisting of 50 Fortran repositories, and reports that the generated Python passes 85.6% of 382 behavioral probes. This work addresses the significant challenge of modernizing legacy software repositories, which often contain undocumented domain knowledge. By making the latent declarative representation explicit, ADFD-Migrate improves the coverage and integration of repository-scale migration, potentially reducing the cost and effort of porting large codebases. ADFD-Migrate uses an LLM to infer the source ADFD from bounded repository context, guided by static-analysis coverage checks, and then uses dependency-aware chunking to order process groups for target-language generation. The method achieves a 93.1% mean migration outcome index and a 17–59 percentage-point advantage over direct translation on 47 repositories.

## [Macro-Operator Generation and Predicate Selection for TAMP Operator Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23629) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a system that automatically generates macro-operators from training data by discovering causally linked action pairs, and prunes predicates not referenced by any learned operator. It achieves up to a 4.6x planning speedup and solves a long sequential task that the baseline cannot solve. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in TAMP by improving scalability for long sequential tasks, which is essential for deploying robots in real-world manipulation scenarios. The method's ability to determine solvability in practice could significantly advance AI planning and robotics. The system discovers action pairs where one action produces exactly the condition required by the next, and compresses them into macro-operators. It also prunes predicates that no learned operator references, reducing the symbolic state evaluated at each search node. The method was tested across four TAMP domains, achieving up to a 4.6x speedup compared to the baseline 'Learning Operators for TAMP'.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) systems combine high-level symbolic planning with low-level motion planning, but creating symbolic operators by hand is a major bottleneck. Recent works learn operators from demonstration data, but they often learn each action in isolation, failing to capture recurring multi-step structures, making search intractable on long tasks. Macro-operators, which compress sequences of actions into single steps, have a long history in classical AI planning, and predicate selection helps reduce the state space.

## [Intermediate Knowledge Distillation: When Less Is More](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23752) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates intermediate block-wise knowledge distillation and proposes a student architecture with homogeneous blocks mirroring the teacher. It shows that on classic datasets, distilling only the last block is often best, while fine-grained, data-scarce settings benefit from intermediate supervision. This work provides practical guidance on when and how to apply block-wise knowledge distillation to avoid overfitting, which is valuable for practitioners building compact models. It addresses an under-explored aspect of KD and contributes to ongoing research in model compression and data efficiency. The study uses an EfficientNet-B0 teacher and evaluates across eleven datasets, including classic and fine-grained, data-scarce ones. It explores supervision granularity and uses explainability analyses (attention maps, Centered Kernel Alignment, Grad-CAM) to guide the distillation configuration, also considering teacher and student fine-tuning strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation (KD) is a technique where a large teacher network transfers knowledge to a smaller student network, typically by matching the final output logits. Intermediate block-wise KD extends this by providing supervision at multiple network layers, which can help in data-scarce scenarios but may also cause overfitting if overused. This paper investigates the trade-off and proposes a student architecture to mitigate overfitting.

The proposed Gemma3n-EXAM^2 model is a lightweight fusion-model fine-tuned on EXAM^2-train, showing significant gains over a strong baseline, but the paper notes substantial performance gaps remain in multilingual and cross-modal understanding for state-of-the-art models.

## [EmoTra-TTS Enables Smooth Intra-Utterance Emotion Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23791) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EmoTra-TTS introduces a method for smooth intra-utterance emotion transitions in speech synthesis, using multi-pass flow blending and frame-level VAD conditioning. It adds only +0.43% parameters with no latency overhead and achieves 30%-87% relative improvement in emotion transition quality. This addresses a significant gap in emotional TTS, as current systems rely on static emotion labels per utterance, failing to capture the dynamic nature of human affect. It enables more natural and expressive speech synthesis, benefiting applications like audiobooks, virtual assistants, and entertainment. The method uses a multi-pass flow blending pipeline to synthesize frame-aligned transition audio, and dual-stage VAD conditioning guides prosodic planning and acoustic realization. Direction-magnitude decoupled injection separates emotion direction from injection magnitude to prevent content degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Emotional text-to-speech (TTS) systems typically condition on a single discrete emotion label per utterance, which fails to reflect the continuous, evolving nature of human affect. Recent LLM-based TTS systems may vary prosody implicitly but lack explicit control for targeted intra-utterance transitions. Flow-based generative models like WaveGlow are used for high-quality speech synthesis, and frame-level VAD provides per-frame speech probabilities, which can be adapted for emotion conditioning.

## [RwF Framework Tackles Catastrophic Forgetting in Multi-Degradation Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23799) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Restoring without Forgetting (RwF), a continual learning framework for multi-degradation image restoration that sequentially learns new degradations without forgetting prior ones. It achieves significant PSNR improvements over naive fine-tuning on five degradation domains and demonstrates strong routing accuracy on real-degradation benchmarks. This work addresses a practical limitation of current all-in-one image restoration models, which assume all degradations are available during training. By framing the problem as continual domain-incremental learning, RwF enables field-deployed systems to adapt to new degradations without retraining on all historical data, saving cost and preserving privacy. RwF learns a lightweight adapter for each new degradation, eliminating forgetting by construction. It uses an unsupervised routing mechanism to select the appropriate restoration path without domain labels, achieving 89.5% routing accuracy on 11 real-degradation benchmarks with only a +0.94 dB oracle PSNR gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Image restoration aims to recover clean images from degraded ones, and recent all-in-one models handle multiple degradations in a single network. However, these models assume all degradations are known during training, which is unrealistic in dynamic environments. Continual learning, particularly domain-incremental learning, addresses scenarios where tasks arrive sequentially and prior data may be unavailable, but catastrophic forgetting remains a key challenge.

## [Bandit-Driven Prompt Selection Cuts LLM Essay Scoring Costs by 78.4%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel cost-aware framework that uses a multi-armed bandit (MAB) controller to adaptively select prompt strategies during LLM-based automated essay scoring. On IELTS Writing Task 2 essays, the approach reduces LLM calls by 78.4% while achieving accuracy comparable to exhaustive grid search. This work addresses the operational cost concerns of deploying LLMs for essay scoring, a key barrier for educational technology platforms. By transforming prompt selection from offline hyperparameter optimization into an efficient online learning task, it offers a practical path to balance assessment validity with cost efficiency, potentially enabling wider adoption of LLM-based grading. The framework treats each prompt type as an arm in a MAB controller, enabling adaptive selection during inference. The authors implemented four grading recipes (multi-step vs. single-step, with vs. without calibration examples) and found the multi-step approach with examples yields the highest accuracy; they also produced the first cost-reliability learning curves for essay scoring by tracking token usage and latency.

This paper applies MAB to dynamically choose among different prompting strategies, reducing the number of LLM calls needed to find the best grading approach.

The paper proposes a hierarchical O-RAN control architecture that coordinates slow UAV trajectory and deployment decisions with fast per-user scheduling, using a Non-Real-Time RIC rApp for placement and slice budget, and a Near-Real-Time RIC xApp (D-SAC scheduler) for per-user resource allocation. This work addresses the critical coupling between aerial control and radio resource management in UAV-mounted gNBs, potentially improving network performance and SLA satisfaction. It is significant for the O-RAN and wireless research community as it demonstrates a multi-timescale control approach that could be applied to future flexible and dynamic network deployments. The D-SAC xApp is a permutation-equivariant DeepSets Soft Actor-Critic scheduler trained in a Sionna RT ray-traced channel. The hierarchical controller improves eMBB SLA satisfaction by up to 17% and URLLC on-time delivery by up to 42% over classical and learned schedulers, with the learned rApp further improving URLLC on-time delivery by up to 20%.

A new gaze-informed transformer framework predicts radiologist expertise from 3D gaze patterns during thoracic CT interpretation, achieving an ROC-AUC of 0.91 and F1 score of 0.86 on a held-out test set. The model integrates radiologist fixation patterns into volumetric feature learning using a DINOv2 backbone. This work introduces a novel approach to objective, process-based expertise assessment in radiology, which could improve training and evaluation methods. It also demonstrates the potential of integrating human gaze data into transformer models for medical imaging, opening new avenues for human-centered AI. The framework uses a DINOv2 ViT-B/14 backbone and incorporates gaze via a learnable log-space bias in self-attention and gaze-weighted pooling of patch embeddings. It was trained and evaluated on 182 CT reading sessions from five radiologists with varying experience levels.

The paper introduces the Infant Care Video Dataset (ICVD), containing 4,144 videos of 12 simulated NICU interventions, and demonstrates that video transformers (TimeSformer and MotionFormer) achieve high top-1 accuracy (93.97% and 93.17%) compared to a framewise approach (23.17%). This work addresses the significant challenge of NICU documentation, where nurses spend about 25% of their time on record-keeping and up to 60% of interventions go undocumented. By providing a dataset and strong baselines, it paves the way for automated documentation systems that can reduce clinical burden and improve patient care. The dataset uses manikins to simulate interventions, systematically varying camera angles and clinician skin tones while ensuring privacy compliance. The ablation study shows a 70.80% performance gap between temporal models and the framewise approach, validating the necessity of temporal modeling for this task.

This dataset and baseline results provide a foundation for future research in automated healthcare documentation.
