## [Resilience Metrics for Embodied Agents: New Framework and Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23839) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first comprehensive resilience metrics suite for Embodied Agents Systems (EAS), defining Rebound, Stability, and Graceful Extensibility, and implements a resilience evaluation layer for diagnosis and optimization. The framework is validated across 400 household tasks with 10 EAS, revealing process-level distinctions hidden by outcome metrics. This work addresses a critical gap in EAS evaluation by shifting focus from outcome-centric metrics to process-level resilience, which is essential for reliable deployment in open-world environments. It provides a practical framework that can improve human-agent trust and guide optimization, impacting AI safety and systems research. The study found significant recovery cost differences among successful episodes (ΔC_rec=25.2), increased instability, and task-family degradation. Metrics-guided optimizations reduced recovery cost and improved stability and graceful extensibility, but revealed a trade-off among resilience characteristics, suggesting deployment-specific configuration is needed.

**Background**: Embodied Agents Systems (EAS) are AI systems that interact with physical environments, such as robots performing household tasks. Resilience engineering, a concept from systems engineering, emphasizes a system's ability to recover and adapt under stress, which this paper adapts to EAS.

## [RefineRank: Joint Box Refinement and Ranking for Surgical STG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23928) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RefineRank introduces a trainable module, RefineNet, that refines and ranks candidate boxes from a frozen open-set detector using language features from a frozen medical vision language model, achieving the highest STG mIoU of 0.421 on MedVidBench. This work addresses a critical trade-off in surgical spatio-temporal grounding between question understanding and precise localization, potentially improving automated surgical assistance and training. It demonstrates that a small box-level module can reconcile these capabilities without retraining large backbones, offering a practical path for integrating VLMs with detectors in specialized domains. In controlled evaluations, coordinate correction raised the candidate oracle upper bound from 0.6772 to 0.7302, and ranking the joint pool of original and refined candidates improved STG mIoU from 0.2719 to 0.4534, outperforming separately trained selectors (max 0.4186). The method uses a fixed decoding rule that returns the highest-scoring original or refined box, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Surgical spatio-temporal grounding (STG) is a task that requires locating the object a procedural question refers to at a specific video time. Existing methods face a trade-off: vision language models (VLMs) understand context but produce imprecise coordinates, while open-set detectors generate localized candidate boxes but their confidence scores do not reflect which box answers the question. MedVidBench is a benchmark for evaluating medical video understanding, including STG tasks.

## [QML Boosts NV-Center Magnetometry Under Information Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23934) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper (arXiv:2608.23934) investigates the use of quantum kernel methods for magnetic field estimation in NV center-based quantum sensors, comparing them with classical machine learning models. The study demonstrates that QML models trained on coherent quantum states significantly improve sensing performance, providing a theoretical upper bound that is only achievable with direct access to coherent quantum information. This work addresses a critical challenge in NISQ-era quantum sensing: extracting reliable information from noisy, finite-shot measurements. By showing that QML can mitigate measurement-induced information loss, it highlights the potential of tightly integrating quantum sensors with quantum machine learning, which could lead to more accurate and robust quantum sensors in practical applications. The study formulates magnetic field sensing as a supervised regression task and compares classical models trained on measurement-based data with quantum kernel models trained on pre-measurement coherent quantum states. The results indicate that QML performance improves significantly with coherent quantum-state information, but not with increased model complexity or changes in learning paradigm.

Quantum machine learning (QML) combines quantum computing with machine learning, and quantum kernel methods use quantum feature maps to compute kernels that classical computers cannot efficiently evaluate. In the NISQ era, quantum sensors face challenges from noise and limited measurement shots, leading to information loss that can degrade parameter estimation.

## [STAIN-FL: Stealthy Backdoor Attack with Contextual Triggers in Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23952) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STAIN-FL introduces a stealthy targeted backdoor attack framework for federated video anomaly detection, using naturally occurring surveillance conditions (low-light, indoor, crowd density) as contextual triggers. It combines anomaly-to-benign label manipulation with gradient masking over least-updated coordinates, achieving low-detectability sparse attacks on UCF-Crime. This work highlights a realistic security threat in federated learning for surveillance, where compromised clients can inject persistent backdoors with minimal impact on clean accuracy. It underscores the need for robust defense mechanisms in distributed video anomaly detection systems. In a non-IID four-client multi-agency setting with I3D features, sparse attacks kept clean accuracy drop below 2% while misclassifying over half of triggered anomalies, with peak backdoor accuracy of 56.7% under FedAvg and 54.2% under FedProx. The backdoor remained above the 25% threshold for an average of 336 post-attack rounds under FedAvg, indicating persistence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning enables collaborative model training without sharing raw data, but it is vulnerable to backdoor attacks where malicious clients inject poisoned updates. Backdoor attacks typically use specific triggers; contextual triggers are naturally occurring conditions that can activate the backdoor without explicit patterns. Gradient masking is a technique that selectively suppresses or rescales gradients to hide malicious updates from aggregation.

## [SCOUT: Hybrid Tool Discovery Cuts MCP Context Use by 99%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23992) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SCOUT, a system for selective tool context optimization in enterprise MCP gateways, which uses hybrid retrieval (BM25 + dense vectors) to inject only relevant tools. In production at PayPal, SCOUT reduced MCP tool-token consumption from 140.2k tokens (70.1% of context) to 1.3k tokens (0.8%), a 99% reduction. This addresses two critical challenges in enterprise LLM deployments: context saturation and tool discoverability, which become acute when managing thousands of tools. By reducing context usage and improving tool selection, SCOUT can significantly cut inference costs and improve agent accuracy, making MCP more viable at scale. SCOUT surfaces two MCP meta-tools, tool_search and execute_tool, where tool_search performs hybrid retrieval using Reciprocal Rank Fusion to return top-k relevant tools. It is backed by zero-downtime catalog update pipelines and is model-agnostic, requiring no client-side modifications.

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 to standardize how LLMs integrate with external tools and data sources. In enterprise settings, a proxy MCP server aggregates many backend servers, but this can saturate the model's context window with tool schemas and make it hard to find the right tool among thousands. Prompt caching helps reduce reprocessing costs but does not free context capacity or improve accuracy.

## [SAGE: Evidence-Grounded Multi-Agent Framework for Ancient Chinese Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24011) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SAGE is a new multi-agent framework that reformulates Chinese ancient document understanding as evidence-grounded inference instead of direct answer generation. It coordinates specialized agents for task-aware planning, tool-mediated evidence acquisition, claim-level verification, and bounded replanning, and outperforms direct-answering baselines on the AncientDoc benchmark across three LVLM backbones. This work addresses a critical limitation of current LVLMs—opaque, single-pass generation that often yields overconfident and weakly grounded responses. By demonstrating that a smaller model (Qwen3.5-9B) with SAGE can surpass much larger monolithic LVLMs, it highlights the importance of structured, evidence-grounded inference over mere model scaling, potentially influencing future designs for document understanding and other complex reasoning tasks. SAGE operates under a constrained shared-state runtime, supporting bounded evidence seeking, answer revision, and abstention when grounding is insufficient. Experiments on the AncientDoc benchmark show consistent improvements over matched direct-answering baselines across three LVLM backbones, with SAGE using Qwen3.5-9B surpassing much larger monolithic LVLMs on most metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Chinese ancient documents present unique challenges due to their visual complexity, linguistic variety, and need for historical reasoning. Current Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) typically generate answers in a single pass, which can lead to hallucinations and weak grounding. The AncientDoc benchmark is a comprehensive multi-task benchmark designed to evaluate VLMs on Chinese ancient documents, covering tasks from OCR to knowledge reasoning. SAGE introduces a multi-agent approach that decomposes the task into planning, evidence gathering, verification, and replanning, aiming to improve accuracy and reliability.

## [ChorusTIC: Training-Free Multivariate Time Series Classification via In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24033) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ChorusTIC introduces a training-free foundation model for multivariate time series classification that uses in-context learning to handle heterogeneous channel configurations without parameter updates. It is pretrained solely on synthetic labeled episodes and evaluated on the UEA-30 and UCR-128 archives. This approach eliminates the need for task-specific classifier fitting, potentially reducing computational costs and enabling rapid deployment across diverse time series classification tasks. It addresses the challenge of heterogeneous channel configurations, which is common in real-world applications like healthcare and industrial monitoring. ChorusTIC combines episode-consistent Random Subchannel Slot Concatenation with a shared dual-axis encoder to model temporal and cross-channel interactions, mapping variable channel configurations into a fixed-width representation. It calibrates feature axes using context-derived distributions and predicts query labels through leakage-protected in-context learning.

The framework uses an orchestrator to coordinate agents for 3D feature recognition, 2D drawing analysis, 2D-3D context fusion, knowledge retrieval, process sequencing, tool selection, and report generation. It combines deterministic modules with LLM reasoning agents, and was evaluated on 300 benchmark cases, achieving 90% source detection accuracy in conflict analysis.

## [Hierarchical Skill Retrieval Boosts Data-Efficient VLA Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24042) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Hierarchical Skill Retrieval (HSR), a novel retrieval framework that decomposes target tasks into skill sequences and retrieves reusable skills from prior datasets to improve data-efficient adaptation of Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models. Experiments on LIBERO and real-world tasks show success rate improvements of 10.3% and 21.3% over the strongest baseline. This work addresses a critical challenge in robot manipulation: adapting large VLA models to new tasks with limited demonstrations. By leveraging hierarchical skill-level retrieval, it offers a practical and effective approach that could accelerate deployment of VLA models in real-world applications, benefiting the robotics and AI/ML communities. HSR first decomposes a target task into candidate skill sequences, evaluating each plan based on semantic plausibility and skill reliability. It then performs hybrid retrieval combining subtask-level language retrieval with behavior-feature reranking, followed by a two-stage pretraining and finetuning pipeline. The method is validated on the LIBERO benchmark and real-world manipulation tasks, with code and videos available online.

Retrieval-based methods reuse existing demonstrations, but existing approaches often rely on visual similarity or task-level language matching, overlooking the hierarchical structure of long-horizon tasks. HSR addresses this by decomposing tasks into skills and retrieving at the skill level, which is more effective when complete task matches are rare.

## [VisCache: Training-Free Visual KV Cache Pruning for Efficient VLLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24063) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VisCache introduces a training-free, plug-and-play framework for coarse-to-fine visual KV cache pruning in Vision Large Language Models (VLLMs), combining keyframe selection with a parabolic layer-wise budget allocation algorithm. It achieves up to 2.35x speedup and significant memory reduction while retaining only 19-28% of the KV cache, outperforming existing baselines. This work addresses the critical bottleneck of long-context inference in multimodal models, where visual KV caches cause prohibitive memory and computation costs. By offering a training-free and plug-and-play solution, it enables efficient deployment of VLLMs in resource-constrained environments, potentially accelerating real-world multimodal applications. VisCache consists of two stages: a lightweight VLM filters temporal redundancy by selecting semantically informative keyframes, and PruneKV, a surgical KV compression algorithm, uses a parabolic layer-wise budget allocation with an asymmetric update mechanism that prunes keys while fusing values. The method is evaluated on long-context VLLM inference, establishing a new Pareto frontier between efficiency and performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Large Language Models (VLLMs) are multimodal models that process both images and text, but their long-context inference is expensive due to the massive memory and computation of Key-Value (KV) caches. KV cache pruning is a technique that selectively removes or compresses cached key and value tensors to reduce memory growth and computational overhead. Existing methods often apply uniform pruning across tokens and layers, leading to information loss, which VisCache aims to overcome with a coarse-to-fine approach.

## [OrbitALIF: Efficient Spiking Federated Learning for Onboard Cloud Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24073) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OrbitALIF, a federated spiking neural network framework for onboard cloud removal in LEO satellite constellations. It achieves competitive cloud removal quality while consuming only 0.287 mJ per inference on neuromorphic hardware, a 72.3 times (98.6%) energy reduction versus an equivalent ANN. This work addresses the real-world challenge of cloud removal in satellite imagery, which is critical for disaster monitoring and environmental surveillance. By enabling efficient onboard processing, it reduces reliance on ground stations, lowers latency, and improves the responsiveness of LEO satellite constellations. OrbitALIF uses a compact 2.30M-parameter SNN backbone with an adaptive gated fusion module (AGFM) and a spectral-spatial hybrid attention module (SHAM). It employs a decentralized federated learning strategy that shares model weights via inter-satellite links, enabling collaborative training without centralizing data.

This paper presents a systematic cross-lingual analysis showing that minimal responses are common in human counseling dialogues but underrepresented in LLM-generated ones, and proposes a two-stage filtering method to identify them. It evaluates current LLMs on curated contexts, finding that commercial models can generate minimal responses when instructed but struggle to determine appropriateness. This research highlights a critical gap in counseling dialogue systems, which often prioritize content-rich responses over interactionally valuable brief utterances. Improving LLMs' ability to use minimal responses appropriately could enhance empathetic AI and better support mental health applications. The filtering method combines utterance length and content criteria, followed by contextual verification using an LLM. Results show that counseling-specific models trained on synthetic data perform poorly, and LLM-based evaluation may undervalue minimal responses.

## [PARTAB: Partition-Aware Reasoning for Scalable Table Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24082) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PARTAB is a new framework that improves LLM table reasoning on large tables by partitioning tables into row-linked regions and hierarchically selecting evidence before answer generation. It consistently outperforms full-table prompting and several recent methods on benchmarks like WikiTableQuestions and TabFact. This addresses a known limitation of LLMs: performance degrades on large tables due to irrelevant context and difficulty localizing evidence. By providing a structured evidence interface, PARTAB enables more scalable and accurate table reasoning, which is valuable for real-world applications involving large datasets. PARTAB performs hierarchical selection over column groups and row-level partitions, representing query-relevant evidence as semantically coherent, row-linked table regions. Evaluations show it achieves 79.31 exact-match accuracy on WikiTableQuestions and substantially reduces reasoning context, with larger benefits on complex tables.

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown strong capabilities in table reasoning, but their effectiveness degrades as tables grow in size and complexity. Existing approaches typically reason over the full table or a single reduced view, which can obscure important row-column relationships. PARTAB addresses this by constructing a structured evidence interface that partitions tables into semantically coherent regions and performs hierarchical evidence selection.

## [Frequency-Domain Evidence Boosts LLM Time-Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an evidence-augmented zero-shot framework that adds global and local frequency-domain evidence (via FFT) to LLM-based time-series anomaly detection, improving detection of spectral anomalies. This approach addresses a known limitation of LLM-based TSAD methods, which primarily rely on time-domain evidence. By incorporating frequency-domain information, it enhances the ability to detect anomalies that manifest as changes in periodic or oscillatory patterns, potentially improving zero-shot performance across various domains. The framework preserves indexed de-seasonalized observations while adding compact frequency-domain evidence computed with FFT at two resolutions: global (sequence-level periodic context) and local (time-localized spectral departures). Experiments on AnomLLM with models like InternVL2-LLaMA3-76B, Qwen2.5-VL-72B-Instruct, Gemini-2.5-Flash, and GPT-4o, evaluated on the TSB-AD-U subset, show that explicit frequency-domain evidence improves LLM-based TSAD baselines.

The Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) is an efficient algorithm to compute the discrete Fourier transform, converting a time-domain signal into its frequency components, which can reveal periodic patterns and anomalies.

## [TransPhy: Physically Grounded Image Editing via Visual In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24119) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PhysVICL-74, a new benchmark with 74 physically grounded transformation rules and 5,240 source-target image pairs, forming nearly 75K training and evaluation contexts. It also proposes TransPhy, a framework that decomposes physically grounded visual in-context learning into physical-rule induction and transition-aligned rendering. This work addresses a novel and challenging problem in image editing, enabling models to infer and apply physical transformations from visual examples, which is crucial for realistic and context-aware editing. It could advance multimodal AI and computer vision applications, particularly in areas requiring physical understanding. TransPhy first predicts the demonstrated rule and an explicit query-specific target-state description, then synthesizes the target image through token-wise mixture-of-experts adaptation with expert routing guided by localized transition cues. The benchmark split separately evaluates novel-instance transfer and unseen-rule generalization, and experiments show improvements in physical-rule adherence, query consistency, and unseen-rule generalization over existing methods.

## [Syn2RealTrack: Decomposing Synthetic-to-Real Gap for Multi-View Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24130) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Syn2RealTrack introduces an online multi-view multi-target tracking pipeline that decomposes the synthetic-to-real gap into three separable issues—camera calibration, shape prior, and object census—each addressed with specific techniques. It achieves a 3D HOTA of 52.0118% on the AI City Challenge 2026 Track 1 evaluation server. This work addresses a significant challenge in multi-camera 3D perception for warehouse scenes, where models trained on synthetic data often fail in real-world deployment. By decomposing the gap and providing targeted remedies, it offers a practical pipeline that could improve robustness and reduce the need for extensive real-world labeled data, benefiting applied computer vision and robotics. The pipeline recovers lens distortion from images alone when calibration is absent, fuses detections across views using a visibility-weighted part-based descriptor that abstains on occluded parts, measures person height in closed form from calibration, and pairs a closed-world cardinality prior with a causal filter to remove phantom boxes. The system adapts by reallocating trust between geometry and appearance without retraining a feature extractor, and code will be released on GitHub.

**Background**: Multi-camera 3D perception systems for warehouse scenes are often trained on synthetic data due to the difficulty and cost of collecting real-world labeled data. The synthetic-to-real gap refers to the performance drop when models trained on synthetic data are applied to real environments, often corrupting ground-plane localization and cross-camera identity association. Traditional approaches treat this gap as a single domain adaptation problem, but Syn2RealTrack argues it enters at three separable points, each requiring a different remedy.

## [PlaceSeek: Human-Centered Geospatial Retrieval via Semantic Grounding and Affective Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24133) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PlaceSeek introduces a novel framework for retrieving urban outdoor places from natural language queries by decomposing them into functional and affective intents, verifying physical evidence via street-view imagery, and re-ranking with a LoRA-adapted vision-language model. Evaluated on 31,956 street-view locations in Milan, it achieves 88.0% Precision@5 and 0.920 nDCG@5, outperforming CLIP, SigLIP, and a VQA-based baseline. This work addresses the limitations of traditional POI-centric, metadata-driven geospatial retrieval, which fails to satisfy open-ended, affective, or activity-oriented queries. By modeling both verifiable visual evidence and human perceptual preferences, PlaceSeek paves the way for human-centered next-generation geospatial retrieval systems, impacting urban computing, vision-language models, and retrieval systems. The framework consists of a Semantic Grounding Module that verifies whether candidate street-view results contain the physical evidence needed for the intended activity, and an Affective Alignment Module that re-ranks physically valid candidates using a LoRA-adapted vision-language model trained on human urban perception judgments. Ablation results show that physical grounding is essential for retrieval validity, while affective alignment improves ranking quality among physically valid candidates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Geospatial retrieval traditionally relies on points of interest (POIs) and metadata, which cannot handle queries about activities or feelings. Vision-language models (VLMs) like CLIP and SigLIP can match images and text, but they lack fine-grained understanding of physical evidence and human perception. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is an efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts large models with minimal computational overhead, making it suitable for training VLMs on specific tasks like urban perception.

## [Zero-Shot Cross-City Object Detection via Pre-Training and Grayworld Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24154) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a modular training pipeline for zero-shot cross-city object detection, featuring multi-dataset pre-training with class-agnostic objectness distillation and a novel Grayworld augmentation. Their optimized variants, RF-DETR-HR and RF-DETR-Grayworld, achieve 47.53 mAP, ranking 1st on the AI City Challenge Track 6 leaderboard, a +24.29 gain over the baseline. This work addresses the critical problem of geographic domain shift in traffic surveillance, where models trained in one city often fail in another. By enabling zero-shot adaptation without accessing target data, it supports privacy-conscious deployments and could significantly improve real-world object detection systems. The pipeline uses a class-agnostic objectness distillation to decouple structural vehicle geometry from semantic taxonomies, and a Grayworld transformation that forces attention heads to discard chromatic shortcuts. The method is evaluated with RF-DETR, a real-time transformer-based detector, and runs within 16GB GPU memory.

**Background**: Zero-shot object detection aims to detect objects without prior training on specific classes, while cross-city detection addresses domain shift between cities. The Grayworld transformation is a color normalization technique that adjusts for illumination variations, helping models focus on shape rather than color.

## [LLM-Guided Action Evaluation for Industrial Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24156) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces LCAE, a method that uses a large language model before training to normalize industrial documents into a frozen action-observation-direction-delay relation basis, which is then used to modulate action evaluation in actor-critic frameworks for operational decisions. LCAE addresses a key challenge in industrial reinforcement learning by embedding domain knowledge from documents into the policy, potentially improving sample efficiency and decision quality in complex industrial processes. This could accelerate the adoption of RL in real-world industrial control where data is limited and safety is critical. The method does not run the LLM or embedding model online during training or deployment; it uses only frozen semantic artifacts and visible numerical history. It states a falsifiable hypothesis that documented relations, when correct and contextually modulated, provide a more useful decision bias than raw action coordinates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Industrial actor-critic methods typically represent continuous actions as anonymous numerical coordinates, requiring the agent to learn which process variables each action affects, in which direction, and after what delay from limited interactions. Fixed industrial documents already describe some of these relations but are not directly usable in numerical policies. LCAE leverages LLMs to extract and normalize this knowledge into a structured basis, integrating it into the policy learning process.

This paper systematically studies preference optimization for non-verbal vocalization (NV) synthesis in TTS, introducing a new NV-CER metric and demonstrating effective DPO-based setups on Emilia-NV and augmented NV-Bench covering 18 NV types. This work addresses a niche but important aspect of expressive TTS, providing practical insights into NV-aware post-training. The findings could influence future TTS research and improve the naturalness of synthesized speech with non-verbal vocalizations. The NV-CER metric treats NV tags as distinct output symbols and computes a weighted pinyin-based CER over both verbal and non-verbal content, enabling controllable optimization without modifying the underlying algorithm. ## [Tlow: Flow-Based Item Tokenizer Boosts Recommendation Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Tlow, a flow-based item tokenizer that transforms semantic embeddings into a standard normal distribution, enabling independent and efficient tokenization. It also proposes a novel codebook guidance mechanism to align codebook and token embedding spaces, improving recommendation performance. Tlow addresses key limitations of existing tokenizers like RQ-VAE (low decoding efficiency due to codebook dependencies) and OPQ (struggles with dimensional correlations and distribution complexity). By simplifying the embedding space, it enables more efficient and effective item tokenization, which is crucial for large-scale recommendation systems and cold-start scenarios. Tlow uses normalizing flows to map embeddings to a standard normal distribution, achieving dimensional independence. The codebook guidance aligns codebook space with token embedding space, enhancing semantic distinctiveness. Experiments on four public datasets show significant improvements, and online tests on WeChat for multi-modal retrieval improved CTR by 10.32% globally and 11.64% for new items.

**Background**: Item tokenizers replace randomly assigned item IDs with token IDs derived from semantic embeddings, addressing parameter explosion and cold-start issues. RQ-VAE is a common tokenizer but suffers from low decoding efficiency due to codebook dependencies. OPQ is more efficient but struggles with complex embedding distributions. Normalizing flows are a class of generative models that transform data into a simple distribution, enabling tractable density estimation and sampling.

## [SenseShift: Encoder-Based Mask Infilling for Continuous Sentiment Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24304) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SenseShift introduces an encoder-based framework for fine-grained sentence-level sentiment-controlled text generation, leveraging bidirectional attention, quantized sentiment signals, and iterative mask infilling. It achieves stronger sentiment controllability while maintaining text quality and robustness compared to larger decoder-based baselines. This work addresses a gap in controllable text generation by enabling continuous, sentence-level sentiment control, which is underexplored in current decoder-based approaches. It could impact applications like story and review generation, offering finer-grained control for content creators and NLP systems. SenseShift uses bidirectional attention and iterative mask infilling to generate local sentences conditioned on target sentiment intensity, unlike causal attention in decoders. It also employs quantized sentiment signals to represent fine-grained sentiment, and evaluations on story and review generation show improved controllability and robustness to out-of-domain data.

SenseShift proposes an encoder-based alternative that uses bidirectional attention and mask infilling to better handle sentence-level control.

## [Metadata-Aware Latent Diffusion for Conditional Cardiac MRI Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24342) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a metadata-aware latent diffusion framework for conditional cardiac MRI synthesis, encoding clinical metadata and slice position as text prompts. It integrates Metadata-Free CFG, Contrastive Batching, and Inverse-Frequency Sampling, achieving an FID of 37.47 on the UK Biobank dataset, a 57.04% improvement over the baseline without these strategies. This work addresses data scarcity and phenotype underrepresentation in cardiac MRI, which are critical for developing robust AI models in medical imaging. By improving metadata adherence and distributional fidelity, it demonstrates the potential of generative foundation models to create clinically meaningful synthetic data, potentially aiding in training and validation of diagnostic models. The framework was fine-tuned and evaluated on 59,058 short-axis CMR images from the UK Biobank. The combined approach improved FID by 28.68% over a previous text-conditioned CMR diffusion baseline that required cardiac geometry, while relying solely on patient metadata. Subgroup analyses showed improved alignment across demographic and acquisition-related metadata, with disease-specific conditioning being the most challenging.

**Background**: Latent diffusion models (LDMs) are a class of generative models that perform the diffusion process in a compressed latent space, enabling efficient high-quality image synthesis. Classifier-free guidance (CFG) is a technique that steers generation toward a condition by combining conditional and unconditional predictions, and the Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) is a standard metric for evaluating the quality of generated images by comparing feature distributions.

This paper introduces a framework using BlazePose to extract anatomical landmarks from ordinary video of squats, bench press, and deadlifts, converting them into joint-angle trajectories for quantitative technique assessment. The framework successfully recovers meaningful kinematic patterns for squats and deadlifts, enabling comparison between repetitions and identification of technique variability. This work makes biomechanical assessment of resistance training more accessible, potentially helping athletes and fitness enthusiasts reduce injury risk and improve performance without needing expensive lab equipment. It aligns with the growing trend of using markerless pose estimation in sports science and could democratize technique analysis. The framework relies on BlazePose, a lightweight pose estimation model, and uses root mean square error (RMSE) to compare joint-angle trajectories against a reference repetition. Performance depends strongly on camera orientation and occlusion, with non-sagittal views distorting 2D joint-angle estimates.

## [Multilevel Fair Allocation under Additive Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24400) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces multilevel fair resource allocation with tree-structured hierarchies, proposing three adaptations of envy-based fairness notions (e.g., WEF1) and analyzing the Multilevel Weighted Round Robin (MWRR) algorithm. It proves that under identical preferences, the three notions coincide and MWRR guarantees them, while under general preferences, MWRR may fail some notions but performs well experimentally. This work extends fair division to hierarchical structures, which is common in real-world organizations and resource allocation scenarios, potentially impacting multi-agent systems and algorithmic game theory. It provides new fairness notions and algorithms that could be applied to settings like organizational budgeting or nested resource distribution. The paper assumes internal nodes' utilities are the utilitarian welfare of their children, and leaves have additive utilities over items. It shows that the choice among the three adaptations is not neutral, and under identical preferences, MWRR guarantees all three, but under general preferences, it may fail some while still performing well in experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Fair division is a field studying how to allocate resources fairly among agents. Envy-freeness up to one good (EF1) is a standard notion for indivisible items, and its weighted variant (WEF1) accounts for agents' entitlements. Weighted Round Robin (WRR) is a scheduling algorithm that allocates items in turns based on weights, and its multilevel extension (MWRR) adapts it to hierarchical structures.

This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating imputation models on wearable data, where existing random-point holdout protocols are unrealistic. The findings could lead to more reliable imputation strategies for health monitoring applications. A new arXiv paper (2608.24492) systematically evaluates supervised uncertainty quantification (UQ) ensembles for LLM hallucination detection across four LLMs, nine datasets, and three generation regimes. The ensembles outperform the best individual scorer in 30 of 32 settings, with gains from as few as 100 labeled instances. This robustness study addresses a critical gap in empirical understanding of supervised UQ ensembles for hallucination detection, offering practical guidance for deploying these methods in real-world LLM applications. The findings on sample efficiency and domain transfer are particularly valuable for practitioners who need reliable hallucination detection without extensive labeled data. The study covers short-form QA, long-form generation, and code generation, and analyzes robustness along sample efficiency, in-domain dataset transfer, and generation regime dependence. Sampling-based black-box ensembles are nearly as effective as full ensembles, while single-generation white-box ensembles offer limited benefit.

## [Scalable Pipeline for Hierarchical Causal Models Applied to Project STAR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24500) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a complete, scalable, open-source pipeline for Hierarchical Structural Causal Models (HSCM) that integrates symbolic identification via pyAgrum's do-calculus with numerical estimation, and demonstrates it on the Project STAR dataset. A key innovation is an adapted Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) that decomposes identified formulas into independent tasks for parallel computation. This work bridges the gap between symbolic causal identification and practical estimation for hierarchical data, which is common in education, healthcare, and social sciences. By providing a scalable, open-source tool, it enables researchers to correctly encode class-level interventions and avoid the pitfalls of flat models that only recover associations. The pipeline includes graph transformations, pyAgrum's do-calculus for automatic identification, adaptation of symbolic expressions into closed-form HSCM formulas, and numerical estimation from fitted local probability models. Validation on canonical HSCM motifs and benchmarks with known ground truth shows that flat baselines fail to encode class-level interventions, and symbolic identification alone is insufficient for practical inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Structural Causal Models (SCMs) provide a framework for causal inference using directed acyclic graphs and do-calculus to identify causal effects. Hierarchical Structural Causal Models (HSCMs) extend SCMs to data with nested structures, such as students within classes. pyAgrum is a Python library for probabilistic graphical models that includes do-calculus and an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) representation for causal queries. The Project STAR experiment is a landmark study on class size effects on student outcomes.

## [FraudBench: Protocol-Sensitive Benchmark for Financial Adversarial Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24551) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FraudBench introduces a protocol-sensitive benchmark for evaluating adversarial robustness in financial fraud and credit-risk detection, covering four public datasets and multiple model types. It demonstrates that robustness conclusions vary significantly depending on the evaluation protocol used. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating adversarial robustness for financial tabular data, where domain constraints and attacker capabilities are often overlooked. Its findings could influence how future research designs robustness evaluations, leading to more reliable and realistic security assessments in financial machine learning. The benchmark evaluates the same dataset-model-attack-defense setting under three protocols: unconstrained attacks, post-hoc feasibility filtering, and deployment-aware constraint-integrated attacks. On Lending Club Loan Data, post-hoc filtering leaves only 3.7 feasible-flipped examples on average, while in-attack projection with attacker mutability masking produces 2,832.3 under the same perturbation budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial robustness in machine learning typically measures how well models maintain correct predictions under adversarial perturbations, often using metrics like robust accuracy. However, financial tabular data involve domain-specific constraints, severe class imbalance, and asymmetric attacker capability, which standard evaluation protocols may not capture. FraudBench argues that robustness is not only a model attribute but also a protocol attribute, and proposes a protocol-sensitive evaluation approach.

COCI is a novel AI-based framework that extracts fine-grained structured metadata from raw Calls for Papers (CfPs) using a multi-stage pipeline combining Large Language Models (LLMs) with semantic mapping. It integrates extracted entities with established scholarly knowledge bases such as OpenAlex, DBLP, TIB ConfIDent, and the AIDA Dashboard. This framework addresses a significant gap in scholarly communication by connecting informal grey literature like CfPs to structured Scholarly Knowledge Graphs, enabling systematic analysis of academic events. It has practical implications for digital libraries and knowledge graph construction, potentially improving research discovery and event tracking. COCI employs a multi-stage pipeline for entity extraction, followed by author disambiguation against OpenAlex and semantic mapping of topics and conference series. The paper is a demo paper, so the impact is moderate, but the technical approach is interesting.

## [Progressive Growth Biases Neural Networks Toward Flatter Minima](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24568) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a theoretical framework explaining how progressive grow-and-optimize strategies in deep learning bias training toward flatter minima, using the concept of constraint relaxation and effective curvature in frozen directions. This work provides a novel theoretical understanding of why progressive growth improves generalization, potentially guiding the design of more effective training strategies. It also highlights the nuanced relationship between flatness and test performance, which is crucial for the deep learning community. The authors prove that local sublevel sets around non-degenerate minima are well approximated by ellipsoids, and basin accessibility under frozen constraints is characterized by an explicit effective curvature. They validate their predictions in toy landscapes and a ResNet/CIFAR-100 setting, finding that flatter solutions do not always lead to better test performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Deep neural networks often have highly nonconvex loss landscapes, yet they generalize well, a phenomenon linked to the geometry of minima found by stochastic optimization. Progressive growth, where a model is trained starting from a small submodel and gradually expanded, has been used empirically to improve training, but its theoretical basis was unclear. This paper provides a formal analysis by modeling growth as progressive constraint relaxation, where parameters are unlocked in nested random subspaces while the orthogonal complement remains frozen at initialization.

## [findr: Transparent and Fair Credit Risk via Semi-Structured Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24582) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces findr, a semi-structured deep regression framework for binary credit risk modeling that decomposes the logit into an interpretable structured component and an orthogonal neural residual, with an in-processing Wasserstein fairness penalty. This framework addresses the critical trade-off between predictive accuracy, interpretability, and fairness in credit risk models, offering a practical solution for regulated industries. It enables practitioners to make explicit trade-offs and maintain auditable decision rules, which is increasingly important in responsible AI. The orthogonalization separates coefficient-based effects from residual nonlinear variation, and the Wasserstein penalty compares score distributions during training to mitigate group disparities. The framework includes diagnostics for structured component contribution, decision agreement, and local directional consistency, and was evaluated on eight public credit datasets using accuracy-fairness frontiers.

**Background**: Credit risk models often rely on logistic regression for interpretability, but it may miss nonlinear patterns. Flexible models like neural networks improve accuracy but their explanations are often post-hoc. Semi-structured regression combines additive models with neural networks to balance interpretability and flexibility, as seen in prior work on semi-structured distributional regression.

## [KENDO: Kernel Ensemble Framework Boosts Bayesian Optimization and Active Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24721) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces KENDO (Kernel ENsemble Disagreement-aware Operator), a unified framework that integrates ensemble Gaussian processes with disagreement-aware acquisition strategies for Bayesian optimization (BO) and active learning (AL). It instantiates KENDO-BO and KENDO-AL, demonstrating up to 5x and 27x speedups respectively over MCMC-based methods. This framework addresses model misspecification in BO and AL without the computational burden of MCMC sampling, potentially making these methods more practical for real-world hyperparameter tuning and data labeling tasks. It could influence future research on scalable Bayesian methods and ensemble-based uncertainty quantification. KENDO replaces hyperparameter sampling with a kernel ensemble and adaptive Bayesian weighting, combined with disagreement-aware acquisition. The approach extends to multi-objective optimization via random scalarization, and numerical tests show competitive or superior performance on synthetic and real-world benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian optimization and active learning often rely on Gaussian processes (GPs) with hyperparameters that are difficult to tune, and model misspecification can degrade performance. Fully Bayesian treatments using MCMC are accurate but computationally expensive. Ensemble Gaussian processes (EGP) combine multiple GPs to improve robustness, and disagreement-aware acquisition strategies use model disagreement to guide sampling, offering a cheaper alternative to MCMC.

## [Efficient EEG Adaptation: 9% Parameters, 20x AUCPR Gain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24727) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a parameter-efficient self-supervised adaptation strategy for EEG foundation models, updating only 9% of parameters. It achieves up to 20x AUCPR improvement over linear probing on three clinical EEG datasets under fixed computational budgets. This work addresses the practical challenge of deploying EEG foundation models in resource-constrained clinical settings, where full fine-tuning is often infeasible. It demonstrates that parameter-efficient adaptation can achieve strong performance with minimal computational overhead, potentially accelerating the adoption of EEG foundation models in real-world clinical applications. The method was evaluated on two state-of-the-art EEG foundation models with different pretraining objectives: BIOT (contrastive) and CBraMod (masked reconstruction). Experiments covered three clinical tasks (TUAB abnormality detection, TUEV event classification, CHB-MIT seizure detection) under both in-distribution and out-of-distribution conditions. Notably, when total window count is fixed, performance remains invariant to patient count, suggesting that temporal window diversity is the key factor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: EEG foundation models are pretrained via self-supervised learning to learn transferable representations from unlabeled EEG data. However, their generalization to diverse clinical datasets is often limited, and full fine-tuning is computationally expensive. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods, such as adapters, have been successful in NLP and vision, and this work extends the idea to EEG foundation models using self-supervised objectives on unlabeled target-domain data.

A new mixed-methods study analyzed millions of AI-related articles and social media posts, along with interviews with 57 AI professionals in 2021 and 2023, to identify how they make sense of AI. This research provides a sociological framework for understanding how AI professionals interpret and shape AI development, which is crucial for policymakers and technologists as AI becomes transformative. It highlights the cognitive challenges and framing dynamics that influence beliefs, values, and actions in the AI ecosystem. The study uses computational text analysis of millions of articles and social media posts, combined with semi-structured interviews conducted before and after the AI surge. The three debates identified are: top-down expert systems vs. bottom-up emergent capabilities (method), passive tool vs. humanlike 'digital mind' (mind), and slowing down vs. speeding up AI development (morality).

## [Unified Bayesian Model for RAG Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24753) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Bayesian evaluation framework that jointly models retrieval success, abstention behavior, and answer correctness in RAG systems, factorized according to the pipeline's information flow. It applies the framework to 27 RAG configurations across three datasets, retrievers, and generators, revealing behavioral differences that are hidden under marginal metrics. This framework addresses a gap in current RAG evaluation methods by decomposing errors along the pipeline, providing a more nuanced understanding of system behavior. It can help practitioners identify specific weaknesses and optimize RAG configurations, potentially improving the development and deployment of RAG systems in real-world applications. The model distinguishes between task success (whether the user received a correct answer) and generator appropriateness (whether the generator behaved appropriately given the retrieval outcome). It also extends to incorporate LLM-as-a-judge annotations as calibrated noisy observations, enabling a unified probabilistic model that combines limited human judgments with cheaper automated assessments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems combine a retrieval component with a generative language model to produce answers grounded in external knowledge. Evaluating such systems typically focuses on end-to-end correctness, but this overlooks how errors propagate through the pipeline. Bayesian inference provides a probabilistic framework for updating beliefs based on evidence, which is well-suited for modeling the uncertainty in RAG components. The paper leverages this to offer a more detailed evaluation.

## [Score-Based Ideal Observer Approximation via Denoising Score Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24768) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a score-based ideal observer (SIO) that reformulates the Bayesian Ideal Observer test statistic using score functions. It uses a denoising convolutional neural network trained on signal-absent images to estimate the score, enabling approximation of the IO test statistic for arbitrary additive signals without per-image posterior sampling or signal-specific retraining. This work could significantly improve the efficiency and flexibility of approximating the Bayesian Ideal Observer in medical imaging and other detection tasks. By avoiding extensive posterior sampling and enabling task-agnostic approximation, it may accelerate the adoption of IO-based performance evaluation in practical imaging systems. The proposed SIO is trained exclusively on signal-absent images, and once trained, it can handle arbitrary additive signals without retraining. Numerical studies on a signal-known-exactly (SKE) detection task with a stochastic lumpy-background model demonstrate that the SIO closely approximates the IO performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: The Bayesian Ideal Observer (IO) sets the theoretical upper bound for binary detection task performance, but its test statistic is often intractable to compute analytically. Traditional numerical approaches rely on Markov-chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) methods, which require extensive posterior sampling per image. Score-based generative models, which learn the gradient of the log-probability density, have emerged as a powerful tool for modeling data distributions. Denoising score matching (DSM) is a technique to train such models by matching the score to gradients of noise-corrupted data.

The paper introduces COPE, a test-time collaboration framework where small and large language models alternate as planner and executor, using plans as a lightweight interface to solve tasks efficiently. Experiments show COPE achieves performance comparable to large proprietary models while drastically reducing inference API costs. ## [ExeFuse: A Neuro-Symbolic Framework for Domain-Specific Knowledge Graph Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10485) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a new task, domain-specific knowledge graph fusion (DKGF), and presents ExeFuse, a neuro-symbolic framework based on a Fact-as-Program paradigm. ExeFuse treats fusion as an executable process, using neuro-symbolic execution and target space grounding to address domain relevance ambiguity and granularity misalignment. This work addresses a critical gap in knowledge graph research by systematically leveraging general knowledge graphs to enrich domain-specific ones, which is essential for improving the completeness and utility of specialized KGs. The proposed task and framework could impact applications such as question answering, recommendation systems, and domain-specific AI, where high-quality domain knowledge is crucial. ExeFuse introduces a novel Fact-as-Program paradigm that treats fusion as an executable process, using neuro-symbolic execution to infer logical relevance beyond surface similarity and target space grounding to calibrate granularity. The authors constructed new datasets to establish the first standardized evaluation suite for DKGF, and extensive experiments demonstrate that ExeFuse effectively overcomes domain barriers.

**Background**: Knowledge graphs (KGs) are structured representations of entities and their relationships, used in AI applications like search and reasoning. CoMMa introduces a decentralized LLM multi-agent framework with contribution-aware aggregation for privacy-preserving and interpretable oncology decision support. It replaces stochastic narrative reasoning with deterministic embedding projections to approximate each agent's marginal utility. This framework addresses privacy concerns in clinical settings by enforcing data decentralization and role specialization, improving interpretability and reliability of AI-driven oncology decisions. It could enhance multidisciplinary tumor board workflows and generalize across heterogeneous clinical environments. CoMMa is evaluated on multiple oncology benchmarks, including real-world multidisciplinary tumor board datasets from large academic hospitals in North America and East Asia, as well as public datasets. The contribution-aware aggregation mechanism provides explicit credit assignment over agents, aligning with clinical requirements.

PHMForge is a new evaluation environment that provides 99 SME-authored scenarios across eight industrial asset classes, served through 39 MCP-native tools, to assess LLM agents on industrial prognostics tasks. It aims to separate protocol fluency from reasoning and tool use, addressing conflations in prior benchmarks. The paper introduces the Compression Calculus, a formal framework for comparing surface evidence with atomic representations and describing how abstraction compounds across layers. It evaluates this framework on large software corpora, showing substantial evidence-to-concept consolidation in two projects. This framework provides a theoretical basis for understanding intelligence as compression, potentially influencing AI architecture and complex systems research. It also offers a new perspective on the role of large language models as dynamic fusion engines rather than sole repositories of concepts. The evaluation uses an open-world concept model on multi-source software corpora, finding that consolidation depends on corpus scale, evidence-unit definition, and concept identity. The observed reduction is driven primarily by within-corpus recurrence rather than cross-source recurrence.

## [Audit of OpenAI Proof Finds Error Was PDF Artifact](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14673) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An independent audit of an OpenAI-generated mathematical proof initially identified a polarity error in a greedy conditioning lemma, but subsequent examination revealed the error was caused by automatic PDF text extraction that removed an overbar from a symbol. The alleged error has been withdrawn, and no confirmed mathematical error remains in the examined lemma. This highlights a critical methodological hazard in auditing AI-generated mathematics: errors can arise not only in reasoning but also in the document-processing pipeline. It underscores the need for careful verification of source documents when reviewing AI proofs, which is increasingly important as AI-generated mathematical results become more common. The proof under audit is from Chapter 6 of OpenAI's 'Ten Advances in Mathematics and Theoretical Computer Science.' The initial error was a polarity error in a 'quantitative greedy conditioning' lemma, but the correct typeset manuscript showed the overbar was present, indicating a PDF extraction artifact.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: AI-generated mathematical proofs are becoming more prevalent, but verifying them requires careful human assessment. PDF text extraction can introduce artifacts, such as missing symbols, which can lead to false error reports. The paper introduces Reconstruction, a blind benchmark that evaluates language models' ability to infer a paper's core idea from its pre-publication bibliography. Across 643 papers in six scientific domains, frontier models achieved only 3-15% match rates, while a multi-agent pipeline improved this to 23-42%. This benchmark addresses a novel task—idea recovery from bibliographies—and highlights the limitations of current LLMs in scientific reasoning. The multi-agent approach's significant improvement suggests potential for AI-assisted research, though the low baseline rates indicate substantial room for advancement. The benchmark employs a strict anti-leakage protocol including temporal citation cutoff, anonymous reference IDs, and frozen per-paper bibliographies. The multi-agent pipeline uses cross-model review and a Swiss tournament over aligned hypothesis slots, achieving approximately 2.4x improvement over the best single model.

Researchers introduced ExPhy, a new benchmark containing 24,000 simulated multi-object physical scenes with explicit labels for mass, friction, and restitution, along with in-distribution and two out-of-distribution splits. They also proposed PhyODE, a physics-guided model that estimates these properties and uses them for differentiable future rollout, achieving significant improvements on the OOD-Initial setting. This benchmark addresses a gap in trajectory forecasting by making physical properties explicit evaluation targets, enabling more rigorous assessment of models' understanding of object dynamics. It could drive progress in developing models that not only predict trajectories accurately but also recover underlying physical parameters, which is crucial for applications like robotics and autonomous driving. PhyODE reduces ADE and FDE by 33.1% and 31.0% respectively on the long-horizon OOD-Initial setting compared to the strongest baseline. The benchmark also includes zero-shot evaluation on ComPhy to assess cross-benchmark transfer, and property-level analyses reveal that accurate trajectory forecasting does not necessarily imply accurate recovery of physical properties.

This paper introduces a slot-selective evidence-masking regime that restricts each module's input to its own evidence span in shared-genome language-model societies. In a sealed natural-language function-composition task, restricted-visibility societies outperformed globally visible twins by at least 20 percentage points in 9 of 10 matched pairs. This work challenges the common practice of exposing all modules to full input and suggests that selective masking can significantly improve compositional generalization in modular neural systems. The societies consist of four cells sharing one frozen pretrained language model and one low-rank adapter, communicating via two model-width continuous vectors. ## [GenCoord: Turning Private Facts into Executable Skill-Path Commitments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22055) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GenCoord introduces a framework that converts private task knowledge into executable skill-path commitments for embodied agents, using a local Qwen3.5-0.8B model to emit multi-step SELF plans and peer REQ requests. Counterfactual interventions demonstrate that correct capability feedback closes the paired local-information gap from 50% to 100% across three seeds. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in multi-agent embodied AI: coordinating actions when each agent holds private information that determines the joint task. By making task consequences the coordination unit, GenCoord enables verified joint action from distributed local reasoning, potentially improving robustness and efficiency in real-world multi-robot systems. The framework includes parsing, checking, canonical materialization, compilation to Mineflayer skills, and verification via handoff and terminal states. Multi-step commitments improve held-out-template success by 6.9 points while reducing model decisions by 32%, and Short DSL reduces peer traffic by 92.8% and median time-to-commitment by 68.2% compared to free-form communication.

GenCoord proposes a novel approach where the consequence of private facts is turned into a shared, executable commitment, allowing agents to coordinate effectively while preserving privacy.

This paper introduces MSIBench, a new benchmark with 904 real-world illusion images and 3,723 image-text pairs, along with a where-what-how taxonomy to systematically evaluate multimodal large language models (MLLMs) under situational illusions. Evaluations of 27 model configurations reveal that current MLLMs are highly vulnerable, and the proposed prompting and fine-tuning methods improve performance by up to 20%. This work addresses a critical gap in the robustness of multimodal AI systems, as real-world situations often deviate from their apparent physical states, which can lead to unreliable model outputs in practical applications. By providing a benchmark and mitigation strategies, it offers a path toward more reliable multimodal perception and reasoning, benefiting developers and users of AI systems in complex environments. The benchmark MSIBench includes 904 real-world illusion images and 3,723 image-text pairs, and the taxonomy categorizes illusions by where they occur, what targets they take, and how they arise. The evaluation of 27 model configurations identified six typical failure modes related to visual observation, grounding, and reasoning, and the proposed methods—prompting for closed-source models and supervised fine-tuning for open-source models—improve performance by up to 20%.

## [SA-RSQ: Sparse Residual Soft Quantization for Multimodal RecSys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SA-RSQ introduces a sparse activation-based residual soft quantization method that stores compact (Index, Probability) tuples via Top-K sparse routing and softmax weights, decoupling per-item storage from codebook dimensionality. It achieves favorable reconstruction-performance and CTR trade-offs across storage budgets of 8-48 bytes per item, with an online A/B test showing +2.51% CTR and +3.66% CPM lifts. This work addresses a critical industrial challenge of high storage and latency overhead in multimodal recommender systems, offering a practical solution that maintains performance while reducing storage. The positive online A/B test results suggest it can be directly applied in real-world systems, potentially improving efficiency and revenue for large-scale recommendation platforms. The method avoids the straight-through estimator by propagating gradients through routing weights and weighted reconstruction. Experiments were conducted on a proprietary food-delivery advertising dataset, and a preliminary Next-Distribution Prediction study was also performed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal recommender systems use high-dimensional features like images and text, which are expensive to store and process. Traditional quantization methods, such as hard quantization, are compact but cause boundary distortion, while dense soft quantization ties representation quality to storage budget. Sparse activation and Top-K routing are techniques that activate only the most relevant components, reducing computation and storage. The straight-through estimator is a common trick to handle non-differentiable operations in quantization, but it can be unstable.

This study proposes using YOLOv26s, a deep learning model originally designed for object detection, to classify facial images of children as autistic or typically developing. The model achieved 92.86% accuracy and an F1-score of 0.9291, demonstrating its effectiveness for ASD screening. Early intervention is crucial for improving outcomes in children with autism, and this approach offers a non-invasive, potentially low-cost screening method using only facial images. If validated, it could assist clinicians in early detection, especially in resource-limited settings, and highlights the versatility of YOLO models beyond object detection. The study compared YOLOv26s with other models, though specific comparison details are not provided in the abstract. The model was trained on facial images, and the high F1-score indicates a good balance between precision and recall, which is important for medical screening to minimize false negatives.

## [HiQA: Hierarchical Contextual Augmentation RAG for Multi-Document QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01767) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HiQA is a new framework that enriches text chunks with cascading document metadata (titles, section paths) and uses a multi-route retriever combining semantic, lexical, and keyword/entity signals. The authors also introduce MasQA, a benchmark for multi-document QA in realistic similar-document settings, and show HiQA improves retrieval and answer quality on MasQA. This addresses a critical weakness in RAG systems when knowledge bases contain many similar documents, which often degrades retrieval accuracy. By improving retrieval and answer quality in such settings, HiQA could enhance practical applications like enterprise document QA and domain-specific assistants, and MasQA provides a standardized way to evaluate MDQA systems. HiQA's multi-route retriever combines semantic, lexical, and keyword/entity signals, and the metadata augmentation uses document titles and section paths. Experiments show benefits are strongest for structured, domain-specific, highly similar document collections, while remaining competitive on public MDQA benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) integrates external documents during generation to improve answer accuracy and reduce hallucinations. However, when the knowledge base contains many semantically and structurally similar documents, retrieval can fail to distinguish relevant chunks. HiQA addresses this by adding hierarchical metadata to chunks and using multiple retrieval routes, and MasQA provides a benchmark to evaluate such systems in realistic settings.

<a id="item-321"></a>
## [Intrinsic PAPR: Fixing Misattribution in 3D Intrinsic Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00500) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Intrinsic PAPR, a framework that leverages Proximity Attention Point Rendering (PAPR) to enable direct per-point supervision, addressing the misattribution issue in point-based intrinsic decomposition and inverse rendering. It outperforms existing methods in novel view synthesis and albedo estimation on synthetic and real-world datasets. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in point-based intrinsic decomposition, where individual primitives learn incorrect features despite correct aggregated renderings. By enabling direct per-point supervision, it improves the accuracy and reliability of albedo and shading decomposition, which is crucial for applications like relighting, editing, and 3D scene modeling. Intrinsic PAPR uses PAPR to eliminate translucent primitives and directly predict appearance at ray-surface intersections, enabling accurate supervision of individual point features. It incorporates a 2D albedo prior adapted with conditional Implicit Maximum Likelihood Estimation (cIMLE) to handle monocular ambiguities and a space carving loss for multi-view consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Intrinsic decomposition separates an image into albedo (reflective properties) and shading (illumination properties), which is an ill-posed problem. Point-based inverse rendering methods often use volume rendering, which aggregates translucent primitives along rays and only supervises the final color, leading to misattribution. PAPR is a point-based rendering method that avoids volume rendering by directly predicting appearance at ray-surface intersections, enabling per-point supervision.

## [Generative Learner for Causal Effect Distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17894) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a multi-head neural network with cosine quantile embeddings to estimate heterogeneous treatment effects and the full distribution of causal effects under the Neyman-Rubin causal model. The method reportedly achieves over 70% improvement in out-of-sample mean squared error compared to existing methods like generalized random forest and double machine learning. This work advances causal inference by providing a generative approach that captures the entire distribution of treatment effects, not just averages, which is crucial for personalized decision-making in fields like medicine and economics. The significant performance gains, especially in small samples, could make it a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners. The treatment-effect subnetwork parameterizes the conditional quantile function via a compositional architecture combining covariate features and cosine quantile embeddings through element-wise multiplication. The method is applied to Gaia DR3 stellar data to formalize the Stefan-Boltzmann law as a causal model, recovering the nonlinear temperature-luminosity relationship.

**Background**: The Neyman-Rubin causal model, also known as the potential outcomes framework, is a foundational approach for causal inference that compares potential outcomes under different treatments. Quantile treatment effects extend this by characterizing the entire distribution of effects, not just the average, which is important for understanding heterogeneity. The proposed method uses neural networks and quantile embeddings to estimate these effects in a generative manner.

## [UPP and OptiSafe: Balancing Safety and Optimality in Robot Path Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23197) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Unified Path Planner (UPP), a graph-search algorithm that dynamically balances safety and optimality via adaptive heuristic weighting, along with the OptiSafe index, a normalized metric to quantify the safety-optimality trade-off. In cluttered environments, UPP achieves a 0.94 OptiSafe score compared to 0.22-0.85 for existing methods, with only 0.5-1% path-length overhead and a 100% success rate in simulation, validated on a TurtleBot. This work addresses a fundamental trade-off in robot path planning, offering a practical solution that maintains high safety without significant path-length penalties. It could improve the reliability of autonomous robots in cluttered environments, benefiting applications like warehouse automation, search-and-rescue, and domestic robotics. UPP uses a local inverse-distance safety field and auto-tunes its parameters based on real-time search progress, maintaining bounded heuristic behavior. The OptiSafe index is a normalized metric that quantifies the trade-off between safety and optimality, enabling rigorous comparison across algorithms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Path planning for autonomous robots often involves a trade-off between finding the shortest path and maintaining a safe distance from obstacles. Traditional algorithms like A* prioritize optimality, while methods like Voronoi diagrams focus on maximizing clearance. UPP is inspired by A* but integrates safety costs into the heuristic, allowing it to adaptively balance both objectives.

This paper introduces RegressionBug4APR, a benchmark of 200 real-world regression bugs from Java and Python projects, and empirically evaluates both traditional and LLM-based automated program repair (APR) techniques on them. The study finds that classical APR tools fail on all bugs, while LLM-based approaches show promise, and incorporating bug-inducing change information improves repair success by 1.6x. This work addresses a critical gap in APR research by focusing on regression bugs, which are common and costly in software maintenance. The benchmark and findings provide valuable resources and insights for researchers and practitioners, potentially guiding the development of more effective LLM-based repair tools. The benchmark includes 200 regression bugs from widely used GitHub repositories, and the study evaluates both traditional APR tools and advanced LLM-based approaches. The context-aware enhancement, which adds bug-inducing change information, yields 1.6x more successful repairs, and the results are consistent across Java and Python, suggesting generalizability.

## [STReason: Modular Framework Merging Spatio-temporal Models with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20073) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces STReason, a modular framework that integrates large language models (LLMs) with spatio-temporal models to perform multi-task inference and generate explanatory reasoning without task-specific fine-tuning. It leverages in-context learning to decompose natural language queries into interpretable programs, producing both numerical solutions and detailed rationales. This framework addresses a significant gap in spatio-temporal data mining, where existing models are often limited to narrow tasks and lack the ability for multi-task inference and long-form reasoning. By grounding explanations in verified computational outputs, STReason reduces hallucinations and broadens the applicability of LLMs to real-world, multi-faceted decision scenarios, potentially reducing expert workload. STReason constructs a new benchmark dataset and proposes a unified evaluation framework with metrics specifically designed for long-form spatio-temporal reasoning. Experimental results show it significantly outperforms advanced LLM baselines across all reasoning metrics, especially in complex, reasoning-intensive scenarios, and human evaluations confirm its credibility and practical utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Spatio-temporal data mining involves analyzing data that varies over both space and time, such as traffic flow or weather patterns, to support decision-making. Traditional models are often task-specific, while large language models (LLMs) excel at reasoning and generating text but may hallucinate when handling numerical or structured data. STReason combines the strengths of both by using LLMs to orchestrate modular spatio-temporal models, ensuring outputs are grounded in verified computations.

## [LLMs Show Promise and Limits in Choice Modelling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21790) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study systematically evaluated seven leading LLMs (ChatGPT, Claude, DeepSeek, Gemini, Gemma, Llama, Mistral) across twelve versions and five experimental configurations for specifying and estimating Multinomial Logit models. The results show that proprietary models with Chain-of-Thought prompting can generate valid utility specifications, while open-weight models struggled. This research explores a novel application of LLMs in econometrics and discrete choice modelling, potentially streamlining workflows for researchers and practitioners. The findings offer practical guidance on prompting strategies and highlight the current capabilities and limitations of different model families, informing future tool development. The study varied three dimensions: modelling goal (suggest vs. suggest and estimate), prompting strategy (Zero-Shot vs. Chain-of-Thought), and information availability (full dataset vs. data dictionary). Notably, GPT o3 in an agentic setting was the only model that could correctly estimate its own specifications by executing self-generated code, and some models performed better with only a data dictionary.
