## [NeuronTune: Fine-Grained Neuron Modulation for Balanced Safety-Utility in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09473) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NeuronTune introduces a fine-grained framework that identifies and modulates sparse safety-critical and utility-preserving neurons across all layers of an LLM, using attack-aware attribution and meta-learning to adaptively adjust activations. This approach achieves superior safety while maintaining utility, outperforming existing state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses a critical challenge in LLM alignment: the trade-off between robust safety and preserved utility. By moving beyond coarse-grained layer-wise interventions to neuron-level modulation, it offers a more precise and flexible approach that could influence future alignment research and deployment of safer, more reliable LLMs. The framework dynamically controls the intervention scope via neuron inclusion thresholds, allowing prioritization of either safety-critical or utility-priority requirements. Extensive experiments demonstrate that NeuronTune outperforms existing state-of-the-art techniques, achieving superior safety while maintaining excellent utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) require safety alignment to prevent malicious use, but current methods often suffer from insufficient robustness against attacks, frequent refusal of benign queries, and degradation in output quality. These issues stem from coarse-grained layer-wise interventions that cannot precisely separate safety-critical and utility-preserving neurons. NeuronTune addresses this by pinpointing and modulating sparse neurons at a fine-grained level, using attack-aware attribution and meta-learning to adaptively balance safety and utility.

## [Formal Verification of Explainability via Epistemic Temporal Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.01479) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a formal framework to specify and verify explainability requirements in multi-agent systems using epistemic temporal logic extended with counterfactual causes. It provides an algorithm and prototype implementation for checking finite-state models against these specifications. This work bridges explainability and privacy in AI systems, offering a rigorous method to balance positive information flow (explainability) against negative information flow (privacy leaks). It could influence formal verification practices for AI safety and regulatory compliance. The framework uses epistemic temporal logic with quantification over counterfactual causes to specify that agents acquire knowledge about why effects occurred. The prototype implementation is evaluated on benchmarks, distinguishing explainable from unexplainable systems and supporting additional privacy constraints.

## [Review of Automation Transparency for Maritime Autonomous Ships](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15959) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a systematic review of 100 studies on automation transparency for Maritime Autonomous Surface Ships (MASS), mapping the Guidance-Navigation-Control stack to shore-based operational modes and identifying where human unsafe control actions concentrate. It also proposes an adaptive transparency framework that couples operator state estimation with explainable decision support. This review addresses a critical safety barrier in the adoption of autonomous ships: opaque decision-making and poorly calibrated human-automation interaction. By synthesizing evidence and proposing design strategies, it provides actionable guidance for improving trust, safety, and regulatory compliance in MASS operations. The review identifies transparency features such as decision rationales, alternatives, confidence/uncertainty, and rule-compliance indicators that improve understanding and trust calibration, though reliability and predictability often dominate trust. It distills design strategies at three layers: sensor/SA acquisition and fusion, HMI/eHMI presentation, and engineer-facing processes, and integrates methods like STPA-Cog + IDAC for Human-UCA identification.

## [VGGT-DP: Boosting Robot Imitation Learning with 3D Vision and Proprioception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18778) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VGGT-DP is a new visuomotor policy framework that integrates the pretrained 3D perception model VGGT as the visual encoder, guided by proprioception, and introduces a frame-wise token reuse (FTR) mechanism to reduce inference latency. It significantly outperforms strong baselines like DP and DP3 on MetaWorld tasks, especially in precision-critical and long-horizon scenarios. This work addresses a critical gap in visual imitation learning by focusing on the visual encoder's structure and capacity, which are often neglected in favor of policy design. By leveraging a 3D vision foundation model and proprioceptive feedback, it improves spatial understanding and generalization, potentially advancing robot manipulation in real-world applications. The FTR mechanism reuses cached VGGT aggregator tokens from overlapping observation frames, computing features only for the latest frame to reduce redundant encoding. Additionally, random token pruning is applied to enhance policy robustness and reduce overfitting.

## [Monotone and Separating Set Functions: Theory and Neural Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Monotone and Separating (MAS) set functions, which preserve the partial order on sets via vector embeddings, and establishes lower and upper bounds on the embedding dimension. For infinite ground sets, it proves that MAS functions do not exist, but proposes a weakly MAS model with Hölder stability. This work addresses a fundamental problem in set-to-vector representation with theoretical guarantees, potentially impacting set containment and monotone function approximation tasks. The proposed weakly MAS model offers a practical solution for infinite ground sets, which are common in real-world applications. The paper provides dimension bounds as a function of the cardinality of multisets and the underlying ground set. It also demonstrates that MAS functions can construct universal models that are monotone by construction and can approximate all monotone set functions, with experiments on set containment tasks showing benefits over standard set models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Set functions map sets to vectors and are used in various machine learning tasks. Monotonicity with respect to set inclusion is a desirable property for tasks like set containment, where the order of sets should be reflected in the embedding space. Hölder continuity is a measure of function smoothness that ensures stability of the model.

## [RoPAR: Robust Motion Generation from Partially Occluded Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12703) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RoPAR, a part-aware masked autoregression model that leverages credible part-level data from videos to generate human motion robustly despite occlusions. It also contributes K700-M, a new benchmark with about 200k real-world motion sequences. This work addresses a critical challenge in motion generation from web videos—handling occlusions—which is common in real-world data. By enabling robust generation from partially observed data, it can improve the scalability and diversity of motion datasets, benefiting character animation and related fields. The method decomposes the human body into five parts, identifies 'credible' parts visible in each frame, and encodes them with a part-aware variational autoencoder. A robust part-level masked generation model predicts masked credible parts while ignoring noisy ones, and a diffusion head refines the full-body sequence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Extracting human motion from large-scale web videos is a scalable solution to data scarcity in character animation, but occlusions and off-screen captures often make some body parts invisible. Existing approaches either discard such data, limiting scale and diversity, or retain it, compromising quality. RoPAR leverages part-level reliability to overcome this dilemma.

## [SPICE: A PyTorch Library for Reproducible Predictive Process Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16715) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SPICE is a new Python deep learning library that reimplements three baseline predictive process mining methods in PyTorch, providing a common configurable framework for reproducible benchmarking across 11 datasets. This addresses the significant reproducibility issue in predictive process mining, enabling fair comparisons and fostering future research by providing a unified framework for benchmarking. SPICE compares its implementations to original reported metrics and fair metrics on 11 datasets, ensuring rigorous configurability and robust comparison of past and future modeling approaches.

**Background**: Predictive Process Mining (PPM) uses machine learning to predict the future behavior of ongoing business processes, such as predicting key performance indicators. ## [Quantization's Impact on LLM Self-Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00282) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study investigates how quantization affects the quality and faithfulness of large language models' self-explanations, finding moderate declines in both. This research is significant because it addresses a critical gap in understanding how quantization, a common optimization technique, affects the transparency of LLMs, which is crucial for high-stakes applications. The findings provide valuable insights for practitioners who need to balance efficiency with interpretability. The study examines natural language explanations and counterfactual examples generated by LLMs quantized with three common techniques at different bit widths. Results show moderate declines in SE quality (up to 4.4%) and faithfulness (up to 3.9%), with a user study indicating reduced coherence and trustworthiness (up to 8.5%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Quantization is a technique that reduces the precision of model parameters, such as converting 32-bit floating-point numbers to 8-bit or 4-bit integers, to make LLMs more memory-efficient and faster to deploy. Self-explanations are explanations generated by LLMs to justify their own outputs, which are increasingly used for transparency in critical applications. Faithfulness refers to how accurately an explanation reflects the model's actual reasoning process.

## [Quantum Tug-of-War Model Reveals Contextuality in Decision Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10034) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a quantum-like extension of the Tug-of-War (QTOW) decision-making model using qutrit states, demonstrating that certain context-dependent decision processes cannot be represented by a single non-contextual classical probability space. The model employs a state-disturbing generalized decision instrument and norm-preserving feedback, and it violates KCBS-type non-contextuality bounds. This work provides a theoretical bridge between quantum foundations and cognitive science, offering a compact single-state representation for contextual decision processes. It highlights an architecture-level trade-off in natural and artificial intelligence: contextual information can be carried intrinsically by transformations of a shared internal state or externalized into additional memory resources. The QTOW model uses a qutrit (three-level quantum system) and includes optional probing operations within one state space. The non-contextuality violation is witnessed by KCBS-type probe families, but classical reconstructions can avoid it by adding context labels, stored history, or restricting the probe set.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Qutrits are quantum information units with three levels, capable of superposition of three orthogonal states. The KCBS inequality is a non-contextuality inequality used to test quantum contextuality, similar to the CHSH inequality for non-locality. The Tug-of-War model is a classical decision-making framework where evidence for competing options accumulates until a threshold is reached.

## [TangramPuzzle: New Benchmark for Spatial Reasoning in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16520) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced TangramPuzzle, a benchmark with 668 validated configurations and 1,336 instances for evaluating compositional spatial reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It includes a Tangram Construction Expression (TCE) for machine-verifiable geometric specifications and a multi-solution-aware verifier. This benchmark fills a gap in existing MLLM evaluations by focusing on precise compositional spatial reasoning under geometric constraints, which is underexplored. It provides a rigorous, machine-verifiable method to assess models, potentially guiding future improvements in spatial understanding for AI systems. The benchmark covers two tasks: part-to-whole Outline Prediction and whole-to-part End-to-End Assembly Generation. The TCE framework includes five components: instance_id, target_outline, initial_state, final_state, and adjacency_graph, enabling separate evaluation of geometric validity and silhouette fidelity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but existing benchmarks often only assess coarse spatial relations and lack rigorous geometric verification. TangramPuzzle uses tangram puzzles, which require assembling geometric pieces to match a target silhouette, to test compositional spatial reasoning. The multi-solution-aware verifier accounts for multiple valid solutions, a feature missing in many previous benchmarks.

## [Framework for Ad Insertion in LLM Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19435) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a framework for inserting ads into LLM responses using two decoupling strategies: decoupling ad insertion from response generation and decoupling bidding from specific user queries via 'genres'. The framework applies the VCG auction mechanism and introduces an 'LLM-as-a-Judge' metric for contextual coherence. This addresses the critical challenge of sustainable monetization for LLMs, balancing revenue generation with user experience and ethical standards. It could influence how future AI assistants integrate advertising, impacting advertisers, platforms, and users. The framework uses VCG auction with genre-based bidding, achieving approximate DSIC, IR, and social welfare guarantees. In synthetic experiments with 10^5 advertisers and 100 slots, VCG clears in ~1.25 seconds on a consumer laptop. The LLM-as-a-Judge metric correlates with human ratings at Spearman's rho≈0.66.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: LLM advertising is an emerging field where ads are inserted into AI-generated responses. Traditional search advertising relies on static keywords, which fails to capture dynamic user intent in conversations. Recent approaches include token-level and query-level bidding, but they face issues like latency and privacy. This paper proposes a genre-based decoupling to address these challenges.

## [ICA: Information-Aware Credit Assignment for Long-Horizon Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10863) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Information-Aware Credit Assignment (ICA), a post hoc reward propagation method that estimates turn-level utility scores from rollout success rates and assigns dense rewards to intermediate steps that introduced high-utility information. Integrated with GSPO, ICA improves performance on BrowseComp, GAIA, Xbench-DS, and Seal-0 benchmarks. This work addresses a critical challenge in long-horizon reinforcement learning for web agents: sparse terminal rewards obscure which actions contributed to success. By providing a more granular credit assignment mechanism, ICA could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of training information-seeking agents in open-ended web environments, potentially advancing the state of the art in web automation and AI research. The method represents fetched webpages as rendered snapshots, preserving layout and multimodal content as stable content-level observations, which helps mitigate issues from text-based parsing. ICA estimates association-based utility scores for tool-acquired information and propagates dense rewards to search and fetch turns, and the code and datasets will be released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: In reinforcement learning, the credit assignment problem refers to the challenge of determining which actions in a long sequence led to a final reward. For information-seeking agents, terminal rewards only indicate whether the final answer is correct, not which retrieved information was useful. Traditional text-based webpage observations often lose layout and multimodal information, making it harder to compare across trajectories. ICA addresses this by using visual snapshots and post hoc reward propagation to assign credit more accurately.

PatientHub is a new unified and modular framework that standardizes the creation, simulation, and evaluation of LLM-based patient simulators. It provides 16 patient simulators, a graph-based orchestrator for multi-turn, multi-session interactions, and a configurable LLM-as-a-judge evaluator supporting multiple rubric types. This framework addresses the fragmentation in LLM-based patient simulation research by providing a reproducible pipeline, enabling fair comparison and reuse. It could accelerate development in counseling training and therapeutic assessment, benefiting researchers and practitioners in healthcare AI. The framework includes a command-line interface for generating patient profiles, running simulations, and applying rubric-driven evaluation at turn and session levels. It demonstrates extensibility by prototyping a new simulator variant with minimal additional code, and the code and data are publicly available on GitHub.

## [ST-Lite: Training-Free KV Cache Compression for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ST-Lite introduces a training-free KV cache compression method specifically designed for vision-language GUI agents, targeting three GUI-specific workload properties. It outperforms existing baselines across seven GUI benchmarks, achieving up to 2.35x decoding speedup at fivefold compression. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying GUI agents under memory and latency constraints, which is essential for practical applications like automated UI navigation and digital assistants. By improving KV cache compression for GUI workloads, it enables more efficient and responsive GUI agents, potentially accelerating their adoption in real-world scenarios. ST-Lite consists of three components: Trajectory-aware Semantic Gating (TSG) filters redundant historical frames, Component-centric Spatial Saliency (CSS) preserves fine-grained element boundaries, and a flat per-layer budget avoids hierarchical misallocation. The method is evaluated on seven GUI benchmarks and two backbones, and its implementation is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache compression is a technique to reduce the memory footprint of key-value caches in transformer-based models, enabling longer context handling and faster inference. Existing compression methods are designed for generic language workloads and fail to account for the unique structure of GUI interaction traces, such as high inter-frame visual redundancy and small UI-element spatial footprints. GUI agents, which use vision-language models to interact with graphical user interfaces, rely on efficient inference to operate in real-time on resource-constrained devices.

## [EstLLM: Boosting Estonian in Multilingual LLMs via Continued Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02041) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces EstLLM, a method using continued pretraining (CPT) with Estonian-enriched multilingual replay on Llama 3.1 8B and Apertus 8B, followed by supervised fine-tuning, preference optimization, and chat vector merging. It reports significant improvements in Estonian language competence, reasoning, translation, and instruction-following, with some English regression that is largely restored by chat vector merging. This work addresses the critical challenge of improving low-resource language capabilities in multilingual LLMs, which are often English-centric. The findings on trade-offs between language-specific gains and English regression, and the effectiveness of chat vector merging, provide valuable insights for the NLP community and developers working on language adaptation. The study uses Llama 3.1 8B and Apertus 8B as base models, applying CPT with Estonian-enriched multilingual replay, followed by mostly English supervised fine-tuning, preference optimization, and chat vector merging. Evaluation includes Estonian benchmarks, targeted pairwise human evaluation, and an Estonian Chatbot Arena-style setup, showing that the more English-centric Llama model achieves larger gains after adaptation, while Apertus exhibits stronger initial Estonian capabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are predominantly trained on English-centric data, leading to uneven performance for smaller languages. Continued pretraining (CPT) is a technique to adapt LLMs to new domains or languages by further training on relevant data, but it can cause catastrophic forgetting of original capabilities. Chat vector merging is a method that combines the weights of a base model and its chat-tuned version to restore instruction-following and alignment capabilities after adaptation.

## [Ollivier-Ricci Curvature Thesis Extends to Directed Graphs for GNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14211) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This thesis presents a comprehensive exposition of Ollivier-Ricci curvature, including novel extensions to directed graphs and applications to graph neural networks. It builds on prior work by Lin-Lu-Yau and Jost-Liu, offering new proofs and theoretical bounds for directed graph curvature. This work bridges pure mathematics and machine learning by providing a rigorous framework for applying curvature to directed graphs, which are common in real-world networks. It could enable more robust and interpretable graph neural networks, benefiting fields like social network analysis and bioinformatics. The thesis covers the 1-Wasserstein distance and optimal transport theory, and extends classical results like Bonnet-Myers and Levy-Gromov to the discrete setting. It also introduces novel combinatorial bounds for directed graph curvature, which are validated through applications in network science and graph machine learning.

This paper introduces RA-CMF, a novel conditional MeanFlow network for CT image reconstruction that integrates a reinforcement learning-driven policy network to spatially adapt the refinement process. The method achieves high accuracy in tumor regions with an average radiomic feature CCC of 0.93 ± 0.09, PSNR of 31.94 ± 2.64, and SSIM of 0.97 ± 0.03. This work addresses critical challenges in medical imaging, such as variability in noise and texture across CT scanners, by providing spatially adaptive reconstruction that improves tumor ROI accuracy. The combination of conditional flow-based reconstruction with reinforcement learning control could lead to more efficient and reliable CT imaging, benefiting clinical diagnosis and treatment planning. The policy network receives MeanFlow rollouts and predicts tile-wise refinement budgets, stopping criteria, and total budget allocation, trained via policy gradient reinforcement learning to maximize reconstruction quality while minimizing computation. The method also improves overall image quality with an average PSNR of 34.23 ± 1.71 and SSIM of 0.95 ± 0.01.

It constructs four types of expert tokens—semantic interaction, geometric structure, dynamic evolution, and ego trajectory—and uses a diffusion-based hierarchical multi-expert fusion planner to generate continuous ego trajectories. By providing explicit world representations as conditioning signals, it could improve planning performance, including collision avoidance and trajectory accuracy, and advance the field toward more reliable end-to-end driving systems. The framework extracts complementary world information through multi-source supervision and encodes it into expert tokens within the VLA. Experiments on NAVSIM v1 & v2 demonstrate future-scene modeling capability and strong planning performance, with ablation studies validating the complementarity of expert tokens and their effectiveness as planning conditions.

## [ForceFlow: Force-Aware Flow Matching for Contact-Rich Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11048) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForceFlow introduces a force-aware reactive framework built on flow matching, featuring an asymmetric multimodal fusion architecture that treats force as a global regulatory signal and a Vision-to-Force (V2F) handover mechanism for hierarchical task decomposition. It achieves a 37% success rate improvement over the strong baseline ForceVLA across six real-world contact-rich tasks. This work addresses a critical challenge in robotics: contact-rich manipulation, which requires precise force feedback and control. By improving success rates and reducing costs, ForceFlow could advance imitation learning and robot control, making robots more capable in real-world tasks. ForceFlow divides manipulation into a vision-dominant approach stage (VLM-based pointing) and a touch-dominant interaction stage (force-driven contact execution), with a V2F handover mechanism. It also demonstrates accurate force signal prediction and superior performance in contact force self-regulation and zero-shot out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization.

**Background**: Imitation learning enables robots to learn manipulation policies from demonstrations, but contact-rich tasks remain challenging due to complex contact dynamics. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that transforms a source distribution into a target distribution via an ordinary differential equation, offering stable training and faster inference than diffusion models. Force/torque sensing provides critical feedback for precise manipulation, and asymmetric multimodal fusion treats different modalities with varying reliability, which is key for integrating force and vision effectively.

## [Baseline Capture: When Incumbent Becomes the Specification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13393) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the concept of 'baseline capture,' a pathology where an incumbent implementation becomes the de facto specification, hindering computational reformulation for modern accelerators. It separates the question of whether a reformulation can be judged from whether its discovery can be automated. This concept is significant for software engineering and systems research, as it highlights a subtle barrier to adopting hardware-friendly reformulations that could yield large speed and energy gains. It provides a lens for evaluating reformulation results and encourages making demands explicit and independent of incumbents. The paper builds on the test-oracle problem, implementation bias, and acceptability-based design, and illustrates 'baseline capture' with cases like shortest-path routing and a single-GEMM audio frontend achieving 1.64x-3.29x speedup and up to 3.03x less energy. It introduces the move of 'buying a verifier' to make a demand explicit, operational, and independent of the incumbent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Computational reformulation involves posing a problem in a hardware-friendly form to leverage modern accelerators, but it requires a way to judge whether the reformulation is correct. The test-oracle problem refers to the challenge of determining correct expected outputs for testing, and implementation bias occurs when requirements are influenced by a specific implementation. This paper synthesizes these ideas to name a common pathology where the incumbent's output becomes the specification.

## [AeroDPO: Lightweight UAV Navigation Matches 7B Baselines via Automated Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07557) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AeroDPO introduces a lightweight 2B-parameter UAV navigation model that matches the success rates of 7B-parameter baselines, using automated Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) with physical simulation rollback to reduce collisions. The method achieves a 49.16% success rate on unmapped scenarios, setting a new state-of-the-art for autonomous aerial agents. This work challenges the assumption that large language models are necessary for UAV navigation, showing that perception quality is more critical than language reasoning capacity. It enables efficient edge deployment of autonomous drones, potentially reducing latency and cost while maintaining high performance, which is significant for robotics and real-world applications. AeroDPO uses a zero-cost automated DPO pipeline that detects collisions, rewinds the environment to extract causal reasoning errors as rejected actions, and applies decoupled privileged interventions to synthesize collision-avoidance maneuvers. An offline vision language inspector filters visual ambiguities, and the model is trained with a data flywheel to improve robustness in out-of-distribution scenarios.

Behavior Cloning (BC) is a common training method, but it lacks negative feedback, leading to high collision rates in unseen scenarios. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a technique that aligns models with preferred behaviors without extensive human annotation, making it suitable for automated training pipelines.

## [Epistemic Transfer Framework for AI-Assisted Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework and evaluation protocol for measuring epistemic transfer, the effect of prior AI-assisted verification on later unassisted performance. It defines two new metrics: Epistemic Transfer Effect (ETE) and Tool-Removal Cost (TRC). This work addresses a gap in current evaluation methods that only assess AI tools while they are present, ignoring long-term effects on user capability. It provides a diagnostic space to distinguish capability building, de-skilling, and other outcomes, which is crucial for designing AI tools that support independent judgment. The protocol combines answer-first and evidence-first AI conditions with active-practice and no-practice controls, delayed tests on held-out claims, and behavioral measures. It also includes participant- and item-level analyses to provide a comprehensive evaluation.

This paper introduces ER-KAN, a Kolmogorov-Arnold Network variant that combines shared Gaussian RBF bases, curriculum noise injection, and entropy-weighted adaptive regularization to achieve superior robustness in noisy, data-scarce settings. Experiments show ER-KAN degrades only 1.4x under sigma=0.1 noise, compared to 10.6x for ChebyKAN and 7.9x for vanilla KAN. This work addresses a critical gap in KAN research, which has largely ignored noisy, data-scarce conditions common in scientific machine learning. ER-KAN's robustness improvements could make KANs more practical for real-world scientific applications, and the proposed noise degradation ratio metric encourages standardized reporting in future efficient-KAN studies. ER-KAN is a 595-parameter network that matches MLP accuracy at moderate noise while degrading more gracefully as noise increases. It achieves 4.2x lower solution MSE than MLP on a damped harmonic oscillator PINN, but all models fail on a Burgers' equation PINN, a limitation the authors report transparently.

This paper presents the first mechanised formalisation of Romanov's Triplet Logic (TLS) in the Rocq proof assistant, including the translation from 3-CNF, the clearing procedure, and the Simple Vertex Intersection (SVI) algorithm. It proves polynomial-time bounds for the sliding-window fragment and establishes a correctness boundary for SVI, along with a verified OCaml prototype named VFR. This work bridges combinatorial logic and formal verification, providing a machine-checked foundation for a novel SAT-related framework. It offers a verified decision procedure for a fragment of TLS and a sound filter for general 3-CNF, which could influence future automated reasoning tools and proof-assistant-based verification of complex algorithms. The Rocq development comprises over 23,000 lines of code with 424 proved lemmas and no unproved assumptions. The extracted OCaml prototype VFR includes a Python runtime and Docker packaging, and benchmarks corroborate the predicted behavior. The paper also formalizes the grouped-window translation and provides a formal counterexample to its completeness.

The Rocq proof assistant (formerly Coq) is an interactive theorem prover that allows expressing mathematical assertions, mechanically checking proofs, and extracting certified programs. This work applies formal verification to a novel SAT-related framework, ensuring correctness of algorithms and bounds.

The paper introduces FreqDiff, a frequency-aware diffusion framework for temporal knowledge graph (TKG) extrapolation, which uses context-aware spectral calibration to better distinguish query-specific evidence from historical facts. Experiments on four public TKG benchmarks show state-of-the-art performance. This work addresses a key limitation in existing diffusion-based TKG extrapolation methods, which often dilute target-discriminative signals by aggregating conditioning on subject histories. By improving the accuracy of future fact prediction, FreqDiff could advance applications in event forecasting, recommendation systems, and other domains that rely on temporal relational data. FreqDiff formulates future object prediction as query-slot denoising and uses a dual-stream denoiser that integrates temporal dependency modeling with context-aware spectral calibration. The spectral branch synthesizes history-conditioned filters from learnable bases, and a frequency-domain regularizer aligns the denoised target with the gold object in spectral space.

Frequency-domain techniques, such as spectral calibration, can help separate salient signals from noise, which is the core idea behind FreqDiff.

## [Update Masking in Federated Learning: How Much Regularization Survives Averaging?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23286) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides a theoretical analysis of update masking as an implicit regularizer in federated learning, proving that client-specific masks weaken the regularization effect by the cohort size, while shared masks restore it by a factor equal to the inverse gradient diversity. This work addresses the challenge of non-IID data in federated learning by explaining why certain masking strategies fail or succeed, potentially guiding the design of more effective federated optimization algorithms that achieve flat minima without explicit regularization. Experiments on CIFAR-10 show that the recovery factor is 1.19 out of a possible 10, but turning off minibatch sampling raises it to 8.96, while changing data heterogeneity a thousandfold leaves it between 1.17 and 1.50. The configurations that preserve regularization train too poorly to be practical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning trains a shared model across clients without pooling data, and non-IID data distributions can hinder generalization. Sharpness-aware minimization is a common technique to find flat minima, but implicit regularization from noise in parameter updates is another approach that has not been fully explored in federated settings. Update masking adds noise by randomly masking updates, which can act as a regularizer.

A new arXiv paper (2608.23908) presents a 2x2 factorial experiment comparing retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and a deterministic tax computation engine in a multi-agent financial advisory system. The study found that enabling the deterministic tax engine significantly reduced tax savings by about 55 percentage points, while RAG had no significant effect. This research challenges the assumption that adding domain-specific computation engines to LLM agents always improves performance. It highlights potential conflicts between optimization signals, which is crucial for designing effective AI systems in finance and other regulated domains. The study used a 2x2 repeated-measures ANOVA, revealing a significant main effect of the tax engine (F(1,29)=9.17, p=.005, η²p=.240), but no significant RAG effect (p=.841) or interaction (p=.553). The RAG-only condition achieved the highest mean tax savings (47.7%), while the baseline performed second-best (30.6%).

This paper introduces IQACO, a novel hybrid method that embeds an offline implicit Q-learning module into ant colony optimization to adaptively adjust pheromone, heuristic, and evaporation parameters for maritime moving-target observation scheduling with agile satellites. Experiments across 14 scenarios show IQACO improves mean observation benefit by 3.40%–9.40% over conventional ACO and accelerates convergence. This work demonstrates that offline value learning can effectively guide metaheuristic search in complex combinatorial optimization, offering a promising direction for dynamic scheduling problems in satellite operations. It could improve the efficiency and responsiveness of Earth observation systems, benefiting maritime surveillance and disaster monitoring. The IQACO method uses a compact search-state representation capturing pheromone distribution, current and historical-best solution quality, and iteration progress. It maintains stability under different objective-weight settings and consistently outperforms conventional ACO across all tested scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Agile Earth observation satellites can adjust their attitude to observe targets, making scheduling a dynamic, sequence-dependent combinatorial optimization problem. Ant colony optimization (ACO) is a metaheuristic inspired by ant foraging behavior, widely used for scheduling and routing. Implicit Q-learning (IQL) is an offline reinforcement learning method that learns policies from fixed datasets without querying unseen actions, using expectile regression to estimate values. IQACO combines these techniques to adaptively control ACO's exploration-exploitation balance.

This preprint (arXiv:2608.23766) reports two in-silico studies of 32,000 Big Five items, showing that representation, structural screening, and candidate-form construction cause consequential local differences in which items reach expert review, despite broad semantic agreement. The findings challenge the assumption that computational evaluators are neutral infrastructure in AI-assisted item generation, highlighting that choices in representation and screening can alter the content psychometricians see. This has implications for the validity and reproducibility of AI-generated assessments. Across embedding configurations, inclusive primary forms shared a median of only 6 of 40 items, showing that changing representation can drastically alter the final item set. Both eligibility policies filled every content cell in every evaluable form, yet presented different wording, indicating that global summaries can conceal instability.

The paper introduces the Coronavirus Optimization Algorithm (COA), a novel evolutionary optimizer inspired by SARS-CoV-2 mechanisms, which integrates success-history adaptation, archive-assisted search, and stagnation recovery. COA achieves the best overall Friedman rank on CEC 2017 benchmarks across 10, 30, and 50 dimensions, outperforming 15 competitive optimizers. COA offers a compact and transparent adaptive evolutionary optimizer that demonstrates competitive performance, potentially providing a new tool for solving complex continuous optimization problems. Its success-history adaptation and archive-assisted search could inspire further advancements in evolutionary computation. COA combines opposition-based initialization, current-to-pbest mutation, binomial crossover, an external archive, success-history adaptation, population reduction, and partial restart. It shows particularly strong performance on composition functions but has limitations on some hybrid functions, and further high-dimensional validation is needed.

Success-history parameter adaptation is a technique that adjusts control parameters based on historical success, improving algorithm performance.

This paper proposes a multimodal framework that integrates spatiotemporal features from pretrained video, audio, and image encoders with structured behavioral cues like head pose, gaze, facial action units, emotion, and wavelet-based audio features to predict student engagement in online learning videos. The framework uses a Perceiver IO latent bottleneck and uncertainty-aware prediction heads, achieving competitive performance on the CASED challenge test set. Accurate engagement prediction is crucial for improving online education by enabling real-time feedback and personalized interventions. This work addresses the challenges of inter-person variability and annotation subjectivity, and highlights the importance of well-calibrated uncertainty metrics for deploying such models in real-world educational tools. The framework models student and instructor personalities as variational posteriors over learnable embeddings to enable partial pooling across participants. It employs evidential regression and spectral-normalized Gaussian process classification heads for uncertainty-aware prediction, and benchmarks on the CASED challenge test set show that all methods converge near random-chance performance, indicating the dataset's difficulty.

This paper proposes a heuristic algorithm for segmenting strings into adjacent groups that match a fuzzy pattern, using a prefix structure to track the mapping of text segments to fuzzy properties. It proves that for the special case of unit-length segments (fuzzy string matching), the algorithm finds all matching segments. This work bridges fuzzy clustering and string matching, offering a new approach to a niche but relevant problem in data clustering and pattern recognition. The proof for the special case adds theoretical value, potentially inspiring further research in approximate string matching and text segmentation. The algorithm leverages a prefix structure similar to the Knuth-Morris-Pratt (KMP) algorithm's prefix function, enabling efficient tracking of segment-to-property assignments. For the general case, it is heuristic and may not find all solutions, but it aims to find a sufficiently large number of solutions; the best segmentation of the entire text is solved via dynamic programming.

**Background**: Fuzzy string matching, also known as approximate string matching, identifies strings that are similar but not exactly equal, which is useful in search and data cleaning. The prefix function, used in the KMP algorithm, computes the longest proper prefix that is also a suffix for each prefix of a string, enabling efficient pattern matching. This paper extends these concepts to segment strings into groups that match fuzzy properties, a form of fuzzy clustering.

This work advances hate speech detection from coarse-grained classification to structured parsing, enabling more precise identification of targets, arguments, and labels in bilingual contexts. It addresses challenges like multiple targets and culturally coded language, which are critical for real-world moderation systems. The framework includes component ablations and bounded-arbitration diagnostics to isolate the contribution of separated perspective generation and arbitration. Structured teacher traces also support training a smaller student model, suggesting potential for efficient deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Hate speech research has evolved from simple classification to structured parsing, where systems jointly identify targets, supporting arguments, and target-level labels. Bilingual hate speech parsing is challenging due to cultural nuances and multiple targets. STATE-ToxiCN is a span-level Chinese hate speech dataset, and TBO split likely refers to a test split designed to evaluate generalization.

<a id="item-374"></a>
## [STA-Net: Decoupled Shape-Texture Attention for Lightweight Plant Disease Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03754) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces STA-Net, a lightweight network for plant disease classification that uses a novel Shape-Texture Attention Module (STAM) combining DCNv4 deformable convolutions and Gabor filter banks. It achieves 89.00% accuracy and 88.96% F1 score on the CCMT dataset with only 401K parameters and 51.1M FLOPs. This work addresses the challenge of deploying high-precision plant disease classification models on edge devices, which is crucial for precision agriculture. By decoupling shape and texture attention, it demonstrates a promising approach to improve lightweight models' performance on subtle pathological features, potentially enabling more efficient and accurate on-device agricultural AI. The backbone is designed using the training-free neural architecture search method DeepMAD, and STAM splits attention into two branches: one using DCNv4 for shape awareness and the other using a Gabor filter bank for texture awareness. Ablation studies confirm that STAM significantly improves performance over baseline and standard attention models, and the source code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 27, 04:00

**Background**: Plant disease classification is vital for food security, but high-precision deep learning models are often too heavy for edge devices. Lightweight networks typically use attention mechanisms designed for generic object recognition, which may not capture subtle pathological features like irregular lesion shapes and complex textures. DCNv4 is an efficient deformable convolution operator that enhances dynamic properties and memory access, while Gabor filters are linear filters used for texture analysis, mimicking the human visual system's frequency and orientation representations.
